# Build notes: core slice

Concept-stage data centre energy optioneering tool, core slice, built against `brief-v2/BRIEF.md` (authoritative), `ASSUMPTIONS.md`, `OPEN-QUESTIONS.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`. Built 2026-10-15.

## Decisions for the owner

Two points are open. The build uses the current default for both, and neither has been decided for you.

**(a) A no-backup option whose on-site plant meets every resilience item.** This is an option with C5 = None (no designated backup) whose retained bridging or firm prime plant still covers backed load, topology, autonomy and islanding. On the test-3 sample there are three: islanded gas recip, fuel cell and rented plant, at 137 to 140 MW against 127.7 MW critical load.
- *Current default: "Does not meet".* The failing item is named "Backup technology: none selected (C5 = None)". This follows §8, which says "None (flagged)", and treats plant that is not designated as backup as not counting.
- *Alternative: "Meets with flags".* The option passes on the §9.7 items, because §9.7 counts retained and prime plant as backed load, and it carries a "no designated backup" flag.
- Where it applies: `resilience` in `src/engine/evaluate.js` (the `v.C5 === 'none'` item). It has no effect on T1 or T3 at defaults, because C5 = None is not generated by default.

**(b) Whether options may contract less grid capacity than the offer (OPEN-QUESTIONS 18).**
- *Current default:* every option uses the full offered connection (the same per-month profile, never resized) and pays the same grid connection cost. Any shortfall is dispatched as unmet load and flagged. Test 2 checks this.
- *Alternative:* options may contract a smaller capacity, with lower connection capex and possibly lower standing charges. On-site-heavy options would then compare more favourably with grid-heavy ones. This needs a sizing rule for the contracted capacity, or a per-option choice, plus a connection cost that scales with capacity. The brief currently allows a smaller capacity only as a user size override on a custom option (§9.2), which is not built in this slice.

## How to run

```
cd /home/claude/build/app
npm install          # react 18.3.1, react-dom 18.3.1, esbuild 0.23.1 (all local to this folder)
npm test             # node tests/run-tests.mjs
npm run build        # node build.mjs -> dist/
npm run test:browser # node tests/browser-tests.mjs: Playwright + Chromium + axe-core on dist/energy-tool-single-file.html
                     # (run the build first; prints SKIP and exits 0 when Playwright or Chromium is not available)
```

Open `dist/index.html` through any static server (for example `npx serve dist`). The compute pool uses Web Workers loaded from `worker.js`. If workers cannot be used, the app falls back to the main thread and says so in the status line. This covers a synchronous failure (the `Worker` constructor throws, as some browsers do on `file://`) and an asynchronous one (`worker.js` fails to load or throws, or a message cannot be read: `worker.onerror` / `worker.onmessageerror`). In the asynchronous case every worker is terminated first and the status line reads "Worker failed: workers terminated, running on the main thread instead." Two more paths fall back the same way: a `postMessage` to a worker that throws ("Worker could not be started") and a worker that goes silent with no error event, for example killed by the browser ("Worker stopped responding"). A watchdog catches the silent case: no message from a worker that has not yet returned its result for 30 s. While working, a worker sends events at least every 0.55 s on T1. An engine error reported by a worker ends the run with the error shown; it is not retried on the main thread, which would fail the same way.

`dist/energy-tool-hosted.html` (port slice) is the single file as page content for a host that adds the document: only `<title>`, `<style>` and the body content, with the run log copied to the clipboard instead of downloaded (see "Slice: Port from the concept line").

`dist/energy-tool-single-file.html` is the same app with `app.css`, `app.js` and the worker inlined, built by `npm run build`. The worker source (`dist/worker.js`) is embedded as a string and the workers are created from a Blob URL (`URL.createObjectURL(new Blob([src], { type: 'text/javascript' }))`), so the file loads no other file and can be opened from a download, including on a phone. The page contains no `<script src>`, no `<link href>` and no relative file reference (checked by test 7). If the browser refuses Blob workers, the same fallback applies. The fallback runs in 30 ms time slices, so the page and the progress panel stay live. Checked in headless Chromium 2026-10-15: the single file from `file://` runs on a Blob worker and requests only itself and the `blob:` URL; the normal build with `worker.js` missing falls back on `file://` (constructor throws) and over HTTP (404, asynchronous `onerror`), and both runs complete.

## Architecture

```
src/
  engine/            pure functions, no UI, no browser APIs
    prng.js          seeded mulberry32, FNV hash, shuffle
    dates.js         month arithmetic (YYYY-MM), model clock
    defaults.js      input model, §18 records for every default, technology library,
                     country table, choice definitions and default states, T1/T3 examples
    profiles.js      seeded synthetic 8760-h profiles per country; hourly cooling/PUE model
    context.js       per-run shared arrays: offered grid profile by month, IT installed by month,
                     hourly facility demand per cooling approach and model year (cached)
    generator.js     choice enumeration, n/a collapse, compatibility rules with counts,
                     archetypes, cap with seeded Latin hypercube sampling, "values not generated"
    sizing.js        §9.2 sizing rules and sizing sentences
    dispatch.js      §9.4 hourly merit-order dispatch, 8760 h per model year, chronological SOC
    evaluate.js      time-to-power, cash-flow ledger, carbon/water, resilience, flags, deliverability
    scoring.js       effective weights, 0–100 normalisation, weighted score, dominance, rank,
                     view layer (applyView) that never touches score or rank
    pareto.js        HOOKS.paretoView: any two axes, 2-D frontier, weighted pick (= rank 1), time-to-power
                     classes, table rows; view settings read from the registry
    view.js          view state helpers: registry defaults (defaultView, viewSetting, tablePageSize) and
                     removable filter chips (activeFilterChips, activeFilterCount)
    geometry.js      (site slice) projection, areas, validity, offsets, clipping and differences,
                     integral image, GeoJSON/KML import and export
    site.js          (site slice) site model: boundary, setbacks, exclusions, zones, points, raster
    blocks.js        (site slice) option → blocks (§10.3), block-set key
    layout.js        (site slice) auto-layout (§10.4), batch with prefix sharing, solar fill
    sitefit.js       (site slice) §10.6 outputs and site-fit flags
    siteapi.js       (site slice) layoutForOption = HOOKS.layout (plan and detail on demand)
    option.js        (port slice) evaluateOption: one option through the run's own per-option chain
    inspect.js       (port slice) Dispatch tab inspector: hourly re-run, phasing timeline, balances, weeks
    sensitivity.js   (port slice) tornado: low/high driver re-runs scored on the base run's ranges
    run.js           pipeline as stage passes (runModelSteps generator; runModel drains it), dispatch
                     cache, worker slicing/merge, re-score, hooks for later slices
    runlog.js        run event log: stage list, logger, merge, validation, JSON/CSV export,
                     input hash, live progress tracker
  worker.js          Web Worker entry: evaluates one slice of the generated list, returns its event log
  useCompute.js      worker pool (hardwareConcurrency − 1, 1–8), cancellation, time-sliced main-thread
                     fallback, live event collection for the progress panel
  App.jsx            header strip, live region, run summary, ranked list, footer guardrail
  components/        Field (inputs + "indicative — unverified" tag), InputsPanel (steps 1–6 +
                     Choices panel with projected count), RunProgress (live stages, last 200 events,
                     log download), RunSummary, RankedList, OptionDetail, ParetoView/ParetoChart/
                     ParetoTable (Trade-offs tab), FilterChips (removable filter chips, both tabs),
                     SiteView + site/SitePlan, SiteFitPanel, SiteEditor, BlocksTable (Site tab),
                     DispatchView + StackChart (Dispatch tab), SensitivityView (Sensitivity tab)
  styles.css         tokens, dark mode, visible focus, reduced motion, phone/tablet/desktop layouts
tests/run-tests.mjs  Node test script (node:assert only)
tests/site-tests.mjs site slice tests 9a–9j, called from run-tests.mjs
tests/port-tests.mjs port slice tests 10–14, called from run-tests.mjs
tests/view-bundle.mjs the view components bundled once for static rendering (shared by the Node tests)
tests/worker-shim.mjs runs src/worker.js in a Node worker_thread (test 6c)
tests/browser-tests.mjs Playwright checks on the built single file (Canvas path, keyboard, paging, skip
                     links, chips, tablet panel, phone overflow, axe in light and dark; Site tab: phone
                     tap drawing, keyboard plan/table/drawing, GeoJSON import, axe in light and dark)
build.mjs            esbuild: dist/app.js, dist/worker.js, dist/app.css, dist/index.html,
                     dist/energy-tool-single-file.html (CSS, JS and worker inlined; worker
                     from a Blob URL) and dist/energy-tool-hosted.html (page content only)
```

Data flow: inputs (UI keeps `null` for "at default" on derived defaults) → `normalizeInputs` → `buildContext` (with the site model) → `generateOptions` → per option `sizeOption` → `buildBlocks` → layout (batched, cached by block-set key; "fill residual" solar sized from it) → site fit → `dispatchConfig` → `dispatchAll` (cached by configuration key, so options that differ only in non-dispatch choices share one dispatch) → `timeToPower`, `economics`, `carbon`, `resilience`, `optionFlags`, `deliverability` → `scoreOptions` over the whole screened set → `applyView` for display only.

Incremental recompute: a change to weights, cost/carbon axis metric or "rank compliant first" re-scores the existing run on the main thread with no dispatch. Any other input change re-runs the pipeline in the worker pool; in-flight workers are terminated first.

## What is implemented

**Input model.** All §5 inputs needed by the engine, with the brief's defaults. Required inputs (location, site area, IT MW, grid now, grid at full connection, full-connection date) have no default; the app pre-fills the T1 reference example so it runs on load and marks those fields "required · example value". The Inputs panel covers steps 1 to 6 plus run and dispatch settings, with the label rule applied (see below).

**Hourly profiles (synthetic).** For each of the 31 built-in countries, `profiles.js` generates seeded, deterministic 8760-h arrays: dry-bulb and wet-bulb temperature, solar yield per MWp, wind capacity factor, wholesale price and grid carbon intensity. They are scaled exactly to the indicative annual targets in `COUNTRIES`, which are themselves indicative and unverified. **These are synthetic profiles, not measured data.** The UI says "Synthetic profile (seeded), calibrated to indicative annual targets — not measured data". No dataset licence has been checked yet. Price and carbon are anti-correlated with wind and solar and follow seasonal and diurnal shapes.

**Dispatch (real, 8760 h).** `dispatch.js` runs the six-step merit order from §9.4 hour by hour over full 8760-hour model years, carrying BESS state of charge from year to year:
1. On-site solar and wind serve load first.
2. PPA energy is shaped hourly (solar, wind, hybrid, baseload or demand-shaped 24/7), delivered over the grid connection, and uses import capacity. PPA volume that is not delivered (above the residual load in the hour, or above the import capacity) is recorded with its wholesale value; since the owner decision of 2026-10-18 its settlement is the "PPA hedge (not counted)", outside the ledger (port slice), and the `grid-ppa` flag explains it.
3. BESS discharge depends on its role. Peak-shave covers the shortfall above grid plus firm capacity, then expensive hours with a 50 % reserve. Shifting and grid services discharge in the n dearest hours of each day. Bridging support covers shortfalls only. BESS charges from on-site surplus first, then from the grid in the n cheapest hours of each day, within the import cap.
4. Firm plant runs when its marginal cost (fuel ÷ efficiency + VOM + carbon) is below the hourly grid price, or to cover the gap above grid capacity. Min stable load is respected per unit, and output forced above need by min stable load is curtailed. Plant is must-run when islanded. The trigeneration absorption-chiller credit is limited to the mechanical cooling load in that hour. Run-hour handling follows the setting ("Flag only" or "Respect limit").
5. Grid import is capped at the offered capacity for that month ("Show unmet load"), or uncapped with the excess recorded and flagged ("Show exceedance").
6. Demand flexibility runs if C16 is on. Anything still unserved is recorded as unmet load.

Each hour is checked for balance inside the engine (`maxBalanceError`). Model years are dispatched explicitly up to the last change in demand, grid or plant availability (capped at 8 years, a run setting). The last year then repeats as steady state over the evaluation period.

**Option generator.** This is the Cartesian product of open choices (locked choices give one value). Irrelevant choices collapse to "n/a" (rule d). Compatibility rules (a), (b), (c) and one builder rule (e) remove impossible combinations, and each is counted. All 11 archetype baselines (A1 to A9, A10a SMR, A10b hydrogen) are always generated, and so are archetype vectors that sit outside the open choices. When the count exceeds the cap (2,000) and "Sample above cap" is on, every archetype is kept, then seeded Latin hypercube sampling runs over the choice dimensions. Draws that land on an invalid or duplicate combination are repaired from the remaining valid set, and the number repaired is reported. Switching sampling off screens every combination. The "values not generated" note lists every omitted value with its reason ("default subset", "locked", "wind default", "excluded by user") and an Include control. The C11 wind info flag shows on the C11 row and in the run summary.

With brief defaults on T1, the open choices give 57,024 valid combinations (57,028 with the 4 archetypes that sit outside the open choices), so sampling is active on a default run. See "Brief issues" (1).

**Sizing.** §9.2 rules with sentences:
- Design load is built from the peak design-day PUE.
- Critical load drives backup unit counts, using the block rule for N, N+1, 2N and catcher.
- Fuel storage is sized from autonomy.
- Bridging is sized to the largest shortfall, using N+1 per block.
- Firm prime is sized to the steady-state average.
- BESS is sized as a percentage of design load, kept as given.
- Roof, carport and residual-ground PV use an area budget.
- Wind count comes from spacing.
- The PPA volume is reported.
- The grid connection is the offered capacity per period, the same shared array for every option, never resized. The grid shortfall at design load is stated per period.

**Economics.** One real-terms cash-flow ledger per option, the source for LCOE, NPV of net cost, capex and opex.
- Capex is timed by phase (C19), with contingency, replacements, and residual value at the end of the period or salvage on removal. The cooling capex delta is relative to the air baseline.
- Opex covers fixed O&M, VOM, fuel, grid energy plus the non-commodity adder (also charged on PPA energy carried over the grid), PPA strike plus shaping on PPA energy delivered over the grid connection (owner decision 2026-10-18, port slice), Scope 1 carbon cost, genset test runs, rental for rented bridging plant, and export revenue.
- Capex scope excludes the data hall shell and IT fit-out, stated in the UI.

**Carbon and water.** Location-based and market-based annual average gCO₂e/kWh with the decarbonisation trajectory, hourly CFE %, the Scope 1/2 split, a trajectory per year, and WUE.

**Resilience check.** Backed load (backup at topology after removing redundant units, plus retained bridging, firm prime and BESS), topology against the requirement, autonomy, islanding if required, and gas dependency. Status is Meets, Meets with flags or Does not meet, with the failing item named.

**Time-to-power.** For each month, supply = offered grid + firm plant available (programme and fuel dates) + bridging BESS. The tool reports first power, full load, power gap in MW-months, and the axis metric (months to full load + 0.5 × power gap ÷ design MW). The phase targets drive the "target missed" flag.

**Flags.** §15 categories: grid, resilience, permitting (IED, MCP, Seveso fuel and hydrogen, wind, stack height, run-hours; non-EU proxy note; "Screening flag, not a permitting determination"), fuel and utilities, targets and ceilings, technology, generation, data, load, and an area-budget info flag. Flags never remove, hide, demote or resize an option.

**Deliverability.** §9.9 formula: Emerging and Nascent shares (mean of capacity and energy shares), permitting major and minor counts, gas "Unknown", hydrogen, and procurement lead time over 24 months. Floored at 0. The site-fit penalty is off and has no effect because site fit is not assessed.

**Scoring.**
- Effective weights are raw ÷ Σ raw, with equal weights and a notice when all are 0.
- Each axis is normalised 0–100 across all screened options (best = 100).
- The weighted score is Σ wᵢ·scoreᵢ.
- Dominance is marked on (cost, carbon, TTP, deliverability) with no better resilience status.
- Ranking is by score. "Rank requirement-compliant options first" is off by default.
- View filters (resilience status, show only with flag, hide with flag, capex ceiling, dominated status, archetypes only, search, hide dominated, sort) are applied after scoring by a pure function and never change any score or rank. Each active filter is a removable chip on both result tabs. The land-take ceiling filter waits for the site slice (Brief issue 13).

**UI.**
- The header strip shows option counts (shown, screened, combinations, dominated), leader and score, model badge, sampling and hide-dominated state, filter count, exceedance-mode warning, effective weights, and the unverified-defaults count (all records; the count affecting the leader is not available yet, Brief issue 16). The leader is `weightedPick()`, the same function as the Trade-offs pick.
- A polite live region announces "Options updated. Leader unchanged" or "New leader: …".
- The run summary sits above the list and expands automatically when an entry changes.
- The ranked table is sortable, paged at 50 rows, with axis mini-bars, badges and flag counts. An option detail row shows scores, economics, energy, time-to-power, resilience, deliverability, sizing sentences, flags and the choice vector.
- The layout is responsive: a sliding inputs panel below 1280 px and single-column fields below 768 px.
- All controls are native and keyboard-operable, with a visible focus ring, skip links ("Skip to results", "Skip to trade-offs chart"), `prefers-reduced-motion` support and dark mode. Text meets 4.5:1 and form-control boundaries 3:1 in both themes. The "indicative — unverified" tags are not tab stops; their basis is the described control's accessible description. Below 1280 px the closed inputs panel is hidden from focus.
- No browser storage APIs are used.

## Defaults used and their labels

Every default is held as a §18 record in `defaults.js` (`getRegistry()`): `{ value, unit, low, high, distribution, basis, source:'', year:'', url:'', confidence:'C', verified:false, sourceToVerify, note, label:'indicative — unverified' }`. No default has been verified, so **every one shows "indicative — unverified"** in the UI while it is at its default, with the basis (source, run setting or judgement) and the source to verify against shown on hover or focus. Test 5d enumerates every record and checks the label, basis, confidence C and verified false.

| Group | Defaults (all indicative — unverified) | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Project | name "Untitled project"; analysis start today + 3 months; seed 20261015; country from location | judgement / run setting |
| Site | aspect 1.5; setback 10 m; height limit 25 m; roads/parking 15 %; off-site land 0 | judgement |
| Load | 1 phase at grid full-connection date; ramp 50 % → 100 % over 12 months; utilisation 80 %; workload Mixed cloud; cooling open; gross-up 2.2; 2 storeys × 6 m + 4 m roof zone; ancillary 2 %; flex 10 % / 100 h (C16 locked Off) | judgement |
| Cooling (§9.3) | per approach: free-cooling threshold, band, COP curve, fan and loss fractions, density (air 1.5, evap 1.8, DLC 4.0, immersion 3.0 kW/m²), WUE, capex delta | source (ASHRAE TC 9.9, vendor data to verify) |
| Grid | steps none; export 0; price and carbon multipliers 1; country price adder; date uncertainty −6/+24 months (not used in this slice) | judgement / source |
| Utilities | gas Unknown, available from start + 18 months, unlimited; hydrogen No; water Unknown; district heating No | judgement |
| Requirements | 100 % backed; N+1; 48 h; islanding No; UPS 5 min; block size 10; catcher n = 4; carbon target, capex ceiling, run-hour limit none; IED 50 MWth; MCP 1–50 MWth; Seveso H₂ 5 t, diesel/HVO 2,500 t; test runs 12 h/yr at 75 % | judgement / source (directives to verify) |
| Economics | 20 years; WACC 7 %; contingency 10 %; AACE Class 5 −30/+50 %; carbon escalation 2 %/yr real; electricity and gas escalation 0; salvage 50 % | judgement / source |
| Weights | 35 / 25 / 25 / 15; presets as §5.7 | judgement |
| Deliverability | −15 Emerging, −40 Nascent, −8 major, −3 minor, −10 gas Unknown, −10 hydrogen, −5 per lead time > 24 months; site-fit penalty off | judgement |
| Run/view settings | cap 2,000; sample above cap On; grid handling "Show unmet load"; run-hours "Flag only"; cost LCOE; carbon location-based; absolute scaling off; compliant-first off; hide dominated off; wind threshold 20 ha; fixed PV 5 MWp; bridging BESS 24 h; timeline 120 months; max explicit dispatch years 8; table page size 50; Pareto view: axes follow the scoring metrics, up to 5 time-to-power classes, Canvas above 500 points, chart shown first | run setting / judgement |
| Choices | every §8 default state (C1 to C20 and the C12 role), including C6/C7 locked to the requirement, C8 open/locked by cooling input, C11 wind default, C15 by district heating | judgement |
| Technology library | capex, O&M, efficiency, min stable load, unit size, life, footprint, programme durations for 18 technologies; fuel prices and emission factors; PPA strikes | source (IRENA, NREL ATB, DEA, Lazard, vendor; to verify) |
| Country table | 31 synthetic calibration rows (temperature, solar yield, wind CF, price, adder, carbon, gas, carbon price, decarbonisation to 2050); FX rates | source (PVGIS, ERA5, ENTSO-E, Ember, TTF/NBP, ETS, ECB; to verify) |

Builder-chosen values not given by the brief, all recorded as judgement or run settings with the label: islanding default "No"; belly-tank autonomy 8 h; BESS initial SOC 50 %; peak-shave 50 % arbitrage reserve; CHP heat-to-power 1.0 and absorption COP 0.7; carport 30 % of the parking allowance; wind 33.75 ha per turbine for spacing; HVO emission factor proxy 0.03 t/MWh_th; salvage 50 %; T1 phase dates at months 18, 30, 42 and 54 (the brief gives 4 × 25 MW but no dates).

## What is not in this slice

- **Parallel coordinates, choice-impact chart, compare view, radar** (§12.2, §12.7). The Pareto scatter is now built (see "Slice: Pareto trade-off view" below); `HOOKS.paretoView` is implemented.
- **Breakevens, Monte Carlo, rank stability, robust pick, P10–P90 ranges** (§13), and the tornado drivers beyond the seven of the port slice. Hooks: `HOOKS.sensitivity` and `HOOKS.monteCarlo`. Every default record already carries low and high values and a distribution. Capex shows only the AACE band. (The port slice added the tornado for one option and the Dispatch tab with the phasing timeline and hourly dispatch.)
- **Scenarios** (§13.3). Hook: `HOOKS.scenario`. The escalation inputs exist.
- **Site layout, plan, massing, site-fit status, unplaced tray, noise proxy, height check per block** (§10). Hook: `HOOKS.layout`. Only an area budget is computed (residual ground PV, plus an info flag when land take exceeds net area). C17/C18 notes state that fit counts are unavailable.
- **Exports** (PDF, Excel, JSON, GeoJSON, PNG, presentation mode), project save/load, undo/redo, snapshots, decision log, plain-English summary (§12.10, §14, §16, §19). Hook: `HOOKS.export`.
- **Representative-day screening tier and the 8760 verification badge/deviation flag** (§9.1). This slice runs the full 8760-h model for every screened option instead. Every result is therefore labelled "Full-year hourly (8760 h)". Hook: `HOOKS.screeningModel`.
- Heat reuse costing and revenue (C15), BESS grid-services revenue, the Scope 2 shadow carbon price, embodied carbon, numeric sweeps, custom option builder and pinning, absolute scaling (setting held, not applied), and per-edge setbacks and exclusion zones.
- **Option card, schematic, key quantities, phasing timeline** (§12.3–§12.6). `OptionDetail` is a basic detail panel (scores, economics, energy, time-to-power, resilience, deliverability, sizing, flags, choice vector, dominance), not the tabbed §12.3 card, and it opens below the chart or the table row, not in the §16 right-hand drawer at 1280 px and wider. Planned with the trade-off views stage (§20 stage 3).
- **Land-take ceiling view filter** (§11, §2 principle 5). Land take per option comes from the site model (§10), so the filter is deferred to the site slice (Brief issue 13). The capex ceiling filter is built.
- **Count of unverified defaults affecting the leader** (§12 header). Needs a per-option map from §18 records to options; deferred with §13/§14, which need the same map (Brief issue 16).

## Test results

See the bottom of this file ("Test run") for the real numbers from `node tests/run-tests.mjs`.

## Brief issues

1. **Default option count conflicts with §7.2 and §17.** The brief says default open choices give "about 200–600 options" and the cap "is only reached when the user opens extra choices". The §8 default states alone multiply far beyond that. C1 bridge × C2 (3) × C3 (4), C4 (3), C5 (3), C8 (4), C9 (3), C10 (3), C12 (3), C13/C14 (1 + 3) give 57,024 valid combinations on T1 even after n/a collapse. **Choice made:** follow the §8 default states literally and let the visible "Sample above cap" setting (default On) screen 2,000 with every archetype kept. The run summary and header strip state the real counts. The brief should either shrink the default open sets (for example lock C3, C9 and C14 by default) or revise the 200–600 figure and the §17 targets.
2. **§17 timing targets assume the representative-day tier.** This slice runs the full 8760-h dispatch for every option, as the task requires. On this sandbox, T1 (2,000 options, 1,700+ distinct dispatch configurations, about 10,000 dispatched 8760-h years) takes several seconds on one thread. In the browser it is divided across the worker pool. The ≤ 500 ms / 1.0 s targets are not met in this slice. They depend on the representative-day screening tier (§9.1), which is not built yet.
3. **C5 "Retained bridging plant" without retained plant.** The brief lists no compatibility rule for C5 = retained when C1 = wait (no bridging plant exists) or C1 = bridge with C3 = remove. Such a combination cannot be described: the backup technology is plant that does not exist. **Choice made:** added rule (e) "Retained-bridging backup requires retained bridging plant", shown by name with its count (5,184 on T1). Possible but weak combinations stay generated and flagged.
4. **Retained bridging as backup: sizing basis.** §9.2 sizes bridging to the grid shortfall, which can be far below the critical load (6.6 MW against 103 MW on T1). That makes every "retained as backup" option fail resilience by construction. **Choice made:** when C5 = retained, the retained plant is sized by the same backup rule (critical load, required topology, autonomy for liquid fuel), taking the larger of the two sizes. This is a sizing rule applied to every such option before any check (§2 principle 5), not a resize after a breach.
5. **Islanded grid as backup in the resilience check.** C1 = Islanded keeps the offered grid connection "as backup". The §9.7 resilience check counts only on-site firm capacity during a grid outage. **Choice made:** the islanded grid connection is not counted toward backed load.
6. **"BESS role" is not a separate choice in §8.** C12 combines size and role. The default role is fixed as "peak-shave against grid cap". **Choice made:** modelled as a separate choice row C12r (locked to peak-shave by default), so rule (c) "BESS as bridging requires a non-zero BESS size" has something to act on.
7. **C12 alternatives.** The brief gives durations 1/2/4 h at 25 %/50 % but only three defaults. **Choice made:** six size alternatives plus None; the defaults are None, 2 h at 25 % and 4 h at 50 %.
8. **Phase dates for T1** are not given (§21). **Choice made:** months 18, 30, 42 and 54 from the analysis start.
9. **Time-to-power metric when full load is never reached.** The brief's formula needs "months to full load". **Choice made:** use the timeline horizon (120 months, a run setting) when full load is not reached in it, plus the power-gap term.
10. **"Supply ≥ demand at the required resilience" for first power** (§9.8) is not defined further. **Choice made:** first and full power test the energy supply only (grid + firm + bridging BESS). Resilience is reported separately by the resilience check.
11. **Deliverability maturity share**: "energy-weighted and capacity-weighted" with one penalty. **Choice made:** the mean of the capacity share and the steady-state energy share.
12. **AC5 "weighted leader" is not defined** (Pareto review, 2026-10-16). AC5 says "the highlighted Pareto pick and the plain-English summary always match the weighted leader". The brief never defines "weighted leader". Ranking is "by weighted score" (§11), but "Rank requirement-compliant options first" (§11 (b)) can put a lower-scoring option at rank 1, and the header calls rank 1 the "leader" (§12). **Interpretation (owner's decision, applied):** the weighted leader is rank 1 under the current weights and ranking settings. The highlighted pick, the header "Leader", the live "New leader" announcement and, when it is built, the plain-English summary all use one function, `weightedPick()` in `src/engine/pareto.js`, so they always name the same option. With the toggle off (default) rank 1 is also the highest weighted score. With it on, the highest-weighted-score option can be a "Does not meet" option ranked lower. It is then marked separately: a dashed ring with "Highest score #k" on the chart, a "Highest weighted score" badge in the table, and a pick-line sentence giving its rank and the reason (the toggle is on and its resilience status is "Does not meet"). Test 8g checks this on synthetic and real options. (The slice first kept the pick on the highest weighted score; its note cited "§11 wording", but the wording is AC5's, not §11's.)
13. **Land-take ceiling view filter** (§11, §2 principle 5) needs land take per option, which comes from the site model (§10). **Choice made:** deferred to the site slice and listed under "What is not in this slice". The other §11 filters have controls: resilience status, show only or hide options with a flag, capex ceiling, dominated status, archetypes, search and "Hide dominated options". Site-fit status is deferred with the site model.
14. **Options with no finite value on a plotted axis** (for example infinite LCOE when an option delivers no energy, T3 grid-only options) cannot be placed on a scatter. **Choice made:** they are listed in the table alternative as "not plotted (no finite value)", counted in the note above the chart, and kept in the frontier calculation's input (where they cannot be on the frontier). This is not one of the §2 principle 5 reductions: nothing is removed from any list, the ranking or the scoring set, and it does not depend on a constraint breach. The brief does not mention the case.
15. **"Any two axes" (§12.2).** **Choice made:** the axes are the four §11 axis metrics with their metric variants: LCOE, NPV of net cost, carbon (location-based, market-based, 1 − hourly CFE), time-to-power and deliverability, seven in all. Deliverability is higher-is-better; the 2-D frontier uses each axis's own direction, and the axis titles state it. Choosing the axis already on the other side swaps the two.
16. **Header "number of unverified defaults affecting the leader"** (§12) needs a map from §18 records to the options that use them (technology records by choice vector, country rows, settings). **Choice made:** the header shows the total number of records (all unverified) and says the leader-specific count is not available in this slice. §13 (inputs with most influence) and §14 item 4 need the same map, so it is deferred to the uncertainty slice.
17. **"Dominated by [option name]" with generated names** (§7.2 step 6). Option names run to 60–90 characters. **Choice made:** the text is "Dominated by #k name": the rank comes first so it survives truncation. It is one line in the ranked table, truncated visually with the full text in the title and the DOM. It is in full in the trade-off table, the tooltip, the live region and the option detail, which keyboard users can open.
18. **Height limit in placement** (site slice, kept in the review; H2 D1). §10.4 step 3 says each placement "tests … height zones", and step 4 says a block that cannot be placed makes the option "Does not fit". Read as a hard test, no 16 m data hall could be placed under T2's 15 m limit, and no option on T2 could "fit or fit with flags" as AC7 requires, whatever C17 and C18 are. §15 lists "height limit exceeded" as a site-fit flag, which can only arise if over-height blocks are placed, and §2 principle 5 lists height among the constraints that are flagged, never enforced. **Choice made:** height is soft in placement (judgement call 11): a compliant cell is preferred; otherwise the block is placed and flagged, and the option is "Fits with flags", never "Does not fit" for height alone. The brief should say in §10.4 step 3 that the height test is a preference with a flag.
19. **Access points and the roads allowance** (H2 I09b). §5.2 says the access points drive the roads allowance, but defines the allowance as 15 % of the net developable area with no term for access. **Choice made:** not built (no rule to implement); the allowance is the fraction alone, and the access point drives fuel-delivery placement only.
20. **Gas connection cost** (H2 I08b). §5.2 says the gas main location drives the connection cost; §9.5's capex scope does not list a gas connection and no cost basis is given. **Choice made (review):** options that burn gas on site pay a connection pipe from the gas main to the buildable envelope at `tech.gasConnection.pipePerKm` (€0.8 m/km, indicative — unverified); with no gas main location (the §5.2 default, "gas off-site unknown") nothing is costed and the sizing sentence says so.
21. **LCOE when an option delivers almost nothing** (review of the site slice, H1; pre-existing). §9.5 defines LCOE per MWh delivered and §11 normalises each axis linearly between the best and worst option. On T3 (no grid) grid-dependent options deliver almost no energy, and a financially settled PPA gives them a net revenue, so 80 options have a negative LCOE down to −13.8 M€/MWh and 26 a non-finite one; the cost axis then cannot separate the other 1,844 options (all within 0.004 points). **Not changed** (outside the site slice; owner decision): see open point 10 of the site slice for the options. **Owner decision 2026-10-18, applied in the port slice:** no PPA income without physical delivery. T3 now has 0 negative LCOEs (26 non-finite remain), but the cost axis still does not separate the ordinary options (1,866 within 0.013 points), now because near-zero-delivery options reach 4.6 M€/MWh at the top of the axis; see "Slice: Port from the concept line", open point 2.

## Run event log (trackability)

Added 2026-10-15. Every run, in the worker pool or on the main thread, emits an ordered event log. The engine is in `src/engine/runlog.js` and `src/engine/run.js`, the UI in `src/components/RunProgress.jsx`.

### Event schema

Each event is a plain object, and the field order is fixed:

```
{ seq, stage, event, t_ms, count, total, detail }
```

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `seq` | Contiguous integer from 0 within a log. In a merged pool log it is renumbered 0..N−1, and the source's own number is kept as `detail.wseq`. |
| `stage` | One of the 12 stage ids below, in pipeline order. |
| `event` | `start`, `progress`, `end` or `warning`. |
| `t_ms` | Milliseconds since the run started, at 1 µs resolution. Workers use the same origin: the main thread passes its absolute start time (`performance.timeOrigin + performance.now()`). **This is the only field that differs between two runs with the same inputs.** |
| `count` | Items processed so far. `start` gives 0, `end` gives the items processed in the stage, `warning` gives null. |
| `total` | Items expected in this stage for this source, or null when not known in advance (option generation start). |
| `detail` | Plain object. It always has `source` (`main` or `worker k`). On `end` it always has `items`, `cacheHits`, `cacheMisses` (null when the stage has no cache) and `warnings` (the number of warning events the source raised in that stage). It never holds timings or anything else machine-dependent. |

Log content is deterministic. Progress for per-option stages is emitted every `PROGRESS_EVERY` = 250 items, counted, not timed. Dispatch reports every option. The input hash is computed on the normalised inputs with sorted keys, so it does not depend on key order. Test 6b checks that two T1 runs give identical logs once `t_ms` is removed.

### What each stage reports

Stages run as passes over the option list, in this order. Each source has exactly one `start` and one `end` per stage it runs. Per-option results are unchanged: T1 and T3 outputs for all 2 × 2,000 options (ids, scores, ranks, LCOE, NPV, capex, carbon, unmet, time-to-power, deliverability, resilience status, flag ids, dominance, dispatch keys) were compared before and after the restructure, and they are byte-identical.

| # | Stage id | `progress` events | `warning` codes | `end` detail (besides items, cache, warnings) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `input-normalisation` | none | `phases-derived` (no phasing table), `required-missing` | `inputHash`, `engineVersion`, `seed`, `choices`, `choicesAtDefault` |
| 2 | `context-build` | none | `profiles-synthetic`, `defaults-unverified` (both on every run, because both are true of every run in this slice) | `country`, `months`, `timelineMonths`, `coolingProfiles`, `phases`, `firstPhaseMonth`, `lastPhaseMonth`, `fullGridMonth` |
| 3 | `option-generation` | `step: enumerate` (raw product, after n/a collapse, valid); `step: compatibility-rule` once per rule a, b, c, e, d with `removed`; `step: archetypes`; `step: cap` with `cap`, `sampling`, `sampled`, `decision` (below cap / sampled / cap exceeded with sampling off), `screened`, `totalCombinations`, `lhsRepaired`; in a worker, `step: slice` | `cap-sampling`, `enumeration-truncated` | `screened`, `totalCombinations`, `sampled`, `slice`, `optionsThisSource` |
| 4 | `sizing` | every 250 options | none | cache = hourly demand cache (cooling approach × model year) |
| 5 | `dispatch` | **one per option**: `count` = options dispatched so far out of `total`, `option` id, `cfg` (short id of the dispatch configuration key, `K` + 12 hex), `cache` `hit` or `miss`; a miss also gives `years`, `explicitYears` and the full `key`; a hit gives `firstSimulatedFor` (the option that was simulated) | `unmet-load` (configurations with unmet load), `grid-exceedance`, `balance-error` (> 1e-6 MWh) | `cacheHits`, `cacheMisses` (dispatch cache), `distinctConfigurations`, `yearsSimulated`, `demandCacheHits`, `demandCacheMisses` |
| 6 | `time-to-power` | every 250 | `full-load-not-reached` | `notReachingFullLoad` |
| 7 | `economics` | every 250 | `lcoe-non-finite` | `ledgers` |
| 8 | `carbon` | every 250 | none | none |
| 9 | `resilience` | every 250 | `requirement-not-met` (count of "Does not meet"; the options are still ranked and shown) | `statuses` (Meets / Meets with flags / Does not meet counts) |
| 10 | `flags` | every 250 | none | `optionsWithMajorFlags` |
| 11 | `deliverability` | every 250 | none | none |
| 12 | `scoring-ranking` | in the pool, `step: merge` with `workers`, `options`, `distinctConfigurations`, `duplicatedAcrossWorkers` | `equal-weights`, `metric-non-finite` | `leader`, `leaderScore`, `dominated`, `equalWeightsUsed`, `rankCompliantFirst`, `weights` |

Warnings record run-level conditions. Per-option conditions stay as flags on the options, and the log counts them; it does not list each one.

### Worker pool and fallback

- **Pool.** The main thread runs stage 1 as a coordinator (source `main`), which gives the input hash. Each worker runs stages 1 to 11 on its slice with its own logger (source `worker k`, own `seq` from 0) and returns the log with its result. For the live panel it also streams copies of its events in batches (every 200 events or 120 ms); test 6c checks the streamed copies equal the returned log. `mergeRuns` runs stage 12 on main and merges with `mergeLogs`: order by stage, then source (main, worker 0, worker 1, …), then the source's own `seq`, renumbered 0..N−1. Within a source the order is that source's sequence order. The merged log never goes backwards in stage order.
- **Main-thread fallback.** `runModelSteps` is a generator that yields between work items. The fallback drains it in 30 ms slices with `setTimeout(0)` between them, so the progress panel updates during the run. The log content is identical to the synchronous run (test 6d).
- **Cancelled or failed runs.** Events received so far are kept per source. "Download run log" then exports a partial log, merged the same way, with `_partial` in the file name and `status: partial` in the JSON header.

Worker slices are taken by index (every k-th option). Options that share a dispatch configuration can land in different workers, and each worker's cache is local. On T1 with 3 workers, 185 of 1,732 configurations are simulated in more than one worker. The merge step reports this (`duplicatedAcrossWorkers`). Results are unaffected; it only costs time. Slicing by configuration key would remove it, but it is not done here.

### UI

- **Run progress panel** (above the run summary, open by default): headline (current stage and percent, event count, warnings), one row per stage with percent complete, a bar, cache hit/miss and warning count, and a scrolling log of the last 200 events. The log follows the newest event until the user scrolls up; a "Follow latest events" control resumes following. On a phone (below 768 px) stages are one column, and each log line wraps under its timestamp, source and stage. There is no horizontal scroll at 390 px (checked headless).
- **Percent per stage** is the mean over the sources expected to run it: workers for stages 1 to 11, main for stage 12. In the pool, stage 1 also runs on main, and the panel shows the workers' share.
- **Accessibility.** One polite, atomic live region announces the current stage only, when it changes, for example "Run in progress: Dispatch (8760 h), stage 5 of 12.", then "Run complete. All stages finished." The event log is `role="log"` with `aria-live="off"`, so individual events are not announced. The status line spinner text is `aria-hidden` while running, so the stage is not announced twice.
- **Download run log**: JSON and CSV buttons. File names are `run-log_<inputHash>_<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSSZ>.json|csv`, plus `_partial` for an unfinished run. The JSON has a header (`schema: dc-energy-run-log/1`, input hash, engine version, mode, run start and export time, validation result, stage list, field list, event count) and then one event per line. The CSV has one row per event, columns in schema order, with `detail` as a quoted JSON string.

Size: a T1 log is 2,086 events (single thread) or 2,089 to 2,155 (pool, depending on worker count), about 1.3 MB as JSON. The UI holds the full log only in the finished run object; the panel keeps 200 events.

## Test 3: root cause and change

**Symptom.** Test 3 ("No option is hidden by default") asserted at least one option with resilience status "Does not meet" on T1 and on T3, and none existed.

**Root cause: the test sample contained no option that fails resilience.** The resilience check was not the cause. T1 and T3 at brief defaults give 185 Meets, 1,815 Meets with flags and 0 Does not meet on each. This follows from the brief:
- The default C5 subset is HVO, diesel and retained bridging plant (§8); gas, BESS-UPS and None are not generated by default.
- C6 and C7 are locked to the requirement (§8).
- Every option is sized to the requirement by the §9.2 backup rule (§2 principle 9). Retained plant is sized by the same rule (Brief issue 4).

So every option meets the requirement by construction, and an assertion that needs a failing option cannot pass on that sample. When the check was probed directly on T1, it failed every case that must fail: C5 = None (backed load and autonomy), topology N under N+1, autonomy 12 h and belly tanks under 48 h, islanding required on grid-only, and retained gas plant with gas = No.

**Change to the test.** The assertion is unchanged and still required. It now runs on a sample that exercises failing resilience: T1 with C5 = None included and the other open choices locked, so all 36 combinations are screened without sampling. The sample contains the must-fail case: grid-only with no backup on a required 100 % backed load, which has 0 MW backed load. The test asserts that case is "Does not meet" with "Backed load" named, that every C5 = None option is "Does not meet" with a named failing item, and that the failing options appear in the default view and are ranked. The T1 and T3 checks (every option shown, no chips, archetypes present, dominated options ranked, T3 grid-dependent options flagged) are unchanged. The hidden-by-default behaviour (`applyView`, `DEFAULT_VIEW`, `hideDominated` off) is untouched.

## Resilience check fix (separate from the test-3 root cause)

This is a separate defect, not the cause of the test-3 failure. It was found while writing the new test-3 sample, because that sample was the first to contain C5 = None options. Without the fix, the new sample's "named failing item" assertion would have failed on three options; the original test-3 failure (no failing option in the sample) would have happened with or without it. In `src/engine/evaluate.js`, `resilience`:
- **Defect.** C5 = None forced "Does not meet" through `fail = … || v.C5 === 'none'`, with no item recorded. An islanded C5 = None option whose retained bridging plant covers the critical load passed every named item, so it showed "Does not meet" with an empty failing list. Three of the 36 test-3 options did this (islanded gas recip, fuel cell and rented plant, 137 to 140 MW against 127.7 MW critical). §9.7 requires the failing item to be named.
- **Fix.** C5 = None now adds a named failing item, "Backup technology: none selected (C5 = None); on-site plant counted above is not a designated backup". Status is derived from the items only. The pass/fail outcome is unchanged (C5 = None still always fails, as before). Only the missing name is fixed.
- **Effect.** No change on T1 or T3, which have no C5 = None options; the outputs are byte-identical before and after. In the test-3 sample, the three islanded options now name "Backup technology", and the grid-only and bridge options name "Backed load", "Autonomy" and "Backup technology".
- **Open point (not changed).** Whether C5 = None with on-site plant that meets every item should be "Meets with flags" rather than "Does not meet" is a brief question. §8 says "None (flagged)" and §9.7 counts retained and prime plant as backed load. The previous rule is kept.

## Review

Opus review of three Haiku verification checks, 2026-10-15. Every call was checked against the files and against a fresh build, not taken from the verifier's summary.

**Haiku calls: 11 accepted, 0 rejected.** Check A's eight verified claims are counted as one call. Every call matched the files at the time it was made. Two are now superseded by later changes and one needed a wording correction (noted below).

| Check | Call | Verdict | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Tests 13/13 on the trackable build | Accepted, now superseded | The suite then had 13 tests; test 7 (single file) was added later, so it is now 14/14. |
| A | Resilience fix, 12-stage log, log determinism, worker merge order, build script, defaults labelled, no browser storage, no `Math.random` | Accepted | Checked in `evaluate.js` (named "Backup technology" item), `runlog.js` (`mergeLogs`: stage, then source, then seq), `build.mjs` and tests 5d, 6a and 6b. None of `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, `indexedDB` or `Math.random` is in `src/`. The only `Math.random` in `dist/app.js` is React's internal key suffix. |
| A | Caveat 1: BUILD-NOTES gave the wrong root cause for test 3 | Accepted, already fixed | "Test 3: root cause and change" now names the cause correctly: the sample contained no failing option. The resilience defect has its own section, which states it is not the cause. |
| A | Caveat 2: the generator.js line 9 comment misdescribed the sampling cap | Accepted, already fixed; clarified in this review | The misleading wording ("Unscreened combinations are not deleted") had already been replaced with an accurate one, but it did not say that the cap includes the archetypes. The comment now states that every archetype plus (cap − archetypes) sampled combinations gives 2,000 in total, matching `lhsSample(…, cap − list.length, …)`. |
| B | Single file not self-contained: the inline script loaded `worker.js` at runtime | Accepted (true of that build), now resolved | The earlier single-file snapshot contains `new Worker("worker.js")`. The current build defines `__INLINE_WORKER_SRC__` and creates workers from a Blob URL, and `build.mjs` fails the build if `worker.js` is quoted in the single file. |
| C | Build exit 0 | Accepted | Re-run: exit 0. |
| C | 14/14 tests pass | Accepted | Re-run: 14/14 (block above). |
| C | Single file has no script/link/`worker.js` references | Accepted, with a wording correction | Counted directly in `dist/energy-tool-single-file.html`: 0 `<script src`, 0 `<link`, 0 `worker.js`, 0 `importScripts`, 0 relative `src`/`href`, 1 `new Worker(` (the Blob URL), 1 inline `<script>` block. "No script references" holds only for external scripts: the raw text `<script` occurs twice, once as the real tag and once inside React's bundled string `"<script><\/script>"`. |
| C | Worker in the single file is byte-identical to `dist/worker.js` | Accepted | The string literal passed to `new Blob([…])` was decoded independently (93,923 chars) and is identical to `dist/worker.js` (93,923 bytes). |
| C | BUILD-NOTES test block verified | Accepted, now superseded | The block in BUILD-NOTES before this review is text-identical to Check C's own run output. This review changed the source, so the block has been replaced with this review's run. |
| C | No TODO/FIXME/XXX | Accepted | 0 matches in `src/`, `tests/`, `build.mjs`, `index.html`. |

**Fixes made in this review** (originals backed up in the reviewer's scratchpad):
1. `src/useCompute.js`, worker fallback. Two paths could leave a run stuck with no progress and no result. Both were reproduced with a mocked React and Worker harness against the old and new hook:
   - A worker that goes silent with no `error` event (killed by the browser, or a hung load). Before: status stays "running" indefinitely. After: a watchdog (30 s with no message from a worker that has not returned its result) terminates the pool and falls back to the main thread, with the status "Worker stopped responding". Measured on T1, the longest gap between worker events is 0.55 s.
   - A worker `postMessage` that throws synchronously (for example `DataCloneError`). Before: the exception escaped the timer callback with workers already started and status "running". After: it is caught, the pool is terminated and the run falls back, with the status "Worker could not be started".
   - Also: the `error`-message path now clears `workersRef`, and the watchdog is cleared on completion, on failure and on effect cleanup (a superseded run).
   - Harness results after the fix (T1, 3 mocked workers): silent → done on the main thread, 2,000 options; throw on post → done; `onerror` → done; `onmessageerror` → done; events then silence → done.
2. `src/engine/generator.js`, header comment on the cap (caveat 2).
3. `BUILD-NOTES.md`: "Decisions for the owner" added at the top; the fallback paragraph covers the new paths; a count error that no check had caught, corrected ("What is implemented" said 57,027 valid combinations; the code gives 57,024 valid plus 4 archetypes outside the open choices, 57,028 in total, which agrees with Brief issue 1 and the test output); test block replaced with this review's run; this section.

**Status: ready to show the owner**, with the two decisions above for them to make. What remains is known and out of scope for this slice: no browser check of the new watchdog path in a real browser (it was checked with the Node harness only); §17 timing targets not met without the representative-day tier (Brief issue 2); the slice exclusions listed under "What is not in this slice".


## Slice: Pareto trade-off view (§12.2)

Added 2026-10-15. Scope: the Pareto scatter only. No scoring, dispatch or ranking code was changed: `scoring.js`, `dispatch.js`, `evaluate.js` and `generator.js` are untouched, and `run.js` changed only to plug the function into `HOOKS.paretoView`. The Opus review of 2026-10-16 corrected this section where it misdescribed the code and changed one expression in `scoring.js` (an infinite-cost dominance miss). See "Review: Pareto slice" at the end.

### What was built

- **`src/engine/pareto.js`** (pure, no UI). This is `HOOKS.paretoView(run, axes, view)`. It reads the scores, ranks and `dominatedBy` marks that `scoreOptions` has already written, and returns a view model: visible points, the 2-axis frontier, the weighted pick, time-to-power colour classes and the capex range. Helpers: `paretoFrontier` (O(n log n), both axes minimised, exact ties do not dominate each other; higher-is-better axes are negated first), `weightedPick` (rank 1, the weighted leader; Brief issue 12), `highestWeightedScore` (ties broken by id as in `rankOptions`; marked separately only when it is not rank 1), `paretoTableRows`, `PARETO_AXES` and `defaultParetoAxes`.
- **Two kinds of "dominated", kept apart.** The engine's dominance mark (§7.2 step 6: all four axes plus status) shows as a **hollow** marker. The frontier on the chart is the 2-axis Pareto set for the plotted pair, drawn as a dashed step line. Both are computed over the whole scoring set, so view filters and "Hide dominated options" never move the frontier or the pick (§11 scoring scope).
- **Axes (any two, §12.2).** X and Y can each be any of seven axis metrics: LCOE, NPV of net cost, carbon (location-based, market-based, 1 − hourly CFE), time-to-power and deliverability (higher is better; Brief issue 15). By default the axes follow the scoring Cost and Carbon metrics (records `view.pareto.xAxis` / `yAxis` = "follow-scoring"). "Reset to default axes" restores them. The axis choice is view state, not an input, so changing it never triggers a run. (Before the review only cost × carbon could be plotted, although this note already said "any pair".)
- **Encodings (§12.2).** Colour = time-to-power. Size = capex (marker area grows linearly with capex from a minimum mark: radius 3–9 px, 2.5–7 px on phones; earlier wording "proportional" was not accurate). Shape = **resilience status** (circle Meets, triangle Meets with flags, square Does not meet), because site fit is not assessed yet (see open points). Weighted pick (rank 1) = orange double ring plus the text label "Weighted pick #1". The table row is highlighted, and a pick line above the chart names the option and its two axis values. When "Rank requirement-compliant options first" makes rank 1 differ from the highest weighted score, that option has a dashed ring, "Highest score #k", a table badge and a pick-line sentence with the reason.
- **Weights move the pick without a re-run.** `App.jsx` already re-scores the latest run on a weight change (`scoreOptions` on shallow copies, no dispatch). The Trade-offs tab reads that re-scored run, so the highlight follows the weights. Test 8b measures 27 ms per preset for re-score plus view on 2,000 options. The §17 target is ≤ 100 ms.
- **Dominated options never hidden by default.** The tab shares the ranking table's view state: one `view` object, the same "Hide dominated options" checkbox, default off, counter and "show" link. The initial view comes from `defaultView()`, which reads the `settings.hideDominated` record (false); `DEFAULT_VIEW` in `scoring.js` is unchanged and test 8f checks it equals the record.
- **Option detail.** Click a point (the option the tooltip names: nearest within 16 px, so the hit area is bigger than the mark), or focus the chart and use the arrow keys (left/right along x, up/down along y), Home/End, P for the pick, and Enter or Space to open. This opens the existing `OptionDetail` component below the chart; Enter on the chart always opens (the × button closes). Table row buttons toggle it (`aria-expanded`). The review fixed a click/tooltip mismatch: click events carry whole-pixel coordinates and pointer moves fractional ones, so where markers overlap a click could open a neighbour of the option the tooltip named.
- **Table alternative.** A "Chart / Table" toggle switches to `ParetoTable`, which has one row per point in the same rank order and the same values (rank, name, score, both axis values, time-to-power, capex, resilience, dominance as "Dominated by #k name", frontier, pick), paged at `view.table.pageSize` (50). Options with a non-finite axis value (for example infinite LCOE on T3 no-grid options) are listed in the table as "not plotted" and counted in a note above the chart. The chart is a single `role="application"` tab stop. A polite live region reads out the focused point's full description, and a hover/focus tooltip shows the same values.
- **Phone.** The width comes from a ResizeObserver. Below 520 px the chart goes square, the markers shrink, alternate x ticks are dropped and the controls stack. Checked in headless Chromium at 375 px: no horizontal page scroll, no console errors. Checked the same way at 1400 px in light and dark: keyboard Enter opens the detail, and the table shows 50 rows.
- **Results tabs.** "Options" (the existing ranked list) and "Trade-offs" (new), with ARIA tabs and arrow-key switching (§16 names these tabs).
- **Canvas above 500 points (§4).** Up to `view.pareto.canvasAbove` (500) plotted points the markers are SVG paths. Above that they are drawn on a Canvas under the SVG, and the axes, frontier, pick ring, focus ring and hit-testing stay in SVG. T1 (2,000 points) uses Canvas. Browser check B4 tests the Canvas path (pixels at the pick, pointer hit-test); B5 tests the SVG path.
- **New default records (§23 label rule).** `view.pareto.xAxis` and `view.pareto.yAxis` ("follow-scoring"), `view.pareto.ttpClasses` (5), `view.pareto.canvasAbove` (500), `view.pareto.display` (chart) and, added in the review, `view.table.pageSize` (50, both tables). All are "indicative — unverified" with basis judgement or run setting, and each shows its label in the UI while at its default. Since the review the code reads every one of them from the registry at the point of use (`registryValue`, `src/engine/view.js`); before, the values in use were hard-coded copies. Test 8f changes each record and checks the behaviour follows. Test 5d now counts 377 records.

### Palette decision

The code has **no energy-source colour system** yet. There is no technology colour token in `defaults.js` and no source colours in `styles.css`. The §16 signature palette belongs to the dispatch stacks and site blocks, which are later slices. This chart encodes time-to-power (a magnitude), not energy source, so a source palette would be the wrong tool here anyway. Choices made:

- **Time-to-power: one-hue violet ordinal ramp, 5 steps** (hollow, dominated markers are stroked at full opacity: at the earlier 0.75 opacity the lightest step fell to 2.5:1 light / 2.7:1 dark). Light theme `#9d74c4 #8659b0 #70429b #592f83 #421c69`. Dark theme `#9466bf #a97fcf #bf9bde #d4b9ec #eadcf8`, so in both themes more contrast with the surface means a longer time-to-power. Violet carries on from the existing `--ax-ttp` axis colour (`#8a4fa3`) in the ranking table, so time-to-power reads as violet throughout the app. Both ramps were run through the dataviz palette validator in `--ordinal` mode against the real surfaces (`#ffffff` and `#1e2125`). All checks pass: monotone lightness, adjacent ΔL ≥ 0.06, single hue (spread 2–4°), and the end nearest the surface at 3.67:1 (light) and 3.78:1 (dark), so every marker clears the 3:1 graphics target. A single hue is colour-blind safe by construction, because the classes differ in lightness, not hue.
- **Quantile classes, with ties merged.** Time-to-power on T1 is heavily clustered (the 2nd and 50th percentiles are 18 and 24 months). Tied quantile edges are merged, so T1 shows 4 classes spread across the ramp, and the legend prints each class's month range.
- **Pick and focus.** The pick ring uses the existing `--focus` orange (`#b4560a` light, `#ffb366` dark), which is separate from the violet ramp and from the status colours. The pick always has a text label too, never colour alone. Focus and selection rings use `--focus` and `--accent`.
- **Status colours stay reserved.** Resilience status uses shape plus a text legend, not the ok/warn/bad colours, so status colours are never reused as a data encoding (§16). Dominated is shown by fill (hollow or solid), which also survives greyscale and forced colours.

### Chart library decision

**Hand-written SVG (plus Canvas above 500 points). Recharts was not added.** The weight was measured: a minimal Recharts 2.15.4 import (ScatterChart, Scatter, axes, ZAxis, Tooltip, Line, grid, ResponsiveContainer) bundles to **112 KB gzipped** on top of React, which is more than the whole current app (`dist/app.js` is 109 KB gzipped after this slice). It also pulls in about 30 packages (d3 modules, lodash and others), and every one of them lands in the single-file build. The JS budget (≤ 1.5 MB gzipped) would still be met, so weight alone is not decisive. The deciding reasons are functional:
1. §4 requires Canvas above 500 points. Recharts renders SVG only, so T1's 2,000 points would need a custom Canvas layer anyway, and Recharts would end up drawing only the axes.
2. The keyboard model (arrow keys between points, P for the pick, Enter to open) and the nearest-point hit area would have to be built on top of Recharts' tooltip model, which works by mouse hover.
3. Tests 8d render the real chart with `react-dom/server` and match every plotted point to a table row. Recharts' `ResponsiveContainer` renders nothing without a measured DOM size, so that test would need a browser.

The chart is about 260 lines (`ParetoChart.jsx`). `package.json` is unchanged.

### Tests added (`tests/run-tests.mjs`, 8a–8d)

- **8a** Pareto set on a hand-checked 8-point example (with a tie and a non-finite point), on 200 random seeded sets against brute force, on a 5-option set scored by `scoreOptions` and read through `HOOKS.paretoView`, and on T1 against brute force.
- **8b** For each of the 5 weight presets: the pick equals the hand-computed argmax of Σ wᵢ × axis scoreᵢ and equals rank 1. Exactly one point is highlighted. The pick changes across presets (4 distinct picks). The dispatch and economics objects are the same references, and the dispatch counter does not change.
- **8c** On T1, T3 and the resilience-fail sample: with default settings every option, dominated or not, is in the Pareto view, the ranked list and the ranking (ranks 1..n). Turning on "Hide dominated options" changes only what is shown: scores, ranks, pick and frontier stay the same.
- **8d** Bundles the real `ParetoChart` and `ParetoTable` with esbuild, renders them to static markup, and checks that every plotted point (`data-id` on its SVG path) has a table row with the same id. The row count equals the number of points in the view, the values agree, and the pick is marked in both. Run on 5 cases: default, NPV vs market with dominated hidden, phone width on T3, the resilience-fail sample, and time-to-power vs deliverability (added in the review).
- **8e–8h and `tests/browser-tests.mjs`** were added in the review; see "Review: Pareto slice".

### Open points

1. **Shape encodes resilience, not site fit.** §12.2 says marker shape = site-fit status. Site fit is not computed until the layout slice (`HOOKS.layout`). When it is, shape should move to site fit, and resilience could move to an outline style. The chart says this in a note.
2. **No P10–P90 error crosses.** These depend on the Monte Carlo / uncertainty slice. Points are P50 from the full-year hourly model.
3. **No brushing on the axes.** That belongs to the parallel-coordinates part of §12.2, which is not in this slice.
4. **Canvas draws all 2,000 points each time.** Drawing and hit-testing are O(n) per pointer move. Fine at 2,000 points; it may need a spatial index at higher caps.
5. **Screen-reader walk-through not done.** The chart is checked by keyboard in headless Chromium (browser checks B2–B4) and axe reports no serious or critical violation in light and dark (B7, B11). NVDA + Firefox and VoiceOver + Safari (§17) are still to do.
6. **Pick when "Rank requirement-compliant options first" is on.** Resolved in the review: the pick follows rank 1 and the highest weighted score is marked separately (Brief issue 12).

## Review: Pareto slice

Opus review of the three Haiku checks of the Pareto view (H1 claims, H2 brief, H3 browser), 2026-10-16. Full record: `/home/claude/build/verification/pareto/OPUS-review.md`. It has a decision and a reason for every finding, the verifier errors, the mutation check and the test output. Originals of every changed file are in `/tmp/claude-0/-home-claude/a66cf92d-3564-56e6-89ee-bb0870057f0d/scratchpad/pareto-review-backup/`. The repo copy (`/home/claude/dc-learning`) was not changed.

**Decisions: 92 accepted, 0 rejected, 6 deferred** (98 findings). The deferred items are listed under "What is not in this slice" and "Brief issues":
- site-fit shape;
- the land-take filter (site slice);
- the leader-specific defaults count (uncertainty slice);
- the option-card drawer (option-card work).

**What changed.**
1. **Contrast (H3).** `--ink-3` (light) `#6b7079` → `#62676f`: lowest 4.65:1 on every light background it sits on (it was 4.07–4.25:1 on surface-2, info, warn and bad). Also fixed:
   - a new `--control-border` for form-control boundaries (WCAG 1.4.11; the old border was 1.47:1);
   - hollow markers at full opacity (2.5:1 at 0.75);
   - `.tag-basis` opacity removed.
   
   Every text and graphic token pair was computed for both themes. axe 4.10.2 (WCAG 2.0/2.1/2.2 A and AA rules) reports 0 serious and 0 critical on Trade-offs chart, Trade-offs table and Options, in light and in dark. The run-log rows axe leaves as "needs review" (clipped by the scroll box) are checked from computed styles: lowest 4.86:1 light, 5.00:1 dark.
2. **Keyboard reach (H3).**
   - "Skip to trade-offs chart" link: it switches the tab, shows the chart and focuses it.
   - The "unverified" tags are no longer tab stops; their basis is the described control's accessible description.
   - The closed off-canvas inputs panel (below 1280 px) is out of the tab order.
   - Chart: 2 Tab + Enter (results: 1 Tab + Enter). Tab only from the top: 208 presses, down from 261; 174 of them in the inputs panel, down from 228.
   - Input sections were not collapsed by default. Collapsing would hide the pre-filled example inputs (steps 1–4) and the C11 row that AC13 refers to. The skip links meet WCAG 2.4.1.
3. **Registry-driven view settings (H1).** `view.pareto.*`, the new `view.table.pageSize` and `settings.hideDominated` are read at the point of use (`registryValue`, `src/engine/view.js`). Test 8f changes each record and checks the behaviour follows, and fails if a `view.*` record has no check.
4. **AC5 (H2).** The pick is rank 1, from `weightedPick()`, which the header leader and live region also use. When compliant-first puts a "Does not meet" top scorer lower, it is marked separately with the reason (Brief issue 12, test 8g).
5. **Dominance with infinite cost (H2 D17).** Confirmed with a reproduction and fixed in `scoring.js`: the tolerance uses a finite scale. Regression test 8e. T1 and T3 scores, ranks, dominance, axis scores and metrics are identical before and after on all 4,000 options.
6. **Other §12.2 deviations (H2).**
   - Any two of seven axes (direction-aware frontier).
   - The undrawn pick says why.
   - "Dominated by #k name".
   - Status clause in the dominance note.
   - "Shown" count in the header.
   - Removable chips on both tabs.
   - Controls for the capex-ceiling, has-flag and dominated-status filters.
   - Relative-to-set note on Trade-offs.
   - Chart Enter opens (not toggles).
   - "Clear all filters" now keeps the sort order.
7. **Defect found by the new browser test.** A click opened the nearest marker to the whole-pixel click position, not the one the tooltip named (pointer moves are fractional), so on overlapping markers a click at the pick opened #968. Fixed: the click opens the hovered option.
8. **Tests.**
   - Node tests 8e–8h added; 8a, 8b and 8d extended.
   - `tests/browser-tests.mjs` (`npm run test:browser`): 13 checks covering the Canvas path (pixels, hit-test), the SVG path, keyboard navigation, table paging through all pages, skip links, removable chips, the tablet panel, phone overflow and axe in both themes.
   - `axe-core` 4.10.2 added as an exact devDependency.
   - Four mutants (hard-coded Canvas threshold, old pick, old tolerance, hard-coded hide-dominated default) each fail exactly the test that guards them.

Size: `dist/energy-tool-single-file.html` 475 KB (was 449 KB). `dist/worker.js` is 111 KB (was 98 KB) because the worker bundle now keeps `buildDefaultInputs` behind `registryValue`.

**Still open.**

- §17 timing: a weight preset repaints the pick in 96–130 ms in headless Chromium on this 2-core sandbox (target ≤ 100 ms on the 4-core reference machine; measured, not asserted).
- Core-slice run constants without a §18 record: last 200 events, progress every 250, 30 s watchdog, 200-event / 120 ms stream batches. Not part of this slice.
