## Slice: Site (§10)

Added 2026-10-16. Scope: brief §20 stage 2, the site slice: site model (§5.2, §10.1), blocks (§10.3), auto-layout (§10.4), site-fit outputs (§10.6), site views (§10.7, 2D plan, small multiples, table alternative) and the integration of site fit into the run, the ranked list, the option detail, the Pareto view and the run summary. Built on the Pareto-slice code (commit 60fe41a); the orchestrator checkpointed the work in progress as 87291e3 on `feature/site-layout`. This slice made no change to git (no commit, checkout, branch or index change). The one git command run was a read-only `git archive 60fe41a energy-strategy-tool/app` into the scratchpad (`baseline60fe41a/`), to run the baseline for the comparison below.

### What was built

**Engine (new files, pure, no UI)**
- `src/engine/geometry.js`: own geometry kernel (no Turf or Clipper, so the single file stays offline and small). Local equirectangular projection on WGS84 radii, signed areas, ellipsoid quad area, ring validation (self-intersection, spikes, minimum area), point/segment/box distances, point-in-polygon, minimum-area rectangle, inward offset with per-edge distances (mitre at convex vertices, arc at reflex vertices with equal setbacks), convex clipping, Greiner–Hormann difference with deterministic perturbation for shared edges, integral image, GeoJSON and KML import and export.
- `src/engine/site.js`: site model. Boundary from a drawn, typed or imported polygon, or the area + aspect rectangle (long side east–west, on the project location). Per-edge setbacks in the user's edge order. Typed exclusions (hard/soft per type, overridable per zone), height zones, POC, gas main, access points, noise receptors (points or areas), off-site land and private-wire distance. Derived: gross area, buildable envelope polygon (setbacks, then hard exclusions cut out), raster envelope, roads/parking/landscaping allowance, net developable area. Raster in a frame aligned with the site's minimum-area rectangle.
- `src/engine/blocks.js`: option → blocks. Data halls (one block per phase, split above 25,000 m², massing from the §5.3 density, gross-up and storeys), substation (AIS/GIS by voltage band), backup gensets as unit arrays (single-deck, double-deck, or a plant room added to the halls), fuel storage, firm plant (gas engines, turbines, fuel cells, hydrogen), BESS containers, ground heat rejection modules, wind pads, rooftop PV and roof plant (on the halls, counted in the building height), off-site tray (SMR by default, off-site wind), and the ground-solar fill (fixed MWp, or "fill residual").
- `src/engine/layout.js`: the auto-layout (algorithm below), batch layout with prefix sharing, cache by block-set key.
- `src/engine/sitefit.js`: §10.6 outputs and the §15 "Site fit" flags (below).
- `src/engine/siteapi.js`: `layoutForOption()` = `HOOKS.layout`, the same code path the run uses, for the plan and the detail on demand.

**Engine (changed):** `defaults.js` (site inputs, spatial library, exclusion types, layout run settings, noise threshold, land-take ceiling, site-fit penalty points, T2 and T4 fixtures, `view.site.smallMultiples`; `normalizeInputs` takes the site area from a valid boundary), `context.js` (site model in the run context), `run.js` (new "layout" stage, site warnings in the context stage, site-fit flags, C17/C18 notes, `HOOKS.layout`, engine version 0.2.0-site), `runlog.js` (stage list and event texts), `sizing.js`, `evaluate.js`, `scoring.js` (each change listed below), `view.js` (site-fit and land-take chips, site flags in the flag filter), `pareto.js` (points carry site fit and land take), `index.js` (exports).

**UI:** a **Site** results tab (`SiteView.jsx`, `site/SitePlan.jsx`, `site/SiteFitPanel.jsx`, `site/SiteEditor.jsx`, `site/BlocksTable.jsx`); site-fit badge and land-take column, filters and sorts in the ranked list; site-fit summary with the unplaced tray and a "Show this option's layout on the Site tab" button in the option detail; Pareto marker shape = site fit (circle Fits, triangle Fits with flags, square Does not fit) with the legend, tooltip, description and table column; header strip site-fit counts and the penalty state; run summary "Site layout" section and the C17/C18 notes; inputs panel site settings, land-take ceiling, noise threshold, site-fit penalty toggle and raster settings.

**Site tab (§10.7).**
- 2D plan, north up, plain plan (no tiles), with layer toggles: boundary, setbacks, exclusions and height zones (hatched; zone limits labelled), blocks (energy-source colour + hatch + short text label where it fits), clearance rings, flags (flag-coloured outline and "!"), POC/access/gas/receptors, dimensions, edge numbers. Scale bar and north arrow.
- Dimensions and a details tooltip on hover **and** on keyboard focus: the plan is one `role="application"` tab stop; arrow keys move between blocks in placement order (Home, End, Escape); a polite live region reads the block's name, size, area, height, clearance, position and any relaxed rule; the matching table row is highlighted. On narrow plans the tooltip sits under the plan.
- Site input that works on a phone: area + aspect rectangle; tap (or click) to draw the boundary, exclusions, height zones and receptor areas, and to place the POC, gas main, access points and receptors; the same with the keyboard (crosshair: arrows 1 m, Shift 10 m, Enter adds, Backspace undoes, F finishes, Escape cancels); typed coordinates ("lat, lon" per line) for the boundary and in every drawing mode; GeoJSON or KML import with a "use as" choice per feature. Per-edge setbacks table, exclusion and zone lists with type, hard/soft and limit editing.
- Site-fit panel (§10.6) for the shown option; small multiples of the top `view.site.smallMultiples` (6) options at one scale (thumbnails laid out one per tick after mount); "Plan as a table" (the text alternative: every block with centre E/N, size, area, height, clearance and status, then the site facts), reachable from the plan with one link.
- The plan always shows the current site inputs. While a site edit is being re-screened the blocks are hidden and a notice says the layouts update when the run finishes.

**Site fit in the run.** Distribution at defaults: T1 963 Fits, 23 Fits with flags, 1,014 Does not fit (2,000 options; the top 20 by rank all fit); T2 (6 ha, 15 m) 0 / 0 / 2,000; T4 (5 MW, 1 ha) 272 / 6 / 1,722. No option is hidden for not fitting, and none is demoted unless the user turns on "Rank requirement-compliant options first" (since the review it ranks "Does not fit" after the options that fit, §11); with the penalty off the site-fit status changes no score (test 9g). Site inputs are not statuses: they size fill-residual solar after the layout (§10.4) and, since the review, set the grid cable and gas pipe route lengths for cost (§5.2), so they do move costs, carbon and scores.

### Algorithm (§10.4)

1. **Raster.** The buildable envelope is rasterised in the layout frame (u along the site's minimum-area rectangle): 2 m cells, 5 m above 50 ha gross (run settings). A cell is buildable only if the whole cell is inside the boundary, at least its edge's setback from every boundary edge (exact per-edge distance per cell), and touches no hard exclusion, so every placed block lies inside the envelope with no tolerance. Per cell the raster also keeps soft-exclusion membership, the height limit (lowest zone touched; the site limit unless wholly inside a zone) and the distance to the boundary.
2. **Order** (`PRIORITY`, the brief's list): data halls; substation; backup gensets and fuel storage; firm generation; BESS; ground heat rejection; wind; then ground solar fills what is left.
3. **Anchor costs** (lowest cost wins; ties: lower row, then column, then unrotated): first hall farthest from the POC, further halls next to placed halls; substation nearest the POC; gensets within 30 m of a hall, then farthest from receptors when there are any, else nearest the halls; fuel next to the gensets, then toward the access point; firm plant nearest the gas main when it burns gas, else farthest from receptors, else next to the substation; BESS next to the substation; ground heat rejection next to the halls (farthest from receptors when there are any); wind farthest from the boundary and halls.
4. **Rules.** For each block a forbidden mask is painted: outside the envelope, soft exclusions (except for solar where allowed), cells within the boundary separation, cells above the height limit, and the true rectangle of every placed block dilated by the required separation (the larger of the two clearance rings or a pairwise rule such as BESS-to-building 10 m or fuel-to-building 6 m). A vertical free-run array over the mask makes one row scan find every free position of a rectangle (the equivalent of an integral-image test); rows are visited in order of their cost lower bound and the scan stops when no row can beat the best.
5. **Relaxation** (soft rules only, `LAYOUT_LEVELS`): L0 default rules; L1 separations at their minimum values; L2 height limit exceeded; L3 both; L4 also soft exclusions. A block goes in at the first level that works; every rule it breaks at its position is recorded and named ("Fits with flags"). Hydrogen's 25 m hazardous-area separation is never relaxed; wind topple and noise buffers are flag-only.
6. **Land budget.** The roads/parking/landscaping allowance is held back as a budget, not a located area: a block that would take the placed land take above the net developable area is not placed.
7. **Unplaced tray.** A block with no position at L4 (or over budget) goes to the tray with its area; the option "Does not fit", and the flag names every unplaced block and the shortfall in m².
8. **Solar fill** (§10.4 "iteration with sizing"). Ground PV takes the remaining buildable cells with its 5 m clearance, leaving out strips under 10 m wide, within the remaining net budget. For "fill residual" options the ground PV MWp is then the filled area ÷ 1.2 ha/MWp (technology library; `applySolarFill`, before dispatch); for "fixed" PV a shortfall is unplaced area. No other sizing depends on the layout.
9. **Batch and cache.** Options with the same block-set key share one layout (T1: 2,000 options → 1,324 distinct layouts). The distinct sets are laid out as one batch sorted so shared leading blocks are placed once (a stack of placement states: the 1,324 T1 sets hold 30,299 blocks, but only 2,956 placement attempts run), with a memo of failed searches. Each result equals the single-set run (test 9d checks 31 sets). Deterministic: no randomness, fixed tie-breaks.
10. **Timing.** T1 layout stage 3.5 s single thread for 2,000 options (2.6 ms per distinct layout including the facts and site fit; §17 target ≤ 20 ms per option on a 15 ha site).

### Site-fit outputs (§10.6) and flags

- **Status**: "Does not fit" when any block (or fixed ground PV area) is unplaced; else "Fits with flags" when a rule was relaxed, a height limit is exceeded (height is soft in placement: an over-height block is placed and flagged), the roof is over-allocated, a wind buffer is not met, hydrogen or an SMR is present, off-site land falls short of what the option needs, or the noise proxy is above the threshold at a receptor; else "Fits".
- **Area budget bar** by energy source against the net developable area, with the not-placed part beyond 100 % hatched in the flag colour, and the same numbers as a table. **Land take** on site (all blocks + ground PV, placed or not) and off site; **shortfall** beyond 100 %; **residual** net area; **expansion headroom** = residual net area ÷ land take per MW IT of the IT-scaling blocks (an estimate; 0 when the option does not fit).
- **Height check** per zone (site limit and each zone): tallest element (building + roof zone, stacks, wind tip) and pass/exceeded.
- **Separation checklist**: each rule with its required, minimum and achieved distance and pass / relaxed / fail (a layout never breaks a minimum: test 9e), plus the genset-distance rule, the POC preference for the substation and the wind buffers.
- **Noise proxy**, labelled "screening proxy, not an acoustic assessment": Lp = Lw − 20·log10(r) − 8 (hemispherical spreading only); distance and level from each noisy block to the nearest receptor and to the boundary; receptor levels for normal operation and for standby gensets separately; flag above `requirements.noiseNightDbA` (45 dB(A) at night) at receptors only. The highest normal-operation level on the boundary is reported for information.
- **Roof use**: roof area split into plant, PV, edge setback and free (over-allocation flagged).
- **Flags** (§15 "Site fit"): `site-does-not-fit` (major), `site-relaxed`, `site-height`, `site-soft`, `site-genset-distance`, `site-wind-buffer`, `site-hydrogen`, `site-offsite-land`, `site-roof`, `site-noise` (minor), `site-soft-solar`, `site-smr` (info); `target-landtake` (Targets and ceilings, minor) when a land-take ceiling is set and exceeded. None of them carries `permitting: true`, so none enters deliverability (see judgement call 13).

### New defaults and labels

112 new §18 records, all "indicative — unverified", confidence C, `verified: false` (basis: judgement 70, source 38, run setting 4); test 5d now counts 489 records. **Review (2026-10-17):** three more spatial records replace literals in the code (`spatial.substation.mvPowerFactor`, `spatial.smr.lw`, `spatial.smr.clearanceM`, judgement; marked "(review)" in the table), `tech.substation.cableKm` (a fixed 1.0 km "POC to site") is removed because the route length now comes from the site model, and `tech.gasConnection.pipePerKm` (€0.8 m/km, range 0.4–2.0, source to verify: gas network connection charging statements) is new; test 5d counts 492. Two existing records changed only their note: `site.roadsFraction` (the allowance is applied to the envelope, because the brief's definition is circular) and `deliverability.siteFitPenalty` (now effective). No existing value changed. The 38 "source" records name the source to verify against (utility and TSO/DSO design standards, manufacturer datasheets, NFPA 855 / FM Global DS 5-33 / UL 9540A, national fuel-storage and hazardous-substance rules, the Danish Energy Agency technology catalogue, an NREL land-use study, ISO 9613-2 for the spreading term); none is verified. Every spatial default is indicative — unverified." under the site-fit panel; the "unverified" tag on the site input fields at their defaults (inputs panel and Site tab editor) and on the raster settings in the run summary.

### Changes to `scoring.js`, `sizing.js` and `evaluate.js`

**`scoring.js`**
1. `siteRank()` added; `markDominance()` now lets option j dominate option i only if j's site-fit status is at least as good as i's (as already done for resilience). Reason: §7.2 step 6, "Pareto-dominated … AND no better resilience or site-fit status"; the core slice compared resilience only because site fit did not exist. Dominance is a mark, not a score: no score or rank changes (test 9g). Effect at defaults: T1 dominated 1,950 → 1,947, T3 1,910 → 1,895 (these counts also move with the metric changes below).
2. `DEFAULT_VIEW` gains `siteFit: []` and `landTakeCeilingHa: null`; `applyView()` filters by them and adds a chip for each. Reason: §11 "the user can filter the view by site-fit status … or land-take ceiling". View only: filters never change a score or rank (tests 4, 8h, 9g).
3. `SORTERS` gain `landTake` and `siteFit`, for the ranked list's new columns. View only.
4. (Review, H2 D9.) `isRequirementCompliant()`: "Rank requirement-compliant options first" (off by default) now ranks an option after the compliant ones when its resilience status is "Does not meet" **or** its site-fit status is "Does not fit". Reason: §11 names both statuses next to the toggle, and §5.7 calls resilience and site fit the two requirement checks; the slice had kept the core's resilience-only rule. Scores never change, only the order with the toggle on; the Pareto pick line names the failing check(s). Test 8g has a site-fit case, and its real-data assertion accepts either reason.

**`sizing.js`**
1. The area budget reads the site model's gross area, buildable envelope (per-edge setbacks and hard exclusions) and net developable area when the run context has one (it always does in a run); the old rectangle formula remains for a context without a site model. Reason: §5.2 derived areas. For the T1/T3 area rectangle with a uniform setback the two give the same numbers, so the pre-layout estimate did not change.
2. The "fill residual" sizing sentence now reads "area-budget estimate before layout … (replaced by the layout fill, §10.4)". Reason: it is replaced by step 3.
3. New `applySolarFill(sz, fill)`: after the layout, "fill residual" ground PV MWp = filled area ÷ ha/MWp; `solarMWp`, the area parts, land take and residual follow, and the sentence states the layout figure next to the estimate. Reason: §10.4 "'fill residual' solar sizing is computed after the other blocks are placed. No other sizing depends on layout." This is the only sizing change and the cause of every difference from the baseline below.

**`evaluate.js`**
1. The `site-area-budget` info flag (area sum vs net area, "site layout and fit status are not assessed in this slice") is removed. Reason: replaced by the layout-based site-fit flags (§15). It was info severity, so it never entered deliverability.
2. `deliverability(ctx, v, sz, disp, flags, siteFit)`: when `deliverability.siteFitPenalty` is on (off by default) an option loses 10 points for "Fits with flags" and 25 for "Does not fit", and the result carries `includesSiteFit` for the "Deliverability (incl. site fit)" label. Reason: §9.9 optional site-fit penalty, §11 label. With the penalty off the formula is the baseline's, item for item (test 9g checks all 2,000 T1 options with site fit real, removed and scrambled).

### Changes to existing tests (all still pass)

- **6a** (per-option stage list): `layout` added between `sizing` and `dispatch`, because the run has a new per-option stage; the summary text now prints `STAGE_IDS.length` (13) instead of a hard-coded 12.
- **6c** (worker pool): the expected worker stage list was `STAGE_IDS.slice(0, 11)` (the first 11 of 12 stages); it is now every stage except scoring and ranking, because the stage list grew to 13 with "layout" and workers now run 12 stages. The assertion is otherwise unchanged.
- **6d**: text only (stage counts from `STAGE_IDS.length`).
- **8f**: `view.site.smallMultiples` added to the probed list with a usage check (rendered Site tab: 6 thumbnails, the top 6 by rank; record 3 → 3, 0 → none), because the test fails by design when a `view.*` record has no check.
- **8h**: four view states with site-fit and land-take filters added, plus a render check of their chips.
- **Browser tests**: `openPage` takes `hasTouch` (for B14); `BROWSER_ONLY=<regex>` runs a subset (development aid; the default runs everything). B1–B13 unchanged; B1 still passes with three tabs (ArrowRight from Options selects Trade-offs). New: B14 phone width with tap drawing, B15 keyboard (plan, table, keyboard drawing), B16 GeoJSON import, B17/B18 axe light/dark on the Site tab (leader, an option that does not fit, drawing mode) with the needs-review contrast check.

### Comparison with the pre-slice baseline (60fe41a), T1 and T3

Both engines run in the same Node process on the same inputs (`referenceT1` / `referenceT3`, start 2027-01); options matched by id (same 2,000 ids in both, nothing missing).

| | T1 | T3 |
|---|---|---|
| "Fill residual" ground PV options (C10 = roof + residual) | 685 | 685 |
| … whose ground PV MWp changed | 685 (−1.50 to +1.24 MWp, mean −0.06) | 685 (−1.61 to +1.70 MWp, mean −0.07) |
| Options with a different LCOE | 685, all fill-residual (largest change 0.12 %) | 685, all fill-residual (73 above 0.1 %, 17 above 1 % with the change divided by the larger of the two values, 21 if divided by the baseline value; largest 0.61 → 0.86 €/MWh on an option whose LCOE is near zero, ground PV 0.57 → 1.15 MWp) |
| Options with a different capex | 685 (largest 1.2 %) | 685 (largest 2.3 %) |
| Carbon metric changed | 685 | 685 |
| Deliverability changed | 353 (fill-residual options that also have emerging technology: the capacity and energy shares change with the PV size) | 353 |
| Time-to-power changed | 0 | 0 |
| The other 1,315 options | LCOE, capex, carbon, deliverability and time-to-power identical | identical |
| Weighted score changed | 2,000 (largest 0.074 points) | 1,999 (largest 0.015 points) |
| Rank changed | 804 (largest 7 places; top 20 unchanged) | 719 (largest 19 places; top 20 the same set, but ranks 4 and 5 swap and rank 10 changes from OQKRTJ7 to OAS6AIL) |
| Leader | unchanged: O1YAUJ8J "Grid · Diesel N+1 · DLC · PV 7.7 MWp · PPA hybrid 100 %", 85.964 → 85.963 (its ground PV 7.8 → 7.7 MWp total) | unchanged: O1Q4ILX5, 70.891 → 70.892 |
| Distinct dispatch configurations | 1,732 → 1,769 | 1,721 → 1,748 |

Causes, and nothing else:
1. **Fill-residual solar sized after layout** (`applySolarFill`, §10.4): the layout fills the real free cells (5 m clearance, strips under 10 m dropped, conservative raster) instead of the area sum, so those 685 options get a different ground PV MWp, hence capex, LCOE, carbon and, where emerging technology is present, deliverability. Different PV sizes also give 37 (T1) and 27 (T3) more distinct dispatch configurations.
2. **Relative scoring scale** (§11, scores are relative to the set): cause 1 moved the axis extremes (T1: cost maximum 286.2598 → 286.2541 €/MWh, carbon minimum 112.1599 → 112.1764 g/kWh, deliverability maximum 97.1008 → 97.0979; T3: deliverability maximum 95.1396 → 95.1375), so every option's normalised scores shift slightly and close neighbours swap ranks.
3. Site-fit status itself changes no score or rank (penalty off), and options without fill-residual PV have identical metrics.

### Energy-source colour system (§16) and the palette validator

The site blocks use the eight categorical hexes of the reference palette (light `#2a78d6 #eb6834 #1baf7a #eda100 #e87ba4 #008300 #6250d6 #e34948`, dark `#3987e5 #d95926 #199e70 #c98500 #d55181 #008300 #9085e9 #e66767`), assigned by convention: grid/substation blue, diesel gensets and their fuel store orange (fuel with a different hatch), gas red, BESS aqua, fuel cells green, solar yellow, nuclear magenta, hydrogen violet, wind the grid blue (with its own hatch). Data halls and heat-rejection plant are infrastructure, not energy sources, and use two neutrals (light `#d7d2c6` / `#a9a59b`, dark `#5a5e66` / `#80858d`). Validator (`validate_palette.js`, surfaces = the plan's envelope fill `#fbfaf6` light, `#2a2e34` dark):

- Eight hexes, adjacent pairs (the check for stacks and bars): **pass** in both modes (worst adjacent CVD ΔE 9.1 light / 8.4 dark, normal-vision 19.6 / 19.3). Contrast below 3:1 for aqua, yellow, magenta (light) and green (dark): relief = every block's ink outline, text label, hatch, legend and table.
- Eight hexes, `--pairs all` (the right check for a plan, where any two blocks can touch): **fail** in both modes (light: green–orange CVD ΔE 3.2, red–orange normal 7.1; dark: magenta–aqua CVD 1.6, red–orange 7.1). The reference says no ordering of eight colours passes all pairs; such forms cap at three colours or need composite encoding.
- First three slots (blue, orange, aqua) all pairs: pass. The most common trio on T1 plans, grid + diesel + gas (blue, orange, red): fail (orange–red normal ΔE 7.1).

Decisions: (1) colour is never the only carrier on the plan (§16 "site blocks have hatch patterns"): each source has its own hatch (gas 135° vs diesel 45° diagonals, fuel a plus grid, BESS a square grid, solar vertical, fuel cells dashes, heat rejection dots, hydrogen large dots, nuclear diagonal cross, wind rings), a short text label on every block large enough (Hall, Sub, Gen, Fuel, BESS, Eng, GT, FC, H₂, HR, WT), a legend, the tooltip and live text, and the table; (2) heat rejection was moved from violet to a neutral, which removes the worst frequent collapse (grid–heat rejection, blue–violet, CVD ΔE 1.9 dark, in 1,340 of 2,000 T1 plans); (3) re-assigning hues to maximise separation of the pairs that co-occur was evaluated over all 40,320 assignments: the best raises the co-occurrence-weighted pass rate from 73 % to 83 % but puts diesel in green and BESS in violet, so the conventional assignment was kept. With heat rejection neutral and the fuel store sharing the diesel hue, a T1 plan shows a median of 4 source hues (maximum 6; 595 of 2,000 plans show 3 or fewer) besides the two neutrals.

### Judgement calls (each also in `SITE-PROGRESS.md`)

1. Own geometry kernel instead of Turf/Clipper (offline single file, size); Greiner–Hormann with deterministic perturbation for degenerate cases.
2. Local equirectangular projection on WGS84 radii (M, N at the origin latitude), about the boundary centroid, or the project location for the area rectangle.
3. Offsets: mitre at convex vertices; arc at reflex vertices with equal setbacks (exact erosion); mitre at reflex vertices with unequal setbacks (conservative). The raster applies exact per-edge distances per cell.
4. The raster is conservative: a cell is buildable only if the whole square is inside the envelope and touches no hard exclusion. A cell takes the lowest height limit it touches, and the site limit unless it lies wholly inside a zone (zones override the site limit up or down).
5. Roads/parking/landscaping allowance = fraction × envelope (the brief defines it as a fraction of the net developable area, which is circular); net developable = envelope − allowance; enforced as a land budget in placement, not as a located area.
6. Default POC = the default access point (no road data offline, so "nearest boundary point to the nearest road" cannot be computed); default access = mid-point of the longest edge.
7. Exclusion type defaults: only flood zones are soft.
8. T2 fixture: T1 with 6 ha and a 15 m height limit. T4 fixture: 5 MW, 1 ha, grid 8 MW now and at full connection (date = start), one 5 MW phase at month 12.
9. Building: one block per phase, split above 25,000 m²; building clearance 6 m (fire access); first hall farthest from the POC, further halls next to placed halls.
10. Unit arrays: at most 12 units, 2 rows, 4,000 m² per array (engines and turbines get fewer units per array); double-deck = ceil(units × `spatial.genset.doubleDeckFootprintFactor`) stacked positions of one unit footprint (0.5: two units per position, half the footprint per unit, 12 m high; the sizing area budget uses the same record since the review); in-building gensets add a plant room to the halls.
11. The height limit is soft in placement (prefer compliant cells, else place and flag): an over-height block makes "Fits with flags", never "Does not fit". At the defaults (2 storeys × 6 m + 4 m roof zone = 16 m) every hall exceeds T2's 15 m limit. The reasoning is in Brief issue 18 (kept after the review).
12. Relaxation levels L0–L4 with a minimum value per rule (judgement); hydrogen's 25 m never relaxed; wind topple and noise buffers flag-only.
13. Noise proxy: hemispherical spreading only; normal operation and standby gensets reported separately; flag only at receptors (boundary level shown for information). The noise flag has category Permitting (§15) but `permitting: false`: it does not enter deliverability, because §5.7/§11 keep site fit out of the score by default and the proxy is not a permitting determination.
14. SMR off site by default (1 ha per 100 MWe); an SMR, hydrogen and an off-site land shortfall each give "Fits with flags".
15. Dominance includes site-fit status (§7.2 step 6).
16. The run keeps a compact site-fit summary per option; the plan and the §10.6 detail are recomputed on demand through `HOOKS.layout` (the same code path; 9d checks they agree).
17. Performance: batch layout with prefix sharing, failure memo, cost-bounded row/column search.
18. Site tab: the plan always shows the current site inputs, with blocks only when the run matches them (site key); layouts for the plan and the thumbnails are recomputed on demand and cached per run and option.
19. Typed coordinates are "lat, lon" per line with a decimal point. GeoJSON/KML import offers a "use as" choice per feature, pre-set from its name (first polygon = boundary); holes in an imported boundary become hard exclusions (type Other); an off-site polygon sets only the off-site area (not drawn).
20. A new boundary resets the per-edge setbacks (the edge count and order change).
21. Heat rejection and data halls draw in neutrals (see the palette section); hydrogen takes violet.
22. Option selector lists every option in rank order (no extra constant); the leader is shown unless another option is chosen (from the selector, a thumbnail, or "Show this option's layout" in the option detail).
23. The site-fit penalty points (10 for "Fits with flags", 25 for "Does not fit") are judgement; the penalty stays off by default.

### Deferred (recorded, not built), by §20 stage

Re-filed in the review (H2 D16–D19): the slice had listed every item under stage 7, but several belong to stage 2 or 6.

**Stage 2 gaps (site slice content, not built):**
- The image/PDF underlay scaled by two reference points (§5.2, §10.2 "the user's underlay if provided"); the plan is the default plain plan.
- User-drawn building footprint override (§5.3, §10.4 step 2.1 "or the user-drawn building").
- Heat reuse plant blocks (§10.4 step 2.6, §6.3 30 m² per MWth): heat reuse is not sized or costed yet (C15 locked to None by default), so there is nothing to place.
- Carport PV block (§6.2 "over parking allowance", §10.3; review K01): carport PV is sized and costed, but the roads and parking allowance is a land budget, not a located area (judgement 5), so the carport has no position to draw. The plan table now says so for any option with carport PV. C10 "Roof + carport" is not generated by default.
- Roads allowance driven by the access points (§5.2 "drives roads allowance"; review I09b): the brief gives the allowance as 15 % of the net developable area and no rule for how access points change it; fuel-delivery placement toward the access point is built (layout.js fuel anchor).
- Multi-parcel boundaries (§10.1 "polygon(s)"; review D27): the first polygon is the boundary, a second is ignored with a note (judgement 19).
- Pinned options in the small multiples (pinning does not exist yet).

**Stage 6:** export of the site and layout as GeoJSON or images (§12.10, §20 stage 6 exports); the flags register with each flag's triggering value, threshold and input link (§15; review D24, all flags, not only site flags).

**Stage 7:**
- §10.5 manual layout editing (drag, rotate, re-arrange, live rule checks, keyboard moves, saved manual layouts) and "manual block editing is disabled with a notice" on phones (nothing to disable yet).
- 2.5D massing with the height-limit plane and sun position (§10.7, optional).
- Online map tiles (§10.2).
- The accessibility audit with the screen-reader walk-through (§17, AC11; review T03).
- Performance tuning to §17 (site edit → all layouts ≤ 1.0 s, review T02).

**No stage in §20:** undo/redo for site edits (§16; no undo exists in the app yet); library values editable in the UI, including the unit-array row/column arrangement (§6, §10.3; review R16, D28).

### Open points

1. **Near-margin spatial failures.** 280 of the 1,014 T1 options that do not fit need less land than the net developable area: the conservative raster, separations and anchor heuristics leave no position for one block. The plan shows why; manual editing (stage 7) is the brief's remedy.
2. **Half of T1 does not fit on 15 ha at the default densities**: air cooling 457 of 513, evaporative 334 of 482, immersion 148 of 507, DLC 75 of 498. The building footprint follows the §5.3 density, gross-up and storeys defaults (indicative); worth checking with the client's building data before reading much into it.
3. **AC7 margin.** On T2 with C17 GIS and C18 double-deck included, 2 of the 2,000 sampled options (196,996 combinations) fit with flags, both DLC with GIS: O1QXJXIC "Grid · HVO N+1 · DLC · PPA hybrid 100 %" (single-deck) and O156F15W "Grid · HVO N+1 · DLC" (double-deck, no PPA); in both the halls exceed 15 m. (Corrected in the review: this line said both had a PPA hybrid.) Deterministic, so the test is stable, but thin; locking cooling to DLC gives more fitting options (not re-checked in the review).
4. **§17 timing.** Auto-layout per option is well inside 20 ms. The whole T1 run is 9.4–11.8 s single thread in Node (layout 3.5 s, dispatch 5.2 s) against the 3.0 s screening target, which the core slice already missed (Brief issue 2); in headless Chromium on this 2-core sandbox the run takes 13 s on 1 worker. A site edit re-screens every option (fill-residual solar changes dispatch), so "site edit → all layouts ≤ 1.0 s" is not met either.
5. **Noise flag vs deliverability** (judgement call 13): §15 lists "noise proxy above threshold" under Permitting, and §9.9 counts permitting flags in deliverability, while §5.7/§11 keep site fit out of the score unless the penalty is on. This build keeps the noise flag out of deliverability: it is category Permitting with `permitting: false` (it carries the note "Screening flag, not a permitting determination"). Owner decision whether it should count. **Stack height (corrected in the review):** this point used to say a stack above the limit is only part of the site-fit "Height limit exceeded" flag. In fact the core slice already raises the §15 Permitting flag `permit-stack` ("Stack height N m above the M m height limit", `permitting: true`, minor) from the site height limit, and it does count in deliverability (−3); the site-fit height check also lists stacks per height zone (category Site fit, not counted). So the height limit input can move deliverability through the stack flag, as §9.9 says.
6. **Palette**: plans fail the all-pairs colour checks, as any eight-colour palette would; identity relies on hatch, label, legend and table. Future dispatch stacks should stack sources in the palette's slot order to keep the adjacent-pair guarantee.
7. **Spatial library values in the UI.** The 87 spatial records are labelled in the registry and covered by test 5d, and the Site tab says every spatial default is indicative — unverified, but there is no per-value library page yet.
8. **Screen-reader walk-through** of the Site tab with NVDA + Firefox and VoiceOver + Safari (§17) not done; checked with axe, keyboard and the live-region text in headless Chromium.
9. **Large sites.** The 5 m raster above 50 ha (T5) is in place but T5 itself is not a fixture in this slice.
10. **T3 cost axis (review, H1 other finding 3; pre-existing core economics, not changed).** On T3, 80 options have a negative LCOE (down to −13.8 M€/MWh) and 26 a non-finite one, so the min–max cost axis (§11) puts the 1,844 options with an LCOE between 0 and 1,000 €/MWh within 0.004 points of each other (25.075–25.079): the cost axis does not separate them, and the T3 ranking comes from carbon, time-to-power and deliverability. Cause: grid-dependent options on a site with no grid deliver almost nothing (e.g. 0.1 TWh of 13.9 TWh demand), a 100 % PPA there is settled financially (`grid-ppa` flag) and its settlement is a net revenue (PPA line −€947 m over the period on OAG1IBQ), and LCOE divides net cost by delivered energy (§9.5 "€/MWh delivered"). The same on both engines (60fe41a and this slice). Owner decision; options: LCOE over demanded energy (with unmet load priced), the NPV cost metric for such sites, no financial PPA settlement without physical delivery, or a robust axis scale (which §11's linear min–max does not allow). See Brief issue 21. **Port slice (owner decision 2026-10-18):** the PPA option is applied; negative LCOEs are gone, the axis compression is not (open point 2 of the port slice).
11. **§17 weight-change repaint (review O5).** B9 now asserts a regression bound (median of 3 ≤ 300 ms on this shared 2-core sandbox) and reports the value against the §17 target of 100 ms on the 4-core reference machine; measured medians 70–166 ms here depending on load. The 100 ms target is not demonstrated on the reference machine.
12. **Route lengths for cost (review I07b, I08b).** Straight line from the POC (or the gas main) to the nearest buildable point, the same for every option; no route factor. At the default POC (a stand-in for "nearest boundary point to the map's nearest road") the cable is 10 m, so the grid connection line is almost all the per-MW charge; replace the POC with the real one. The gas pipe rate (€0.8 m/km) is new and unverified, and it applies only when a gas main location is entered.

### Test run (this slice)

`npm run build` exit 0 (`dist/energy-tool-single-file.html` 711.4 KB, worker 182.7 KB). `npm test` exit 0, 2026-10-16, Node v22.22.0: **31/31 passed** (the 22 existing tests and 9a–9i). `npm run test:browser` exit 0: **18/18 passed** (B1–B13 existing, B14–B18 new); Chromium 141.0.7390.37, Playwright 1.56.0, axe-core 4.10.2.

**T1 run time.** `npm test`: T1 full run 10.68 s for 2000 options, T3 11.08 s (single thread; the baseline suite printed 10.03 s and 10.59–10.77 s on earlier days). Measured separately in fresh processes, three T1 runs each: this slice 11.8, 11.5, 9.4 s, with the layout stage 3.47 s in the last run, so **about 5.9 s without the layout stage**; the baseline engine on the same machine 6.7, 8.1, 6.1 s.

```
T1 run: 2000 options screened of 57028 combinations in 10.68 s (single thread, 1769 distinct dispatch runs, 10616 8760-h years)
T3 run: 2000 options in 11.08 s
PASS  1. Per-hour energy balance (supply = demand + losses + curtailment + unmet), T1 and T3, full 8760-h years  (2369 ms)
PASS  2. Grid connection equals the offered capacity on every option, never resized; import never exceeds it in "Show unmet load"  (1011 ms)
PASS  3. No option is hidden by default  (12 ms)
PASS  4. Score and rank do not change when a view filter changes  (104 ms)
PASS  5a. Determinism: same inputs + seed give identical outputs  (11373 ms)
PASS  5b. Weight change re-ranks without re-dispatch; effective weights sum to 1  (0 ms)
PASS  5c. "Show exceedance" removes unmet load on T3 and raises "Import exceeds grid capacity"  (1552 ms)

During development: 9i first failed (it counted the leader's thumbnail blocks with the main plan's; the test now counts the main plan only); an earlier 6c failure came from the longer stage list (fixed as described above). The Site tab checks B14, B15, B17 and B18 passed on their first run. B16 (GeoJSON import) first failed because the test deleted its temporary file before Chromium read it (Chromium reads a chosen file only when the page asks for its text; the page then showed "Could not read the file"); the test now deletes it afterwards. Writing B16 also found a UI defect: the Boundary section of the editor collapsed as soon as a drawn, typed or imported boundary was applied (its `open` attribute followed "site is still the area rectangle"); it is now set once when the editor opens. After looking at screenshots the tooltip was moved beside the block (under the plan when it is narrower than 480 px), the phone thumbnails were set to two columns and heat rejection was made neutral. The full suites above ran after all of these changes.

## Review: Site slice

Opus review of the three Haiku checks of the site slice (H1 claims, H2 brief, H3 browser) and the orchestrator findings O1–O5, 2026-10-17. Full record: `/home/claude/build/verification/site/OPUS-review.md` (a decision and a one-line reason for every finding, the verifier errors, the T1/T3 before/after effects and the full test output). Originals of every changed file: `/tmp/claude-0/-home-claude/a66cf92d-3564-56e6-89ee-bb0870057f0d/scratchpad/site-review-backup/original/`. No git command was run; dispatch and generator logic are unchanged.

**Decisions: 40 accepted, 12 rejected, 23 deferred** (75 findings: H1 8, H2 requirement rows not IMPLEMENTED 28, H2 deviations 28, H3 6, O1–O5 5). The deferred items are in "Deferred (recorded, not built), by §20 stage", the open points and Brief issues 18–21; the deviations are in "Deviations from the brief (recorded in the review, H2)".

**What changed.**
1. **Phone layout (O1).** Below 768 px, once a run has finished, the run progress panel, its event log, the run summary and the run flags fold into one closed "Run details" disclosure under the status line; during a run the progress panel stays unfolded. The tab bar now ends at 813 px and the active tab starts at 832 px (it was about 5,400 px). Desktop and tablet are unchanged.
2. **Site-fit wording (O2, H1 6b).** The header no longer says "no effect on any score or rank": the status changes no score (and no rank unless compliant-first is on), while site inputs size fill-residual solar and set the route costs. Test 9g checks exactly that on real runs.
3. **Site tab width (O3, H3 F1).** The option selector shrinks with an ellipsis instead of widening the page (it was 1514/1400 px at 1400 and 1114/1000 at 1000); all options stay listed and the title holds the full name.
4. **Keyboard (O4).** "Skip to site plan" is the third skip link: the plan in 3 Tab + Enter (was 36 after "Skip to results", 218 Tab only).
5. **B9 (O5, H3 F2).** Asserts the median of three repaints ≤ 300 ms on this sandbox and reports it against the §17 target of 100 ms (open point 11).
6. **Route costs (H2 I07b, I08b).** The grid cable route is the straight line from the POC to the buildable envelope (`tech.substation.cableKm`, a fixed 1.0 km, removed); options that burn gas pay a pipe from the gas main (`tech.gasConnection.pipePerKm`, new) only when a gas main is entered. Test 9j.
7. **Compliant-first (H2 D9).** Ranks "Does not fit" after fitting options too; off by default.
8. **Records (H2 D4, D5, D6, D8).** MV power factor, SMR clearance and L:W are records; the double-deck record is read; rooftop PV sizing uses the same roof-plant rule as the block.
9. **Texts.** BESS relaxed separations state the UL 9540A / fire-barrier condition (D3); stale C17/C18 generator notes (D25); the soft-exclusion record note (D2); "Leader unchanged (same choices, re-sized)" (H3 F3); route lengths and block notes in the plan table; the AC7 line, the T3 top-20 order and the "17 above 1 %" definition (H1); open point 5 on stacks (corrected); deferred items re-filed by stage (H2 D16–D19).
10. **Tests.** 8g, 9c and 9g extended; 9j new; the `constrainedT1` fixture fixed (its flood zone and gas main lay outside the site); test 7 reports build vs source time; browser B19–B21 new.

**Effect on T1 and T3.** Only the route costs moved numbers; every other review change left every number bit-identical.
- T1: leader unchanged, O1YAUJ8J 85.9634 → 85.9642; top 20 the same set in the same order; 141 ranks move by at most 2; capex −€1,633,500 on every option (cable 1.0 km → 10 m, with contingency); LCOE −0.08 to −0.25 %.
- T3: no number changes (no grid connection, no gas main); leader O1Q4ILX5 unchanged; 0 rank changes.
- T2: leader unchanged; 142 ranks move by at most 2. T4: leader unchanged; 617 ranks move by up to 11 and the top-20 set changes (the fixed cable was most of its grid line).

**Still open.** The T3 cost axis (open point 10, Brief issue 21); §17 timings on the reference machine; the screen-reader walk-through; the stage 2 gaps listed above; the judgement values of the route costs.

Weight-change repaint: median 70 ms (asserted ≤ 300 ms here; §17 target ≤ 100 ms on the 4-core reference machine).
Phone (375 px): tab bar ends at 813 px; active tab content starts at 832 px (bound 1,000 px).
```

## Slice: Port from the concept line (2026-10-18)

The owner kept this line (verified Pareto view and site engine) and asked for the other line's useful work to be ported onto it, plus one decision on PPA economics. Source: the other line's app (read-only copy in the scratchpad, `other-line/energy-strategy-tool/app/`) and its commits, read with `git show`: f1fd5c5 (tornado), e9ed8f6 + c3f81f5 (loading message, startup errors, hosted page; their `site.js` / PV-fill parts skipped), f236acc (Dispatch tab), e1161ce (bridging-battery timing fix and Dispatch fixes). The other line's site layout (231e7fc, a5be4d1) was not ported: this line's verified site engine supersedes it. Nothing was pasted unchanged: the two code bases diverged (this line has the layout stage, "fill residual" solar from the layout, site fit, route costs and a registry-driven view layer). No git command was run. The state before the slice (32/32 Node, 21/21 browser) was copied to the scratchpad (`port/baseline/app/`) and every before/after number below compares against it (`port/snap.mjs`, `port/cmp.mjs`, snapshots in `port/snaps/`). Progress log: `PORT-PROGRESS.md`.

### Item 1. Bridging battery supplies from its programme month (e1161ce)

**Problem (the other line's independent check, confirmed here).** A bridging battery (C2 = BESS + solar) was counted in time-to-power from month 0, while dispatch made it available from month 0 without a main battery and from the BESS programme month (21) with one. §9.8 sets every source's supply-available date from its planning, procurement and construction.

**Change (same rule as the other line).** `techAvailMonth(ctx, TECH.bess)` (sizing.js, now exported) is the one month: sizing records it (`sz.bridgeBess.availMonth`, and the sizing sentence says so), time-to-power counts the bridging battery only from it (evaluate.js), and dispatch uses it for all battery capacity (dispatch.js `bessAvailM`; the special case for C2 = BESS + solar is gone).

**Adapted beyond the other line (judgement call 1).** The BESS capex line and its fixed O&M were booked in year 0 for bridging-battery options (because the battery was counted from month 0) and in the availability year for every other option. They now follow the same month for every option, as every other plant is booked at its availability. The other line left them in year 0.

**Effect.** Default T1 and T3: bit-identical (C2's default subset has no BESS + solar). With BESS + solar added to C2:

| | T1 + BESS + solar | T3 + BESS + solar |
|---|---|---|
| Bridging-battery options | 413 (278 with a main battery, 135 without); month 21 | 413 |
| Leader | OOB3CL1 unchanged, 99.7048 → 98.9963 ("Grid + bess + solar only bridge → retain for grid services · retained-plant backup · Immersion · PV 3.3 MWp · BESS 4 h · PPA hybrid 100 %") | O114KGKR unchanged, 73.7173 → 73.1602 |
| Top 10 | same set, same order | same set; ranks 5 and 6 swap |
| Time-to-power | 270 options later full load (by up to 21 months), 168 later first power | 413 later (first power 413, full load 252) |
| Ranks moved | 1,745 (largest 1,262: O10WQVLG, no main battery, full load month 0 → 21) | 1,827 (largest 673) |
| Capex-timing part alone (judgement call 1) | LCOE of all 413 changes (largest −12.8 %, islanded battery-only options); leader LCOE 445.1 → 416.4 €/MWh; leader score identical with or without this part | LCOE of all 413 changes (largest −13.8 %) |

The other line reported the same 413 options, the same 270 later full loads and the same leader 99.7 → 99.0, which this line reproduces.

**Test 10** (port of the other line's test 13): on T1 + BESS + solar, every bridging-battery option has sizing month = dispatch month = the time-to-power month (supply rebuilt month by month), BESS capex in that year; for one option with and one without a main battery, no charge or discharge in any hour before the month, the battery is used after it, and the re-dispatch equals the run's annual figures. Test 12d repeats the check through the Dispatch tab inspector.

### Item 2. Owner decision 2026-10-18: no PPA income without physical delivery

**Rule.** PPA settlement counts only on PPA energy physically delivered over the grid connection to the load. The ledger's PPA line is now `delivered MWh × (strike + shaping adder)` (evaluate.js). The settlement on contracted volume that is not delivered — its wholesale value less the PPA price on it, positive = net income — is `eco.ppaHedge` ("PPA hedge (not counted)": by year, total over the evaluation period and present value at the WACC, discounted like opex). It is never written to a ledger row, so it is outside the cost ledger, LCOE, NPV, the capex/opex split and the score. Before, the ledger booked the PPA price on the whole contract and netted the undelivered volume back at the hourly grid price as income.

**What "not delivered" is.** Contracted hourly volume above the residual load in the hour, and volume above the import capacity (dispatch.js now records `ppaUndeliveredMWh` and `ppaUndeliveredSpotValue`; the hourly arrays keep the contracted volume `ppaContract`). The engine delivers PPA energy to the load only (merit order step 2, §9.4); it never routes PPA energy into a battery, so the "(or battery)" part of the decision has nothing to count in this engine (open point 4).

**Flag.** `grid-ppa` now appears wherever there is undelivered volume and explains the hedge: "PPA limited by import capacity: X MWh of contracted PPA volume over the 20-year evaluation period is not delivered physically (Y MWh limited by import capacity, Z MWh above the load in the hour). Its settlement, a net income of €A m (present value €B m), is reported as "PPA hedge (not counted)": it is not in the cost ledger, LCOE, NPV or score, because PPA settlement counts only on energy delivered over the grid connection." Minor when part is import-limited (§15 "PPA limited by import capacity"), info when it is only above-load volume. On T1 and T3 all 1,113 PPA options have import-limited volume, so the flag set is the same as before (all minor); only the text changed. The option detail shows a "PPA hedge (not counted)" row and a note; the Dispatch tab balance shows contracted against delivered volume.

**Ledger reconciliation (AC3).** Test 11b checks every T1 and T3 option (4,000): NPV = Σ discounted ledger capex + opex, LCOE × discounted delivered energy = NPV, capex share = PV capex ÷ NPV, capex lines = ledger capex, every row's totals = its lines; largest relative difference 6.4e-16 (limit 1e-3); no ledger row carries the hedge.

**Effect** (against the baseline copy; item 1 changes nothing on default T1/T3, so these are the slice's total effect on them):

| | T1 | T3 |
|---|---|---|
| Leader | O1YAUJ8J unchanged ("Grid · Diesel N+1 · DLC · PV 7.7 MWp · PPA hybrid 100 %"), 85.9642 → 92.7124; LCOE 133.50 → 153.77 €/MWh | **changed**: O1Q4ILX5 ("Islanded rented temporary generation · Gas CHP-trigen · retained-plant backup · Air · PV 5.3 MWp · BESS 2 h · PPA hybrid 100 %", now rank 2) → O826DRQ (same but "PPA hybrid 50 %", no BESS; was rank 2), 70.8916 → 97.1139; the top 10 sit within 0.0005 points of each other |
| Top 10 | 6 of 10 kept. Before: O1YAUJ8J, O1BM95B8, O1GBHO2A, OSCP27D, O14EVMZU, OMTCPB2, O1VA67D3, OV4UD3O, ORCKDL0, O1QWIDO. After: O1YAUJ8J, O1BM95B8, OSCP27D, O1GBHO2A, O14EVMZU, OMTCPB2, O6FDBB2, O1OSKM4K, OUTRYDE, O12DDOCS | 9 of 10 kept. Before: O1Q4ILX5, O826DRQ, O1FU8UKR, OMDIQOD, O1EQ82QV, ONV51PO, ON9TFWU, OH6NSUZ, ORMUQVQ, OAS6AIL. After: O826DRQ, O1Q4ILX5, O1EQ82QV, OMDIQOD, ON9TFWU, ORMUQVQ, O1FU8UKR, OH6NSUZ, OQKRTJ7, OAS6AIL |
| Ranks moved | 1,990 of 2,000 (largest 865) | 1,242 (largest 378) |
| LCOE changed | all 1,113 PPA options (largest change +42.3 % on the change relative to the new LCOE, (new − old) / new; +73.2 % on the change relative to the old LCOE, (new − old) / old; same option, OZ57BJM, 93.62 → 162.16 €/MWh) | 1,099 of 1,113 PPA options |
| Negative / non-finite LCOE | 0 / 0 → 0 / 0 | **80 / 26 → 0 / 26** (the 26 deliver no energy at all) |
| Cost axis (LCOE range) | 93.6–286.0 → 151.8–286.0 €/MWh; the 2,000 options spread over the whole 0–100 | −13.8 M to 4.61 M €/MWh → 157.3 to 4.61 M €/MWh |
| Cost-axis spread of the options with LCOE 0–1,000 €/MWh | 2,000 options over 0–100 points, before and after; P10/P50/P90 of the cost scores 34.4/54.9/71.9 → 30.6/70.4/88.5 | 1,844 options within 0.004 points (25.075–25.079) → 1,866 within 0.013 points (99.987–100.000); P10/P50/P90 of all cost scores 25.077/25.078/25.078 → 99.993/99.999/100.000 |

Why the T1 leader's LCOE rose by 15 %: its PPA line over 20 years went from €311.9 m to €574.2 m. Of the €262.3 m difference, €246.1 m is the non-commodity adder (55 €/MWh, GB) that the old rule credited as income on 4.48 TWh of PPA volume that never crossed the connection, and €16.2 m (present value €7.8 m) is the wholesale settlement now reported as the hedge. On T1, 877 PPA options have a hedge income and 236 a hedge cost. With BESS + solar included, T3's negative LCOEs go 77 → 0 (25 non-finite remain) and T1's leader is unchanged (snapshots `i2-*-bess.json`).

**What the decision did not fix on T3.** The negative LCOEs (one end of the axis) are gone, but the linear min–max cost axis (§11) is still compressed, now from the other end: grid-only options on a site with no grid deliver almost nothing (O13FMGZ0 "Grid · HVO N+1 · Immersion · BESS 2 h": 26 MWh over 20 years, from the battery's initial charge) and reach 4.6 M€/MWh. So on T3 the cost axis still separates nothing and the ranking comes from carbon, time-to-power and deliverability; the T3 leader's tornado shows every swing at 0.00 points for the same reason. See open point 2.

**Test 11a**: on T1 and T3, for every option and evaluation year, the ledger PPA line = delivered × (strike + shaping), the hedge = undelivered wholesale value × electricity escalation − undelivered × price, contract = delivered + undelivered; options without a PPA have no hedge, no PPA cost and no `grid-ppa` flag; every option with undelivered volume has the flag with the hedge explanation (severity by import limitation); no negative LCOE on T1 or T3. It also rebuilds the old rule from the same dispatch (PPA price on the whole contract, undelivered volume back at the hourly grid price including the adder): 80 negative LCOEs on T3, matching the baseline run exactly.

### Item 3. Dispatch tab (f236acc + e1161ce)

**Engine.** `option.js evaluateOption(ctx, v, { recordYears })` runs one option through the run's own per-option chain: sizing → blocks → layout (`runLayout`, one block set; test 9d shows it equals the run's batch) → "fill residual" solar from the layout → site fit → dispatch (with hourly records) → time-to-power → economics → carbon → resilience → flags → deliverability. To keep the run and this function identical, the run's flags assembly and unmet summary moved into `allOptionFlags` and `unmetSummary` (evaluate.js), which run.js now calls (outputs byte-identical). `buildContext(inputs, { site })` accepts the run's site model so a re-run does not rasterise the site again. `inspect.js inspectOption` (adapted from the other line) re-runs the option with every model year recorded and returns the monthly phasing series (grid offered, firm plant per plant and source, battery bridging, supply, design load, gap), per-year balances from the hourly arrays (now also solar, wind, contracted PPA and per-plant output), the engine's annual figures, week helpers (`weekRange`, `weekPresets`; week 52 is 8 days, from e1161ce) and `compareWithRun`, the check against the ranking's figures.

**UI.** `DispatchView.jsx` + `StackChart.jsx`: option selector (every option in rank order, as on the Site tab; the leader unless another is chosen, also from "Show this option's phasing and hourly dispatch" in the option detail; the choice survives tab switches); time-to-power facts; the monthly phasing chart (§12.6) with the power-gap strip and markers (full grid connection, first power, full load, battery available); year buttons, a week box that keeps typed text, previous/next and presets (peak demand, most unmet, most curtailment, least renewables); the hourly chart with the demand line and unmet strip; the battery charge level; the annual energy balance with the engine's hourly error; the check against the ranking ("match" or the differing figures); tables for the timeline and the week. Charts are keyboard applications (arrows, Shift for faster, Home, End, Escape) with an on-screen readout and a polite live region, as the Pareto chart and the site plan. The re-run happens after the tab paints, takes 0.5–0.7 s in headless Chromium here, and is cached per run and option.

**One energy-source colour system.** The chart bands use the site plan's tokens (`--src-*`), not the other line's four new tokens: solar yellow, wind the grid blue with the plan's wind hatch, battery aqua (discharge above the axis, charging below), firm plant per source as on the plan (gas red, fuel cells green, HVO-diesel orange, hydrogen violet, nuclear magenta; plants of one source share a band) with the plan's hatch, grid import blue (PPA arrives over the connection, so it sits in the grid band; readout, table and balance split it), export blue below the axis, curtailment a neutral (`--src-curtail`, not an energy source), unmet load and power gap in the app's flag colour (`--bad`) in a strip above the plot, never beside a band. The legend and readout swatches are the plan's swatches.

**Palette validation** (`scratchpad/port/palette.mjs`, dataviz validator, surfaces `--surface` #ffffff light / #1e2125 dark; every set of sources that shares a chart on T1 and T3, in stacking order, adjacent pairs and all pairs; full output `scratchpad/port/palette.txt`):
- Light: every set passes the hard gates except hydrogen + grid (2 options; blue–violet, normal-vision ΔE 10.5). Many sets sit in the 6–8 colour-blind warning band: gas–battery (red–aqua, ΔE 6.9) and gas–fuel cells (7.2).
- Dark: the normal-vision floor (15) fails for fuel cells vs battery (ΔE 11.9; e.g. 308 options with solar, battery, fuel cells and grid), gas vs solar (13.0) and hydrogen vs grid (9.8). These are non-adjacent slots of the reference palette, which by its own documentation passes all pairs only for its first three slots; with colour following the entity, any subset can share a chart.
- Decision: solar, battery and grid (present in most charts) stay plain and pass all pairs in both themes (light CVD 9.1, normal 22.9, contrast below 3:1 for yellow 2.17 and aqua 2.82, relieved by the readout and tables; dark CVD 8.4, normal 19.8, contrast ≥ 3:1). Every other source band carries its plan hatch, so the failing pairs always differ in texture; bands are separated by a 1.5 px surface gap, stack in a fixed (merit) order, and the legend, readout and tables name every source. Re-stepping the shared hexes would have changed the verified site plan and was not done (open point 5).
- Curtailment neutral: first #898781 in both themes (the other line's), which sat at ΔE 14.5 (light) / 12.6 (dark) from the battery colour it touches below the axis; changed to #6f6d68 light / #b9b7b0 dark: ≥ 15 from battery, grid and solar (light 19.3 / 17.5 / 27.6, dark 20.1 / 23.2 / 17.2) and 5.17:1 / 8.05:1 on the surface. Flag strip `--bad` 8.15:1 / 8.03:1.

**Tests 12a–12e** (port of the other line's test 12): 24 inspections (T1: leader, rank 2, every archetype, a battery option, prime + bridging plant, an option that does not fit; T3: six; T1 + BESS + solar: bridging battery with and without a main battery), half of them rebuilding the site model, equal the run in every dispatched year (every annual field and the per-plant figures), time-to-power, LCOE, NPV, PPA hedge, solar size, deliverability, site fit and flags (12a); 146 inspected years: 17 hourly sums equal the annual totals, plant hours sum to the firm output, solar + wind = renewables, the balance closes in every hour (12b); the timeline sources add up to the time-to-power supply every month and Σ gap = the time-to-power gap (12c); 52 weeks cover 8,760 hours with an 8-day week 52, presets valid, "most unmet" is the maximum, and the bridging battery is idle before its month (12d); the rendered tab: keyboard applications, plan colour tokens only, firm plant and wind with their hatch, tables, balance, check "match" (12e).

### Item 4. Tornado sensitivity tab (f1fd5c5)

**Engine.** `sensitivity.js` re-runs one option (default the leader) at the low and high value of each driver through `evaluateOption` (so the layout and "fill residual" solar follow, e.g., the IT load), and scores it against the base run's axis ranges and effective weights with this line's arithmetic (`axisScore` and `SCORE_KEYS`, now exported from scoring.js and used by `scoreOptions` itself), clipping each axis score to 0–100. The base re-run reproduces the run's score exactly (difference 0 on the T1 and T3 leaders); the run, the other options and their ranks are untouched. `tornadoSteps` yields after each re-run so the tab runs it in time slices; `tornado` drains it.

**Drivers and their §18 records** (each "indicative — unverified"): grid full-connection date −6/+24 months (the input `grid.dateUncertainty`; intermediate grid steps move with it), grid import price ×0.7/×1.5 and grid carbon ×0.8/×1.2 (the low/high of the input records `grid.priceMultiplier` and `grid.carbonMultiplier`, applied to the current value), WACC 4 %/12 % (`economics.wacc` low/high), and three new records for drivers whose input has no range: `sensitivity.gasPriceFactor` 0.7/1.3, `sensitivity.carbonPriceFactor` 0.5/1.5, `sensitivity.itLoadFactor` 0.8/1.2 (judgement). `sensitivity.topDrivers` = 12 (run setting, §13.3) limits the rows shown. Test 5d now counts 496 records.

**Carbon and gas price inputs (the other line's gap, fixed).** The engine read only the country values; `economics.carbonPrice` and `utilities.gasPrice` ("by country") were ignored, which is why the other line scaled them on the built context. context.js now uses an entered number (the gas transport adder still applies) and the country value otherwise; the Inputs panel has the two fields (empty = the country default, shown as the placeholder), and `isAtDefault` treats a "by country" record as at default only while no number is entered. The tornado drives both prices through these inputs.

**UI.** `SensitivityView.jsx`: option selector (every option, the leader by default), "Run sensitivity" (15 re-runs, 0.7–1.4 s in headless Chromium, one per tick with a progress line), the base score with the check against the ranking, a tornado chart (score at the low value L and high value H around the dashed base line; two lightness steps of one neutral, 3.24:1 and 10.25:1 light, 4.14:1 and 10.90:1 dark, with L/H text) that is a keyboard application (up/down, Home, End; readout under the chart and in a live region), and a table with every driver's record, "unverified" tag, low and high values, scores, LCOE and swing. Results are cached per run, option and weighting.

**Results.** T1 leader: IT load 21.65 points (LCOE 154.9/153.0 €/MWh), grid date 8.33, grid price 5.53, WACC, grid carbon, carbon price 0.015 (Scope 1 from genset test runs only), gas price. T3 leader: every swing 0.00 points although the LCOE moves (gas price 166–222 €/MWh): the compressed T3 cost axis (item 2).

**Tests 13a–13c** (port of the other line's test 9): base re-run = run score on the T1 and T3 leaders, rows sorted by swing, every driver once, later grid date never better time-to-power, carbon price moves LCOE only with Scope 1 emissions, IT load moves LCOE, scores in 0–100, the run unchanged, clipping on a synthetic case (13a); every driver's record exists with the label, changing `sensitivity.itLoadFactor`, `economics.wacc` or `sensitivity.topDrivers` changes the rows, and entered carbon and gas prices reach the context and a gas option's LCOE (13b); the rendered tab (13c).

### Item 5. Loading message, startup errors and hosted page (e9ed8f6 + c3f81f5, non-site parts)

- `index.html`: a loading message inside `#root` (React replaces it on mount; if scripts never run it stays and says to open the file in a web browser), an expanded `<noscript>`, and an inline startup-error handler (error and unhandledrejection) that shows the error on the page (role=alert) while the app has not started or after it stopped, and stays quiet once the app runs.
- `build.mjs`: `__HOSTED__` (false in the normal and single-file builds) and a third output, `dist/energy-tool-hosted.html`: the single file with `__HOSTED__` true, keeping only `<title>`, `<style>` and the body content (the build fails if document tags remain). The single-file build is unchanged otherwise (worker inlined, no outside reference).
- `RunProgress.jsx`: in the hosted page the run-log buttons copy JSON or CSV to the clipboard, with a select-the-text fallback if the browser refuses; the download code is dropped at build time. Adapted: the flag is written out at each use, not held in a module constant as in the other line, because esbuild did not inline the constant in this bundle (the first hosted build still carried both labels and the download code); test 14 now checks the hosted page has no download code and the single file no copy label.
- Loading text says 10 to 20 seconds (this line's run takes about 13 s in headless Chromium).
- **Test 7 changed** (it asserted one inline script): the single file now has two, the startup handler and the app; the app is the larger one. **Test 14** (port of the other line's test 11): index.html structure; single file and hosted page both have two inline scripts that parse, the worker inlined and equal to `dist/worker.js`, no `<script src>`, `<link href>`, relative or outside reference or stylesheet import; the hosted page starts with `<title>` then `<style>` and has no doctype, `<html>`, `<head>`, `<body>` or `<meta>`; copy label and fallback in the hosted page, download code only in the single file.

### Item 6. Tab bar with five tabs

Options, Trade-offs, Site, Dispatch, Sensitivity. The tab list scrolls sideways inside itself on narrow screens (no page overflow); its baseline is an inset shadow so the selected tab still covers it while the list clips its overflow; the focus ring is drawn inside the tab so the clipping cannot hide it. Keyboard: the ARIA tabs pattern with automatic activation, arrows wrapping, and Home/End (new); the selected tab is scrolled into view inside the list (without moving the page) on every change, including from a skip link or "Show this option's …". The three skip links are unchanged and still the first three Tab stops.
