### Verification findings on the port (H1, H2, H3; 2026-10-18)

Reports: `verification/port/H1-claims.md`, `H2-brief.md`, `H3-browser.md`. The decisions below answer them; where a point is deferred, the reason is given.

**Changes now recorded (H2 section 3, "not recorded" or "partly recorded").**
- *Gas driver scales the commodity price only* (T-3). The tornado's "Gas commodity price" driver (`sensitivity.gasPriceFactor`) multiplies the commodity price; the gas transport adder stays fixed, because the label names the commodity. The other line scaled the built price including the adder. Kept, as a deliberate change. Against the other line, the low value is 0.3 × adder higher and the high value 0.3 × adder lower, because the adder no longer moves with the factor.
- *Tornado option list: every option, not the top 50* (T-9). The option selector lists every option, leader by default; there is no cap. Kept.
- *In-cell swing bars dropped* (T-10). The tornado chart replaces the table's bar cells; the table keeps the low, high and swing values. Kept.
- *Inspector's bridging battery read from sizing* (B-4). `inspect.js` reads the bridging battery from `sz.bridgeBess`, the same value the run uses, instead of deriving it as max(0, supply − grid − firm). The derivation gave the same series whenever the battery is the only bridging source; the sizing read cannot drift from the run. Kept. Check: the inspector equals the run for 10 options (H1 check 6, 0 MWh difference). (Opus review: H1 check 6 used default T1 options, none with a bridging battery; the evidence for this series is tests 12c and 12d on T1 + BESS + solar, where grid + firm + battery equals the time-to-power supply in every month.)
- *Carbon driver label* (T-4). Renamed "Carbon price (Scope 1)" because the driver moves only Scope 1 emissions through the carbon price, not the market-based Scope 2 figure. Kept.
- *Loading and hosted-page wording* (L-3, D-19). The `<noscript>` browser list order and the unmet-load warning symbol ("! Unmet load") changed for wording and visibility only. Kept.
- *`__HOSTED__` guard* (L-10). The `typeof __HOSTED__` guard was removed; `build.mjs` defines `__HOSTED__` in every bundle, so the shipped builds are safe. The test bundle (`tests/view-bundle.mjs`) does not include `RunProgress.jsx`. Kept.
- *Chart role* (D-6). Recorded in judgement 10.
- *Tornado precision and rounding* (R24). Tornado scores show 4 significant figures in the detail table (the brief asks 2 in summaries, 3 in detail). Not changed; a rounding item for the stage-7 polish, see open point 9.
- *Tornado swing for LCOE* (R19). The engine computes `lcoeSwing` for every driver; the table shows LCOE at low and high only. Not shown as a swing; open point 9.
- *Tornado top-driver count* (R20). `sensitivity.topDrivers` (12) has no UI control. The note "editable 1–30" in the record refers to the code clamp (1–30), not to a control. Open point 9.
- *View selections without a §18 record* (R41). Option selectors open on the leader, and the Dispatch default year (most unmet) and week (most unmet week), are view state with no record. Open point 9; the owner decides whether they need records.

**Brief requirements left open, now recorded (H2 section 2).**
- *§12.6 bridging-period shading* (R14) and *§12.6 grid-date uncertainty band* (R15): MISSING. Deferred, not built in this slice: the grid-date band needs the grid-date range, which is deferred with the Monte Carlo and uncertainty stage (stage 5; BUILD-NOTES 190), and the shading needs the bridging months drawn on the phasing chart, which needs the representative-period layout of stage 3. Both are visible gaps for the owner; they are not hidden. (Opus review, correction: neither depends on a later stage. The grid-date range is already an input record, `grid.dateUncertainty` (−6/+24 months), which the tornado uses, and the bridging months follow from the sizing (`bridgeActive` before the full-connection month, from each bridging plant's availability month), which the inspector already uses. The phasing timeline is a §20 stage 3 item (line 196); both parts are deferred to that stage as a scoped change, not for a missing dependency.)
- *§17 initial load and bundle size* (R35): measured after the H checks; see "Fixes after the H checks" below.
- *§12.6 phasing timeline layout* (R13 "Gantt"): the monthly stacked chart is the implemented form; the brief's Gantt layout is open. Judgement, owner to confirm.

**H1 claim results.**
- *Claim 2c* (+42.3 % denominator): corrected in item 2 above; both denominators are stated.
- *Claim 5c* (battery first discharge, month 27, against time-to-power month 21): the verifier's check (`check5c`) found that the one option where the first discharge is later (O1I6WHKZ, no main battery, C3 backup) charges from month 21 and discharges only against a shortfall after firm plant and import. No shortfall occurred in months 21 to 26, so the battery was available and not needed. The time-to-power count is the availability month, which is correct. Not a timing defect. The owner may still want the time-to-power count to follow the first discharge; that is a definition choice, open point 10.
- *S2* (item-1 scores 98.9963 and 73.1602): these are the item-1-only state, before the PPA decision. The intermediate engine is not in the repository, so these scores are **not verified** in this slice. The final scores quoted in this section (T1 leader OOB3CL1 98.9977; T3 leader O114KGKR 99.2194, with BESS + solar) are the ones the tests reproduce.

### Fixes after the H checks (2026-10-18, before the Opus review)

- **§17 bundle size (R35), measured.** `npm run build` on this branch: `dist/app.js` 574,357 bytes, **184,020 bytes gzipped** (gzip -9), against the ≤ 1.5 MB gzipped JS budget: met. `dist/energy-tool-single-file.html` 809,951 bytes, 262,021 bytes gzipped (worker inlined, 190.2 KB). `dist/worker.js` 194,719 bytes.
- **§17 initial load (R35), measured.** Single file from file:// in Chromium 141 (headless, 1400 × 900, 2-core sandbox), 3 cold loads, median: DOMContentLoaded 175 ms; the app shell (header, inputs panel with 194 controls, run progress) is rendered and operable at about 0.3 s; the result tabs and ranking appear when the first run ends, 15.97 s (15.8–16.2 s; the Node suite's T1 run took 16.1 s on the same machine in the same session, 10.9 s in the builder's run). Against "initial load to interactive ≤ 3 s": **met for the inputs, not for the results**, which wait for the full 8,760-hour screening of 2,000 options; the representative-day tier (stage 4) is the remedy, as already recorded for the run time. Script: a 40-line Playwright probe, not part of the suite.
- **axe "needs review" items (H3).** The one needs-review rule on the Dispatch and Sensitivity tabs is **`color-contrast`** (axe cannot decide contrast for text over layered or semi-transparent backgrounds). B26/B27 already check every such node from computed styles: Dispatch 2,773 nodes (3,025 for an option with firm plant and a battery), Sensitivity 773 nodes, lowest ratio 4.86:1 (light), 0 below the minimum, light and dark. No other needs-review rule appears on any tab.
- **Committed single file refreshed.** `energy-strategy-tool/energy-tool-single-file.html` (the download-and-open copy) was still the site-slice build (d3ba899): no Dispatch tab, no tornado, old PPA rule. Replaced with this branch's `dist/energy-tool-single-file.html`.
- **Site tab at 375 px with a wider font (found by GitHub CI, 2026-10-18).** On GitHub's Ubuntu runner `system-ui` resolves to DejaVu Sans (Inter in this sandbox), and B14 and B20 failed: the page scrolled sideways, scrollWidth 388 px against 375 px. Cause: the site-fit separation checklist (`SiteFitPanel.jsx`, five columns, auto table layout) could not wrap its cells, so its minimum width exceeded the screen. Fix: `overflow-wrap: anywhere` on the site-fit table cells (`styles.css`), as the site editor's tables already have. Checked at 375 px with the Site tab fully open, forcing DejaVu Sans, FreeSerif and DejaVu Sans Mono: scrollWidth 375 px, no element past the edge, for all three. The other phone-width checks (B13, B22, B23, B24) passed on the runner's fonts unchanged.
- **Re-run before the Opus review:** `npm test` 44/44 (exit 0; T1 16.13 s, T3 14.25 s, machine shared with the build), `npm run test:browser` 29/29 (exit 0; B9 repaint median 112 ms ≤ 300 ms asserted; axe 0 serious/critical, light and dark).

### Judgement calls

1. Bridging-battery capex and fixed O&M follow its supply month, like every other plant (item 1; quantified above).
2. The PPA decision is applied symmetrically: the whole settlement on undelivered volume (strike payment and spot value) leaves the ledger, so a hedge loss is not counted either (236 T1 options have one).
3. The hedge is valued at the wholesale price without the non-commodity adder, which applies to energy taken from the grid, not to a financial settlement. The old rule's credit of the adder on undelivered volume (€246 m on the T1 leader) disappears with it.
4. The hedge is reported as a total over the evaluation period and its present value at the WACC (discounted like opex), with the undelivered volume split into import-limited and above-load parts.
5. `grid-ppa` is raised wherever there is undelivered volume (minor if import-limited, info otherwise) and carries the hedge explanation; on T1/T3 this changes the text only.
6. Market-based carbon and hourly CFE are unchanged by the decision (open point 4).
7. Dispatch tab: every option in the selector (no top-50 limit), the choice kept in App across tabs (the other line reset it); year and week reset when the tab remounts.
8. Firm plant of one source shares a band; PPA stays in the grid band (as the other line), split in the readout, table and balance.
9. Firm plant and wind carry their plan hatch in the charts (palette validation); solar, battery and grid stay plain. Curtailment neutral re-stepped per theme.
10. Charts are `role="application"` with a live region, as this line's Pareto chart and site plan (the other line used `role="group"`); readouts move under the chart below 480 px (StackChart) and always sit under the tornado.
11. The inspector and the tornado run on the main thread on request (the inspector after the tab paints, the tornado one re-run per tick), with small caches; not in a worker (open point 6).
12. Tornado ranges: the input's own record where it has a range, applied to the current value for multipliers and as absolute values for WACC; new `sensitivity.*` records otherwise; the grid date driver moves intermediate steps too; ties in swing are ordered by driver id.
13. Gas and carbon price fields added to the Inputs panel with the engine fix (empty = country default).
14. Tornado colours: two steps of one neutral (an ordinal low/high pair, not energy sources), with L/H text.
15. `__HOSTED__` written at each use (see item 5).
16. Tab list: inset baseline, inset focus ring, Home/End.
17. New tests numbered 10–14 after this line's 9a–9j; browser checks B22–B29.

### Open points

1. **Time-to-power counts battery power without regard to its energy** (the other line's open point, unchanged; §9.2, §9.8): a bridging battery sized to the shortfall with 24 h of energy counts like firm capacity for first power and full load, whatever its state of charge. Relatedly (pre-existing, not changed): with C3 = Remove, time-to-power stops counting the bridging battery at the full grid connection while dispatch keeps using it and the ledger keeps its capex without a removal.
2. **T3 cost axis still compressed** after the PPA decision (item 2): 1,866 ordinary options within 0.013 points because near-zero-delivery options reach 4.6 M€/MWh; the same on T1 with BESS + solar included, before and after the slice (51 islanded "BESS + solar only" options deliver 1,250–144,794 MWh in 20 years at 0.019–0.94 M€/MWh and compress the other 1,949 options into 0.046 → 0.040 points). The remaining owner options from Brief issue 21: LCOE over demanded energy with unmet load priced, the NPV cost metric for such sites, or a robust axis scale (which §11's linear min–max does not allow).
3. Grid-ppa flag membership: on T1/T3 every PPA option is import-limited, so the info-severity branch (above-load volume only) is exercised only by the unit logic, not by the reference runs.
4. **PPA beyond the ledger**: the engine never routes contracted PPA energy into a battery ("or battery" has nothing to count); market-based Scope 2 still applies certificates for the whole contract and hourly CFE still counts contracted volume up to the residual load even when it is not delivered. Both are carbon accounting, outside the decision as stated; owner decision.
5. **Palette**: in dark mode three source pairs that can share a dispatch chart fail the normal-vision floor (fuel cells–battery 11.9, gas–solar 13.0, hydrogen–grid 9.8; hydrogen–grid 10.5 in light too); identity relies on the plan hatch, gaps, position, legend, readout and tables. Re-stepping the shared palette would change the site plan.
6. **§17 main-thread long tasks**: the on-demand re-runs run on the main thread; the Dispatch tab's first re-run is one task of a few hundred milliseconds, and the tornado's longest slice was 179 ms on the phone run (the target is none above 50 ms). A worker for these re-runs is the remedy.
7. Tornado drivers not yet built (§13.3): PUE parameters, capex by technology, solar yield, PPA price, BESS cost, lead times; and breakevens, Monte Carlo, scenarios.
8. Screen-reader walk-through of the two new tabs not done (axe, keyboard and live-region text in headless Chromium only). The hosted page was checked in a host-like document in headless Chromium, not on the real host.
9. **Tornado and view-setting details not yet built** (H2 section 2, R13–R24, R41): LCOE swing per driver shown in the table (computed, not shown); tornado scores at 4 significant figures (brief: 3 in detail views); `sensitivity.topDrivers` has no control; the option selector, Dispatch default year and week carry no §18 record. The owner decides whether view defaults need records (AC15).
10. **Battery time-to-power definition** (H1 claim 5c): time-to-power counts the bridging battery from its availability month (21 here), while the first discharge can come later when there is no shortfall to cover. The verifier found no timing defect. Owner to confirm the availability month is the intended measure, or to count from the first discharge. (Opus review: confirmed with the dispatch data, see "Opus review (2026-10-18)" below; §9.8 defines supply by availability date, so the availability month is the brief's measure.)
11. **Brief §12.6 gaps** (H2 R14, R15): bridging-period shading and the grid-date band are not built; see "Verification findings" above for the reason and the stage they belong to.

### Opus review (2026-10-18)

Independent Opus review of the slice (`git diff d3ba899 644c77b`) after the three Haiku checks. Full report: `verification/port/OPUS-review.md`. Scripts and raw output: `/home/claude/review-port-scratch/` (`c1`–`c7`, `test-*.txt`, `final-*.txt`).

**Judged correct, no change.** The bridging-battery month (`techAvailMonth`, 21 on T1) is the one month in sizing, time-to-power, dispatch, the BESS capex and fixed O&M year and the inspector. The PPA rule matches the owner decision: ledger PPA line = delivered MWh × (strike + shaping); the settlement on undelivered volume (`eco.ppaHedge`) is in no ledger row, so it is outside LCOE, NPV, the capex/opex split and the score. Inspector and tornado reproduce the run; 11 distinct edge options (T3 leader, infinite LCOE, PPA with zero delivery, no battery, battery-only bridges with C3 remove/backup/prime/gridServices, islanded battery bridge, retained battery) re-run, render every year and run the tornado with no NaN, "undefined" or "Infinity" in the output or the HTML. H1 S2 is now verified (item-1-only leaders 98.9963 and 73.1602 rebuilt from the dispatch with the old PPA line; 77 negative LCOEs on T3 + BESS + solar).

**H1 claim 5c: not a defect.** O1I6WHKZ charges from month 21; in months 21–26 the peak hourly net demand (14.75–19.66 MW) stays under the 20 MW import cap, so the bridging-support rule has no shortfall to cover; the first shortfall (0.41 MW) and first discharge are in month 27; unmet load is 0 throughout. Time-to-power tests capacity against design load at peak PUE, which passes 20 MW in month 23. Both follow their definitions; open point 10 can close.

**Fixes (text and robustness only; every score, rank, LCOE, NPV, hedge, carbon, time-to-power and flag id/severity identical before and after on T1, T3 and T1 + BESS + solar):**
1. *Hedge present value sign.* The `grid-ppa` flag (`evaluate.js`) and the option detail hedge row (`OptionDetail.jsx`) printed |PV| after the total's "income"/"cost" word. 23 T1 options (28 on T1 + BESS + solar, 0 on T3) have a total and a PV of opposite sign, e.g. O12A2ZQY "a net cost of €0.171 m (present value €0.001 m)" whose PV is a net income of €1.49 k. The PV now names its own sign when it differs ("present value a net income of €0.001 m"; option detail "present value net income €1.49 k"). ## Work package 0: research the defaults (started 2026-10-22)

Branch `feature/defaults-research`. Build order confirmed by the owner on 2026-10-22 (as recorded, 0 to 10).

### Decisions

- **Label for researched defaults (owner, 2026-10-22; OPEN-QUESTIONS 36).** A default that has been researched, given its source, year, link and access date, and approved by the owner carries the label **"sourced — not independently checked"** instead of "indicative — unverified". It is not marked verified (`verified: false`) because the research was done by Claude and approved by the owner, not checked by a person responsible for the data; it becomes verified only after such a check. Values with no published source keep the label "judgement" with a reason. The owner chose this middle label over marking approved values verified, and over keeping them "indicative — unverified". In the registry: `label: 'sourced — not independently checked'`, `source`, `year`, `url`, `accessed` filled, `confidence: 'B'`, `verified: false`. Interpretation (reversible): confidence B is read here as "published source, approved by the owner"; the brief's wording for B ("published source verified") is stricter, so the record keeps `verified: false`. Test 5d will change to accept the two labels and to require a filled source record wherever the new label is used.

### Batch 1 applied and tested (2026-10-22)

Approved by the owner on 2026-10-22, each with its source record and the label "sourced — not independently checked" (confidence B, verified false): EU ETS carbon price €88.30/t (EU-27, Norway, Iceland; new record `country.carbonPrice.euEts`), UK ETS €75/t (`country.carbonPrice.ukEts`), renewable hydrogen €246/MWh_th, range 210–300 (`fuel.hydrogenPrice`), ECB reference rates of 22 Oct 2026 (`project.fx`). Switzerland keeps €85/t, unresearched. The country table takes its carbon price from the two scheme records.

UI: the field tag shows "sourced" (compact) or the full label, with the source, value date, link, access date and approval date on hover; the header strip counts both labels; the run log's warning counts both. Test 5d accepts either label and requires a complete source record (source, year, https link, access and approval dates) wherever the new label is used.

Test changes caused by the new defaults (not by code): with UK ETS at €75/t the T1 Balanced leader (O1YAUJ8J, unchanged) is now also the pick of the Lowest cost and Lowest carbon presets. Test 8b no longer requires Balanced and Lowest cost to differ (the distinct-picks check stays); browser check B9 times the "Speed to market" preset instead of "Lowest cost". Picks: Balanced, Lowest cost and Lowest carbon → O1YAUJ8J; Speed to market → OLN9RSS; Lowest risk → OCRJR70.

Test run: `npm test` 44/44; `npm run test:browser` 29/29 (axe 0 serious or critical, light and dark). T1 full run 13.8 s single thread (2,000 options).

T1 against the "before" export of 2026-10-21 (`docs/user-guide/data/`, commit 441635e), build plan step 4: the same 2,000 options and the same top 10 in the same order. Every LCOE rises (median +4.0 %, largest +8.5 % for islanded gas engines) because the UK carbon price on Scope 1 combustion went from €50 to €75/t; the leader's LCOE moves only 0.02 % (€153.77 → €153.80/MWh, diesel test runs only), and its score rises from 92.71 to 92.92 because the other options moved further (scores are relative to the run). Hydrogen and exchange rates do not change T1 (no hydrogen plant outside archetype A10b; rates are display only).

### Ramp at energisation 25 % (2026-10-22)

The owner's judgement (OPEN-QUESTIONS 51, from the notes on the guide, page 48): `load.rampStart` 0.25 (was 0.5), range 0.1–0.5, basis judgement. T1 against the batch 1 run: the same 2,000 options; the leader (O1YAUJ8J) and ranks 1–9 unchanged, rank 10 changes; LCOE median +0.6 % (−0.1 % to +1.3 %), because less energy is delivered while buildings ramp up and the fixed costs are spread over less; the leader's power gap falls from 24.9 to 15.4 MW-months (less early demand above the 20 MW grid), LCOE €153.80 → €154.07/MWh, location-based carbon 113.2 → 112.7 g/kWh. `npm test` 44/44.

Browser checks with the 25 % ramp: `npm run test:browser` 29/29.

## Slice: Site tab as Beta (work package 2, part; 2026-10-22)

Branch `feature/site-tab-beta` (from `feature/defaults-research`). Built while the owner was away, who asked for progress on sections their notes on the guide did not touch. Decided in the walkthrough of 2026-10-21 (BRIEF §2 principle 3, §10, §11, §12.12, AC 27); no open question is answered here.

What changed:
- **General settings** (topbar button "General settings"): one switch, "Site tab (Beta)", off by default, with the mock-up's wording (`docs/user-guide/mock/m10-settings.html`). Registry record `view.siteTabBeta` (judgement, indicative — unverified). Nothing else from the mock-up (theme, run-log option, "About this copy") is built: the brief calls those proposals to confirm with the owner.
- **Off (default):** no Site tab and no "Skip to site plan" link; no site-fit item in the header strip; no site-fit view filters, land-take filter, land-take or site-fit columns in the options table; no site-fit shape, legend, tooltip text or column on the Trade-offs tab (all markers are circles); no site-fit section or site-fit flags in the option detail; site-fit flags are not counted in the table's flag counts nor offered as flag filters; the run summary says site fit is a Beta feature and the C17/C18 notes carry no "N options do not fit" count; the land-take ceiling input and the site-fit penalty toggle are hidden.
- **On:** everything as before, with "Beta" on the tab, the header item, the table column, the chart legend, the option-detail heading and the filters.
- **Turning it off** returns from the Site tab to Options, clears site-fit view filters, and switches the site-fit penalty off (it belongs to the Beta settings only, BRIEF §9.9).
- **Engine:** "Rank requirement-compliant options first" uses the resilience status only (`scoring.js isRequirementCompliant`), as BRIEF §11 decided. Dominance marks (§7.2 step 6) no longer use site fit either.

Interpretation (reversible): dominance without site fit. BRIEF §7.2 step 6 still says "no better site-fit or resilience status", but site fit is now kept out of the main flow, and a mark that depended on a hidden status would be unexplained with the tab off. Making dominance depend on the switch would make the dominated count and "Hide dominated options" change with a view setting. Dominance therefore uses resilience only, always. The brief's §7.2 wording should follow if the owner agrees.

Not changed (waits for open questions): the site inputs other than the area stay in the inputs panel, because the layout still runs in every run and sizes "fill residual" ground solar and the route costs (OPEN-QUESTIONS 25; the owner's note on guide page 30 asks for a user-specified solar area). Hiding them would hide values that move results.

Tests: test 8g now checks that an option that does not fit is not demoted (it checked the opposite before; review D9); test 9f renders the run summary with the tab on for AC 7, and checks that with it off there is no "do not fit" note; test 8f covers `view.siteTabBeta`; new test 15 (AC 27, `tests/beta-tests.mjs`) checks on T2 that scores, ranks (compliant first off and on) and dominance marks are identical with site fit removed, that site-fit flags are hidden and never permitting flags, and that the rendered views carry no site fit with the tab off. Browser: the Site tab checks (B14–B18, B20, B21, B25) turn the switch on first, as a user would; new check B30 covers the default, the Beta label, an identical ranking off → on → off with no re-run, and the return to Options.

Test run (Site tab as Beta, 2026-10-22): `npm test` 45/45; `npm run test:browser` 30/30 (29 in the full run, then B30 alone after making its header-strip text check case-insensitive: the labels are styled in capitals). axe: 0 serious or critical, light and dark, including the Site tab with the switch on. Browser checks B28 and B29 now expect four tabs by default; B21 and B25 turn the switch on and put the focus starting point back at the top of the page. The guide's screenshots of the header strip and the options table predate the "General settings" button and the hidden site-fit columns; `tools/screenshots/shot_real.py` now turns the Site tab on before its Site shots and captures the settings, and the figures should be re-captured when the owner asks for the books to be rebuilt.

## Slice: power adequacy first (work package 3b, part 1; 2026-10-23)

*T1 figures in the slice and package notes below (adequate counts, leaders, scores, removals) are as measured when each slice was built. Later slices, batch 2 and the verification fixes changed them; they are kept as a record, not as current values. The current T1 figures are in "Verification fixes after Haiku checks H1–H3" at the end of these notes (Haiku check H1, L-5).*

Branch `feature/adequacy-first` (from `feature/solar-areas`). The owner's decisions of 2026-10-22, OPEN-QUESTIONS 37–41 (38: power adequacy first, a deliberate exception to principle 5). Built when the owner asked to go back to the app.

What changed:
- **Adequacy status** (`src/engine/adequacy.js`, `adequacyOf`): an option is adequate only with no unmet load in any dispatched hour, no import above the grid capacity (exceedance mode), and no time-to-power power gap against the design load (full contracted IT at design-day PUE plus ancillary) in any month. No tolerance (OPEN-QUESTIONS 40). Every option carries `adequacy` with unmet MWh, hours, peak MW, the longest event, exceedance MWh and hours, and the power gap.
- **Longest unmet event** (`dispatch.js`): the longest unbroken run of hours with unmet load in each dispatched year (`unmetLongestH`). A run that crosses a year boundary counts as two events, because each year is dispatched separately.
- **Ranking** (`scoring.js`): adequate options rank above every option that is not. Within each group the order is as before: weighted score, with "rank requirement-compliant options first" applied inside each group when it is on. Scores never change. **Dominance**: an option is only dominated by one with at least equal adequacy (as with resilience and site fit).
- **Flags**: `adequacy-short` (major, category Adequacy) on every option that is not adequate, with the numbers; run flag `adequacy-count` (info), or `adequacy-none` (major) when no option is adequate. "Not adequate (power)" is offered in the show-only / hide flag filters.
- **Grid shortfall analysis** (`shortfallAnalysis`, in the run output as `run.shortfall`): for each cooling type the run generates, the phased design load against the offered grid capacity month by month: the temporary shortfall (before the full grid date) and the permanent shortfall (from it on), each with peak MW, its month, the number of months and MW-months; and the IT load the full grid supports (full grid MW ÷ (design-day PUE + ancillary)). In a worker-pool run the adequacy run flag and the shortfall are recomputed over the merged set.
- **Trade-off view**: when the highest scorer is not rank 1 because it is not adequate, it is marked separately with the reason "adequacy" ("ranked #N, below the pick, because it does not serve its load in every hour").
- **Screens**: options table: Adequacy column with the reasons, a note with the counts, and a red divider row where the not-adequate options begin (rank order only); option detail: a Power adequacy section above the scores; header strip: adequate and not-adequate counts; run summary: "Grid shortfall against the design load" table. The Dispatch tab inspector returns the adequacy too.

Interpretations (reversible, to confirm with the owner):
- Score order within each adequacy group, and "rank requirement-compliant options first" applied inside each group (rather than resilience before adequacy).
- "Not adequate" counts exceedance (import above the grid capacity in exceedance mode) and the monthly power gap as well as unmet load, so the rule gives the same answer in both grid-handling modes and matches OPEN-QUESTIONS 39 (firm supply against the design load). **Confirmed by the owner 2026-10-23 (OPEN-QUESTIONS 55)** for the power gap.
- The shortfall analysis is shown per cooling type, because the design-day PUE, and so the design load, depends on cooling.

T1 effect (2,000 screened options): 953 adequate, 1,047 not. The leader changes from OG27VF9 (Grid · Diesel N+1 · DLC · PV 2.0 MWp · PPA hybrid 100 %, score 92.74, not adequate: it relies on the grid and has unmet load and a power gap before the full grid connection in 2029-07) to OPIAR4Q (Grid + rented temporary generation bridge → remove · Diesel N+1 · DLC · PV 1.5 MWp, score 89.83, adequate). The highest scorers (92.7) leave 300 to 1,000 MWh unmet over 500 to 1,000 hours; the highest of them 608 MWh over 830 h, longest event 581 h, with a 15 MW-month power gap. Shortfall: air cooling 137 MW design load, temporary peak 12 MW (2029-06), permanent 16.7 MW from 2031-12, full grid (120 MW) supports 87.8 MW IT; DLC 113 MW, temporary only (6.5 MW), 106 MW IT; evaporative 129 MW, permanent 9.0 MW, 93 MW IT; immersion 111 MW, temporary only (6.0 MW), 108 MW IT.

Tests: 8b and 8g now expect the pick to be the highest score among adequate options, and the top scorer marked with the reason "adequacy" when it is not adequate; 6c checks the pool run's adequacy statuses, adequacy run flag and shortfall against the single-thread run; 12a checks the inspector's adequacy equals the run's; new test 17 (`tests/adequacy-tests.mjs`): the definition and flag on every T1 and T3 option, adequate before not adequate, score order within each group, scores unchanged when rescored without adequacy, dominance, nothing hidden by default, the run flag, the shortfall recomputed by hand, and the longest event against the hourly records.

Header strip: the power adequacy count sits after the leader, so the leader stays the second item (browser check B9 reads it by position).

Test run (power adequacy first, 2026-10-23): `npm test` 47/47; `npm run build`; `npm run test:browser` 30/30, axe 0 serious or critical, light and dark.

## Slice: ordering from day one and the permitting allowance (work package 3b, part 2; 2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/adequacy-first`. The owner's decisions OPEN-QUESTIONS 44 and 45 (2026-10-22) and 56 (2026-10-23).

What changed:
- **Programme months** (`sizing.js` `programmeMonths`, `techAvailMonth`): plant is ordered from the analysis start, in parallel with construction of the first phase. Supply available month = permitting allowance + procurement + construction. Before, planning, procurement and construction were added one after another for every technology.
- **Permitting allowance** (new record `requirements.permitAllowanceM`, 6 months, judgement, the owner's decision): only for plant held by significant environmental permitting (new `src/engine/permitting.js`, one rule shared with the permitting flags):
  - IED: every combustion plant of an option whose aggregated combustion thermal input (prime, bridging and backup units, the sum the `permit-ied` flag uses) is at or above `requirements.iedMWth` (50 MWth);
  - Seveso: liquid-fuel plant when the diesel/HVO store is at or above `requirements.sevesoFuelT`, and hydrogen plant when the hydrogen store is at or above `requirements.sevesoH2t`.
- **Own consent** (OPEN-QUESTIONS 56): onshore wind, ground-mount solar and SMRs (`ownConsent` in the technology library) keep their planning and consent months. The planning-month records of the other twelve technologies are removed (registry 491 → 480 records, with the new allowance record).
- **One rule everywhere:** sizing (prime and bridging plant), dispatch (solar and wind availability, battery), and the capex years (`evaluate.js`, renewables now read `techAvailMonth`). The permitting flags now call the same `permittingTriggers`.
- **Sizing sentences:** the prime and bridging sentences no longer state availability; a new sentence per plant says the month and how it was reached, e.g. "Gas reciprocating engines (bridging) available from month 21: 6 permitting allowance (IED) + 9 procurement + 6 construction months."

Interpretations (reversible):
- The IED test is the option's aggregated thermal input, as the `permit-ied` flag already counts it, so backup gensets count. On a data centre campus they alone are usually above 50 MWth, so all combustion plant, rented temporary generation included, is normally held for 6 months. **Confirmed by the owner 2026-10-23 (OPEN-QUESTIONS 57).**
- Fuel cells are not combustion plant, so they are never held by the IED. Batteries and rooftop solar are never held.
- Backup gensets have no availability month in the engine (as before), so the allowance does not change them.
- "Day one" is the analysis start.

Months now (held / not held): rented generation 10 / 4; fuel cells 9; rooftop solar 10; HVO-diesel prime 18 / 12; BESS 15; gas engines 21 / 15; gas turbines 24 / 18 (the owner's example); CHP 27 / 21; hydrogen 33 / 27; ground solar 21, wind 54, SMR 120 and not before 2035 (unchanged).

T1 effect: in the 2,000 screened options, gas-engine bridges are available from month 21 (was 24), CHP from 27 (was 33), fuel cells from 9 (was 15; gas still arrives at month 18), rented generation from 10 (was 6). 953 options are still adequate and the leader is unchanged (OPIAR4Q, score 90.10, was 89.83; the scores move with the shorter lead times of the other options).

Test run (ordering from day one, 2026-10-23): `npm test` 48/48 (new test 18, `tests/programme-tests.mjs`: records, the rule for every technology, the owner's 24-month gas turbine example, every T1 and T3 plant's month and regimes against the permitting flags, allowance 0 moves only held plant); `npm run build`; `npm run test:browser` 30/30.

## Slice: firm generation sized two ways (work package 3b, part 3; 2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/adequacy-first`. The owner's decision OPEN-QUESTIONS 58 (2026-10-23): size permanent on-site generation both ways, as two choices.

What changed:
- **New choice C4s "Firm generation sizing"** (after C4): Average load (baseload), as before; Permanent shortfall (top-up). Default open, both. Not applicable when C4 = None (rule d). Record `choices.C4s` (judgement).
- **Top-up sizing** (`sizing.js`): S = the largest (design load − grid capacity) in any month from the full grid date to the end of the timeline (islanded: the design load); n = ⌈S ÷ unit size⌉; units = n + ⌈n ÷ block size⌉ (N+1 per block, as bridging plant). Sentence "… (firm top-up): permanent shortfall … = N units (X MW)". The programme sentence names "firm top-up".
- **Rule (g)** (`generator.js`): the top-up sizing is not generated for a cooling type with no permanent shortfall, unless the option is islanded; it would repeat C4 = None. The cooling types with a permanent shortfall come from `adequacy.js` `permanentShortfallCoolings(inputs)` (a context without the site model, about 20 ms), so the inputs panel's projected count applies the rule too.
- **Names:** a top-up plant is named "… top-up" (e.g. "Gas CHP-trigen top-up"). Baseload names are unchanged.
- **Archetypes** keep the baseload sizing (the first open value), so A4, A8, A10a and A10b are unchanged.

Interpretations (reversible): rule (g) rather than duplicates of C4 = None; the top-up redundancy is N+1 per block, as for bridging plant; the top-up covers the whole permanent shortfall on its own, even where retained bridging plant ("Retain as prime") also covers it.

T1 effect: 53,284 combinations (rule g removes 10,080); the 2,000-option sample is drawn from a different space, so most options are new. 929 adequate. Leader O1AN3BKP (Grid + rented temporary generation bridge → retain for grid services · retained-plant backup · DLC · PV 2.5 MWp · PPA hybrid 50 %, score 91.53). 528 top-up options (289 adequate, best rank 33: a CHP top-up of 18 MW on evaporative cooling, LCOE €181/MWh, capex €56 m, 152 g/kWh) against 817 baseload options (418 adequate, best rank 136: CHP of 90 MW, LCOE €184/MWh, capex €270 m, 249 g/kWh).

Test 3: the T3 "grid-dependent" check now excludes "wait" options with a top-up plant, which is sized to close the shortfall; they are counted separately.

Test run (firm generation sized two ways, 2026-10-23): `npm test` 49/49 (new test 19, `tests/topup-tests.mjs`); `npm run build`; `npm run test:browser` 30/30 (B9 now uses the first preset that moves the pick: "Lowest carbon" on this sample).

## Slice: bridging plant split when the grid arrives (work package 3b, part 4; 2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/adequacy-first`. The owner's decision OPEN-QUESTIONS 59 (2026-10-23), the owner's alternative (b) of OPEN-QUESTIONS 42. Built while the owner asked for work to continue without their input.

What changed:
- **New C3 value "Keep shortfall share as prime, rest as backup"** (`split`), open by default.
- **Sizing:** SP = the largest (design load − grid capacity) from the full grid date to the end of the timeline; kept units = ⌈SP ÷ unit size⌉ + ⌈n ÷ block size⌉ (N+1 per block). If the bridging plant is smaller than its kept share, it is enlarged to it (the plant is bought for both jobs). `bridge.primeUnits`, `bridge.primeMW`.
- **Running plant:** `sizing.js` `bridgeMWAt`: the whole plant before the full grid date, the kept share after. The firm supply by month, time-to-power and the inspector's timeline read it.
- **Dispatch:** a second plant record `bridgeKept` (the kept share, mode 1 from the full grid date) next to the bridge record (whole plant, before the grid date), so run hours and per-plant energy show the two periods. The Dispatch tab names it "bridging share kept as prime".
- **Economics and resilience:** as "Retain as backup": the plant is kept (capex not removed, fixed O&M and rental continue) and counts as retained bridging plant in the resilience check.
- **Rule (h):** the split is not generated with C2 = BESS + solar only (nothing to keep running), or for a cooling type with no permanent shortfall (it would repeat "Retain as backup").

Interpretations (reversible): rented bridging plant can also be split (the kept share keeps being rented, as "Retain as backup" keeps renting today); the bridging plant is enlarged to its kept share when that is larger.

T1 effect: 59,764 combinations (rule h removes 3,888, rule g 12,672). 931 adequate. 127 split options in the sample (64 adequate, best rank 23: rented bridge 7 × 2 MW, 6 kept for the 9.0 MW permanent shortfall of evaporative cooling, LCOE €167.5/MWh). Leader: Grid + rented temporary generation bridge → retain for grid services · Diesel N+1 · Immersion · PV 3.3 MWp · PPA hybrid 50 % (score 91.26).

## Slice: permitting outlook (work package 3b, part 5; 2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/adequacy-first`. The owner's decision OPEN-QUESTIONS 46 (2026-10-22). Built while the owner asked for work to continue without their input; the outlook values are judgement for the owner to review.

What changed:
- **Records** `permitting.outlook.<id>` (judgement, indicative — unverified, each with its reason): gas engines, gas turbines, CHP, rented generation and hydrogen "uncertain"; HVO-diesel prime "unlikely"; standby gensets "likely". `PERMIT_OUTLOOK_COUNTRY` holds country overrides and is empty: country differences are a research item (work package 0).
- **Outlook items** (`permitting.js` `permitOutlookItem`, computed in sizing as `sz.permitOutlook`): every plant held by the IED or Seveso (prime, bridging, and the standby gensets) with its regimes, outlook, reason, ready month, the month it is first needed and the float. Needed: bridging plant at the first month of shortfall (islanded: of load); a baseload prime and the standby gensets at the first month of load; a top-up plant at the first month of permanent shortfall from the full grid date.
- **Standby gensets** now have a ready month in the outlook only: the same programme rule (allowance if held + procurement + construction). The engine still gives backup no availability month elsewhere (resilience and dispatch unchanged).
- **Flag** `permit-outlook` (Permitting): minor when an item is "unlikely" or late, info otherwise. Not marked as a permitting flag for deliverability, so it changes no score. Offered in the flag filters.
- **Option detail:** a "Permitting outlook" table (plant, outlook with reason, ready / needed with float or months late).

Interpretations (reversible): the outlook does not change scores or adequacy; the same outlook in every country until researched; standby gensets are included because they are held by the same IED test.

Finding for the owner: in T1 the standby gensets are ready at month 24 (6 permitting + 12 procurement + 6 construction) and the first building is energised at month 18, so they are 6 months late in every option. CHP is ready at month 27 against a first need at 18 (baseload) or 59–62 (top-up). Lead-time defaults are unresearched.

## Slice: IT the grid supports by period, grid-matched phasing (work package 3b, part 6; 2026-10-23)

OPEN-QUESTIONS 38, step 3 ("report the IT load the grid can support and a grid-matched phasing reference option").

- `shortfallAnalysis` adds, per cooling type, `periods` (each run of months at one grid capacity, with the IT it carries at the design-day PUE plus ancillary load) and `gridMatched` (each phase energised no earlier than planned and only once the grid alone carries the cumulative IT at design load; month and delay, or "not within the timeline").
- The run summary's shortfall section shows both tables.
- Interpretation (reversible): the grid-matched phasing is a **reference shown in the run summary, not a ranked option**, because it serves a later and possibly smaller load than every option, so its scores would not be comparable. It uses each phase's full design load (no ramp), which is conservative.

T1: grid 20 MW to June 2029, 120 MW from July 2029. Air: 14.6 / 87.8 MW IT; phase 1 waits 12 months, phases 2 and 3 on time, phase 4 never. DLC: 17.7 / 106.1 MW IT; phase 1 waits 12 months, the rest on time.

Test 22 (`tests/gridref-tests.mjs`): periods cover the timeline at one capacity each; IT supported; grid-matched months by hand.

## Slice: backup per building or pooled (work package 3b, part 7; 2026-10-23)

The owner's decisions OPEN-QUESTIONS 47 and 48 (2026-10-22). Built while the owner asked for work to continue without their input.

What changed:
- **New choice C5a "Backup arrangement"** (after C5): Per building / Pooled across the campus; default open, both; n/a when C5 = None or Retained bridging plant (rule d).
- **Sizing** (`sizing.js`; the genset block now runs after the bridging plant is sized, so it knows the retired plant):
  - Per building: each phase is a building; its critical load = backed share × its IT × (1 + design cooling fraction); its own N and redundancy (C6) on blocks of 10. Bridging plant retired into backup (C1 = Bridge with C3 = Retain as backup, Retain for grid services, or the share not kept as prime under the split) backs up whole buildings in phase order when its units meet the building's critical load at the required topology; gensets back up the rest. `backup.unitsByPhase`, `backup.coveredByRetired`.
  - Pooled: the retired plant's units less one per block of 10 count against the campus critical load; the gensets are sized to the rest, with campus-wide redundancy (as before when there is no retired plant).
  - Fuel storage is sized for the critical load the gensets back up (`backup.genCriticalMW`).
- **Capex:** per building, the genset capex is booked by each building's units; pooled by IT share, as before.
- **Deliverability:** pooled options carry "Client-perceived risk: backup pooled across the campus", the minor permitting-flag penalty by default; record `deliverability.pooledBackupRisk` (minor / major, judgement) and an input to raise it.
- **Names:** pooled backup is named "… N+1 pooled".

Interpretations (reversible): each phase is one building; the retired plant must cover a whole building's critical load at the required topology or it backs up none of it (never shared with gensets in a building); pooled counts the retired plant with its redundant units removed; per building is the first value, so archetypes use it; genset fixed O&M stays by IT share.

T1 effect: 102,676 combinations (C5a doubles the genset options). Per building, each 25 MW building needs 10 + 1 diesel units with DLC (44 in all, against 43 pooled) and 12 + 2 with air cooling (56 against 52); before this change every option was pooled. The T1 bridging plant (a few engines) never backs up a whole building, so per building it does not reduce the gensets; with no grid before the full connection (test 23) it backs up building 1. 940 options adequate; the leader is Grid + rented temporary generation bridge → retain as backup · retained-plant backup · Immersion · PV 2.0 MWp · BESS 2 h · PPA hybrid 50 % (90.62).

Test runs (3b parts 4–7, 2026-10-23): part 4 `npm test` 50/50, browser 30/30; parts 5–6 `npm test` 52/52, browser 30/30; part 7 `npm test` 53/53 (new tests 20–23: `split-tests.mjs`, `outlook-tests.mjs`, `gridref-tests.mjs`, `backup-tests.mjs`), `npm run build`, browser 30/30.

## Slice: battery sized to the permanent shortfall, counted as firm supply (work package 3b, part 8; 2026-10-23)

The owner's decision OPEN-QUESTIONS 60 (2026-10-23): a battery whose MW covers the shortfall and whose energy covers the design day's shortfall hours, with a daily recharge check, counts towards adequacy.

What changed:
- **New C12 value "Sized to the permanent shortfall"** (`shortfall`), in the default subset. Rule (i): not generated when islanded, for a cooling type with no permanent shortfall, or with a role other than peak-shave.
- **Design day** (`context.js` `designDayPUE`): the year's hottest day of the cooling type's temperature basis, shifted so its hottest hour is at the design temperature; hourly PUE from the same cooling model, so the peak hour equals the design load.
- **Sizing** (`sizing.js`): for each month from the full grid date, shortfall(h) = installed IT × (PUE(h) + ancillary) − grid capacity; MW = the largest; usable energy = the largest daily sum; stored MWh = usable ÷ √RTE. Recharge check per month: Σ min(MW, headroom(h)) × RTE ≥ the day's shortfall energy. `sz.shortfallBess`.
- **Firm supply:** when the check passes, the battery's MW counts in the time-to-power supply from the full grid date (once available), so it closes the power gap and can make an option adequate; the inspector's timeline shows it with the batteries. When it fails, the battery is built and dispatched but not counted, with the minor flag `bess-recharge`.
- **Dispatch:** role `shortfall`: discharges only to cover the grid shortfall, no arbitrage, recharges from grid headroom in any hour.

Interpretations (reversible): the design day is the hottest day shifted to the design temperature; the battery covers the permanent shortfall only (bridging covers the temporary one); recharge within the same day, from the grid only.

T1 effect: 120,916 combinations (rule i removes 15,984). The T1 permanent shortfall lasts most of the design day (air: 16.7 MW peak, 291 MWh), so no T1 battery recharges and none counts. Test 24 shows the case where it does: a 125 MW grid with evaporative cooling needs 4.0 MW / 21.9 MWh, which closes the permanent gap (power gap 250 → 31 MW-months, the rest before the full grid date).

Test run (3b part 8, 2026-10-23): `npm test` 54/54 (new test 24, `tests/bess-shortfall-tests.mjs`); `npm run build`; `npm run test:browser` 30/30.

## Work package 4: flexibility and battery logic (2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/flex-batteries` (from `feature/adequacy-first`). Decisions: walkthrough of 2026-10-21 (BRIEF §5.3, §5.5), OPEN-QUESTIONS 33 (C12 keeps the economic battery's sizes; "sized to the trigger hours" added when flexibility is on), 34 (flexibility acts in trigger hours and against shortfalls, within the same limits) and 61 (IT shed against a shortfall is not adequate). AC 19 and AC 20.

What changed:
- **Gensets (AC 19):** `requirements.testRunHours` 12 → 6 h a year per unit; `requirements.testRunLoad` 75 % (already separate, now on screen); new `requirements.gensetPrimaryHours` (default 0). Above 0 the gensets' N units are a dispatch plant `backupPrime`, must-run only in the year's most expensive grid-price hours (`dispatch.js` `topPriceHours`), after the other plant; fuel, carbon and run hours are counted and costed. At 0 there is no plant record, so nothing changes.
- **Resilience reserve battery:** full IT × `requirements.upsMin` (5 min) MWh; never dispatched (not in `storage`); battery energy costed per building at `tech.upsReserve.capexPerKWh` €500/kWh (judgement), 1 % fixed O&M, replaced every 10 years. Same for every option, so it moves every LCOE equally.
- **Load shapes** (`context.js` `itShape`): `load.shape` flat / daily (default; unchanged results, swing `load.dailySwing` blank = by workload) / AI training (`aiTrainUtil` 90 %, dip to 55 % for 1 h every 6 h) / AI inference (`aiInfUtil` 70 %, trough 60 % of the 15:00 peak, weekends 90 %). AI values from the mock-up, judgement, to research. Each shape averages to its stated utilisation; sizing stays at 100 % of IT.
- **Flexibility with triggers** (`triggers.js`, `dispatch.js`): trigger = grid price ≥ €250/MWh (default), grid carbon ≥ 400 g/kWh, or temperature ≥ 28 °C (weather and grid stress, a proxy); events of consecutive trigger hours, at most `flexEventH` 4 h, then `flexRecoveryH` 12 h recovery. In an event hour (step 1b) the flexible share (10 % of IT) is taken off: battery response first (on by default), then IT curtailment within the hours a year. Step 6 (shortfall) uses what the trigger step left this hour and the same hours budget. Curtailment costs `flexCostPerMWh` €500/MWh (new ledger line `flex`). Notice (`flexNoticeH`) is recorded, not modelled. Shed against a shortfall is recorded as `flexShortMWh` and makes an option not adequate (reason "shed").
- **Trigger analysis:** the run summary shows trigger hours in the profile, events, event hours and hours within the limit (`run.flexAnalysis`).
- **Economic battery sized to the trigger hours** (C12 `trigger`, rule j: only with C16 = On and trigger hours): MW = flexible share × full IT; MWh = MW × longest trigger event ÷ √RTE. Hold fixed (`load.flexBatteryFixed`): threshold (default), hours (the trigger level is set by binary search so the event hours reach `flexBatteryHours`; it applies to the whole run), size (`flexBatteryMW`/`MWh` entered).
- **Records and inputs:** all new values are judgement, indicative — unverified, on screen in the load and requirements sections.

Interpretations (reversible): primary-power hours are the most expensive grid-price hours; the reserve battery costs battery energy only (UPS electronics are fit-out); weather and grid stress is a temperature proxy; notice is not modelled; "hours covered" sets the trigger level for the whole run; the trigger battery's role in other hours is C12r (peak-shave by default).

Finding: the synthetic price profiles peak near €215/MWh (UK, adder included), so the default €250/MWh trigger never occurs; trigger flexibility needs the real price data of work package 1. With a €190/MWh trigger, T1 has 343 trigger hours, 135 events and 248 event hours a year; a 10 MW / 42.6 MWh trigger battery serves about 1,900 MWh and leaves about 20 MWh to curtail, against about 750 MWh curtailed without it.

Test run (work package 4, 2026-10-23): `npm test` 57/57 (new tests 25–27, `tests/package4-tests.mjs`); `npm run build`; `npm run test:browser` 30/30 (weight-change repaint median 203 ms on this sandbox, within the 300 ms bound; it was about 100 ms earlier today, so watch it).

## Work package 5: the O1–O3 PPA and carbon rules (2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/ppa-rules` (from `feature/flex-batteries`). Decisions: walkthrough O1–O3 (2026-10-21), OPEN-QUESTIONS 22 (O1: do not generate undeliverable PPAs), 49 and 62 (PPA off unless ticked; then each strategy with and without, one coverage). AC 21–23.

What changed:
- **PPA tick** (new records `utilities.ppaInclude` false, `utilities.ppaType` hybrid, `utilities.ppaCoverage` 100 %; inputs in the utilities section): C13 is locked to None unless ticked; ticked, C13 = None and the entered type, C14 locked to the entered coverage. C14 offers 25, 50, 75, 80 and 100 %. Archetypes with a PPA (A2, A7, A9) use the entered type and coverage when one is ticked, and keep their own definition otherwise (they are references, always generated). The **T1 example ticks a hybrid PPA at 100 %**, so the PPA rules stay exercised (T3 inherits it, and rule k removes it).
- **O1, rule (k):** a PPA is not generated when islanded or when there is no grid capacity at any time; archetypes drop their PPA in those cases.
- **O2:** hourly CFE counts PPA volume only up to the grid energy used for the load in the hour (PPA delivered + grid import), not up to the residual load.
- **O3:** the `grid-ppa` flag is replaced by `ppa-import-limited` (minor) and `ppa-above-load` (info), each with its share of the undelivered volume (the shares add to 100 %); the hedge explanation is in each.
- **Known issue 13, fixed with the owner's approval:** an economic plant whose remaining demand was below its minimum stable load was skipped even when the grid connection was full, leaving that demand unmet. It is now "needed" when the remaining demand exceeds the remaining grid capacity. It surfaced because a PPA fills the import capacity: before the fix every T1 PPA option was "not adequate" (0.2 MW unmet in about 15 hours).

T1 effect: 55,060 combinations (rule k removes 5,400; T3 41,880); 2,000 sampled; 818 with the hybrid PPA (636 adequate after the fix) and 1,182 without. Before the fix the leader had no PPA and the best PPA option ranked 931 (every PPA option not adequate). After it: 1,566 of 2,000 adequate (930 without a PPA, 636 with); leader Grid + rented temporary generation bridge → remove · HVO N+1 · DLC · PPA hybrid 100 % (score 92.69, CFE 66.6 %; undelivered PPA volume 0.015 % import-limited and 99.985 % above the load).

Test run (work package 5, 2026-10-23): `npm test` 58/58 (new test 28, `tests/ppa-tests.mjs`; the port test checks the two O3 flags on every option); `npm run build`; `npm run test:browser` 30/30. Browser check B19 now closes "Run details" when it opened because the generation changed (§12): since the T1 example ticks a PPA and the defaults do not, "Reset to defaults" changes the rule counts.

## Work packages 6 and 7: results cards, trade-off defaults, Compare (2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/results-cards` (from `feature/ppa-rules`). The owner's standing instruction of 2026-10-23 ("I'm happy to follow your recommendation every time"): OPEN-QUESTIONS 24, 29, 30 and 35 answered with Claude's recommendations, recorded as such.

What changed:
- **`src/engine/cards.js`** (pure): `topCards` picks five distinct strategies (C1, C2, C4, C5), the best-ranked option of each in rank order, skipping options whose figures equal a better-ranked one's; `prosCons` judges LCOE, capex, location carbon, hourly CFE, time-to-power and deliverability against every option in the run (pro: best 10 %; con: worse half; up to three each); `identicalResults` (KNOWN-ISSUES item 5) marks "same results as #k"; `describeWeightChange` writes the live description.

Interpretations (reversible): pros = best 10 % of the run, cons = worse half; the assumed-defaults list on each card waits for work package 2; "same results as" compares nine figures plus resilience and adequacy, to ten significant figures.

T1 at the time: cards #1, #2, #3, #4, #6 (#5 repeats a strategy); 64 options marked "same results as". *Superseded*: the run checked by Haiku (H3) gave #1, #2, #3, #5, #6; the current figures are under "Verification fixes" below.

## Work package 9: tornado drivers and measures (2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/results-cards`, committed with packages 6 and 7 (the edits shared files with them). The decisions are the walkthrough's (BUILD-PLAN package 9); no open question was listed.

What changed:
- **Sixteen drivers** in `src/engine/sensitivity.js` (`TORNADO_DRIVERS`): the seven built before, plus capex, capacity factors, PUE, battery capex, grid connection capex, evaluation period, escalation, flexibility trigger level and generator test hours.
- **Seven ticked by default** (`DEFAULT_DRIVER_IDS`, the decision log's list): gas price, grid price, WACC, carbon price, capex, capacity factors, grid connection date. The Sensitivity tab has a tick box per driver, "Default seven" and "Tick all"; the choice stays while the app is open.
- **Measure** (`TORNADO_MEASURES`): LCOE by default (record `sensitivity.defaultMeasure`), NPV of net cost, location-based carbon, weighted score. Rows sort by the swing in the measure; changing the measure re-sorts the stored rows without a re-run (`sortRows`). The base check still compares the weighted score with the ranking.
- **New multipliers, all 1 in the main run** (so they change nothing outside the tornado), each a record labelled indicative — unverified:
  - `economics.capexFactor` (0.7–1.5): every capex line, before contingency; replacements follow.
  - `economics.bessCapexFactor` (0.7–1.3): lines named BESS or battery.
  - `economics.gridCapexFactor` (0.5–2): the grid connection and reinforcement line.
  - `utilities.reYieldFactor` (0.85–1.15): hourly on-site solar and wind output in dispatch (part of the dispatch key).
  - `load.coolingFactor` (0.8–1.2): the cooling and mechanical share of the hourly facility load, and the cooling term of the design-day PUE.
  - `sensitivity.flexTriggerFactor` (0.8–1.2): scales the price, carbon and temperature trigger levels together.
- Evaluation period (15–25 years), escalation (−2 to +2 %/yr) and test hours (3–24 h/yr) use their records' own low and high.

Interpretations (reversible): the capex factors scale lines by name; "capacity factors" means on-site solar and wind (not the PPA profile); "PUE" scales cooling energy rather than setting a PUE value.

Tests: 13a (rows sorted by the LCOE swing; score sort still checked), 13d (each new driver moves the result it acts on; default seven; measures), browser B23 (seven ticked, measure switch, untick asks for a new run).

Site geometry, site-fit flags and the site plan are left out.

## Work package 3: contracted grid capacity and the grid connection charge (2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/grid-contract` (from `feature/results-cards`). OPEN-QUESTIONS 19 and 20 answered with Claude's recommendations, accepted in advance by the owner (2026-10-23); OPEN-QUESTIONS 50 built; KNOWN-ISSUES item 7 resolved by the answer to 20.

What changed:
- **Contracted capacity, a rule per grid strategy** (OPEN-QUESTIONS 19): new records `grid.contractWaitMW`, `grid.contractBridgeMW`, `grid.contractExportMW` (blank = the full offer). `contractedCap(ctx, C1)` in `context.js` gives min(offer, contracted) per month. Sizing (`grid.byMonth`, top-up sizing), time-to-power, the timeline (`inspect.js`) and dispatch (`cfg.gridLimit`, part of the dispatch key; it limits load import and grid charging) use it. Islanded options are unchanged: they already use the connection as backup only.

The cable route from the POC is now its own line, "Grid cable route (POC to site)", also only on grid-connected options. The tornado's grid connection capex driver scales both. Before: "Grid connection and reinforcement" = offered MW × the standard rate per MW + cable, on every option.
- **Expected figure** (OPEN-QUESTIONS 50): `expectedConnectionCharge(inputs, C1)` = the standard rate per MW for the voltage band (substation records, indicative — unverified) × the contracted MW at full connection; shown under the field with a "Use the expected figure" button.

T1 effect: the €18 m connection line (120 MW × €150k/MW) leaves every option, so LCOEs fall by about €3/MWh; the leader is unchanged (Grid + rented temporary generation bridge → remove · HVO N+1 · DLC · PPA hybrid 100 %, score 92.70, LCOE €153.05/MWh). Islanded options also lose the cable route.

Interpretations (reversible): the substation stays on every option (it serves on-site plant too); editing one option's capacity after the run is not built; the export strategy's contract has no field of its own yet (record only).

Tests: 31 (80 MW of 120 MW never exceeded in any hour; sizing and time-to-power follow; blank = offer; charge default 0 and flagged; one amount on grid options, none on islanded; expected figure; tornado driver with a charge), 9 updated (cable route line), 13d (grid capex moves only when there is something to scale), browser B33.

## Work package 2: knowledge levels, assumed-default flags, economics section (2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/input-model` (from `feature/grid-contract`). OPEN-QUESTIONS 21, 25, 26 and 28 answered with Claude's recommendations, accepted in advance by the owner (2026-10-23); KNOWN-ISSUES item 6 resolved by 26. The Site tab's Beta switch (AC 27) and the solar areas were built earlier on `feature/site-tab-beta` and `feature/solar-areas`.

What changed:
- **Only the location and the site area are required.** `load.itMW` and the three grid values default to blank ("Not known"), each a record. `resolveUnknowns` (in `normalizeInputs`) fills them and writes `inputs.assumedInputs` (path → how it was set):
  - IT load: the sum of the phases if phases were entered; otherwise the massing rule inverted (OPEN-QUESTIONS 26): area × `load.estimateCoverage` (35 %) × storeys ÷ gross-up × whitespace density of the cooling type (air when open). T1's 15 ha → 71.6 MW. The estimate has a floor of 1 MW (`Math.max(1, …)`), reached only by sites under about 0.2 ha (Haiku check H2, L8).
  - Grid: now `grid.unknownNowMW` (0), full `grid.unknownFullPerITMW` (1.4) × IT, date start + `grid.unknownFullMonths` (48). One value for every country; country values to research.
  - The T1 example now enters 100 MW and 20 / 120 MW at month 30 explicitly (`T1_EXAMPLE`), so every T1 figure is unchanged.
- **Assumed-default flags** (`src/engine/assumed.js`, AC 16): one info flag (category "Assumed default", `assumed: true`, `path`) per key input not entered that the option uses (25 key inputs, each with a rule for which options use it, for example the gas price only on gas-burning options, the grid values only on grid-connected ones), plus one flag counting the registry records. They are listed under "Assumed defaults" in the option detail, on the cards and in Compare, and kept out of the flag counts and the "Flags" sort.
- **Cooling not chosen** (OPEN-QUESTIONS 28): the open choice compares all four types, flagged "assumed" on every option.
- **Evaluation periods** (OPEN-QUESTIONS 21): `economics.displayPeriods` (10, 15); `eco.byPeriod` holds LCOE and NPV for each period and the scoring period (`economics.periodYears`), from the same ledger function; shown in the option detail. The context covers the longest period.
- **Wind land** (OPEN-QUESTIONS 25): unchanged (net developable area of the site model, independent of the Beta tab); recorded as the answer.
- **Inputs panel:** IT load and grid values accept blank with "Not known (…)" placeholders and the estimate in the hint; a new section **5 · Economics** (WACC with its plain explanation, scoring period, further periods, contingency, carbon price, three escalations, currency and revenue note); later sections renumbered (6 Requirements, 7 Choices).
- **PDF:** inputs filled by `resolveUnknowns` are listed as "Default: <how it was set>".

Not built: country values for "Not known" grid inputs, latest carbon and gas prices with dates (package 0 research), currency per site (package 1), typical PUE and maximum demand next to the cooling input.

Tests: 32 (defaults-only run, estimates, flags per option and their removal, periods).

## Work package 8: screening tier and dispatch for any period (2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/input-model`, committed with package 2 (the edits shared files). OPEN-QUESTIONS 23 answered with Claude's recommendation, accepted in advance by the owner (2026-10-23).

What changed:
- **Run setting `settings.screeningModel`** ('full' by default, or 'repDays') and **`settings.verifyTop`** (10). Off by default: power adequacy needs every hour.
- **Representative days** (`representativeHours` in `dispatch.js`, cached on the context): per month the design day (if in that month), the day with the highest mean grid price and the most typical day (smallest standardised distance to the month's mean price, solar and wind); weights add to 8,760 h. Chosen from the country profiles, so every option uses the same days (T1: 25 days). *Replaced after Haiku check H2*: three clusters a month (k-medoids, `settings.repDaysPerMonth`), see "Verification fixes" below.
- **Weighted dispatch:** `dispatchYear(..., rep)` loops over the representative hours with each hour's weight on every annual total (energy, cost, carbon, hours, plant output and fuel, flexibility budget). With `rep = null` every weight is 1, so the full-year arithmetic is unchanged (all earlier tests pass unchanged).
- **Verification** (`src/engine/verify.js`): after scoring, the best `verifyTop` options are re-run with `evaluateOption` (full year), their figures replaced (`model: "Full-year hourly (8760 h), verified"`, `screening` keeps the screening figures), and every option re-scored. In the browser's worker pool this runs on the main thread after the merge, in time slices, with a notice; `refreshAdequacyFlag` recounts adequacy.
- **Screens:** "Dispatch model" in Run and dispatch settings; the header strip and each option's detail say which model produced the figures; the Dispatch tab offers only verified options in a screening run, and **any period** (start day, 1–31 days) beside the weeks.

T1 result (test 33) at the time: LCOE within 3.8 % of the full year for every option (AC 4 target ±5 %); location carbon within about 12.5 %; adequacy count unchanged (1,566); 9 of the top 10 the same; leader unchanged; about 9 s against 15 s. *Superseded*: see "Verification fixes" below (0.77 % and 3.40 %).

Interpretations (reversible): battery charge carries from one representative day to the next; the longest unmet event is counted within representative days only; carbon is not part of the ±5 % check.

Tests: 33, browser B35.

## Work package 1: map pin, country from the pin, display currency (2026-10-23)

Branch `feature/input-model`, committed with packages 2 and 8 (the edits shared files). OPEN-QUESTIONS 63 (map data), 64 (ASHRAE) and 65 (weather processing) — the build plan's "ask the owner" items for this package — answered with Claude's recommendations, accepted in advance by the owner (2026-10-23).

## Work package 11: engine fixes from the Haiku checks and the Pego study (2026-10-24)

Branch `feature/engine-fixes` (from `feature/verification-fixes`). The owner asked to continue the app development; every decision follows Claude's recommendation, accepted in advance by the owner (OPEN-QUESTIONS 68–76, build plan package 11). | Known issue | Fix | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 18 Entered grid price adder ignored | `grid.priceAdder`, when entered, replaces the country value; field "Network charges and levies on grid energy" in section 4 (country value as placeholder) | `context.js`, `InputsPanel.jsx` |
| 17 Dispatch stops before full load | `settings.maxDispatchYears` 8 → 15 (field "Most dispatch years" in the run settings); run flag `dispatch-truncated` (minor) when a configuration needs more years than the cap; the timeline grows to cover the last phase, its ramp and the full grid date plus a year | `defaults.js`, `dispatch.js` (`dispatchYearsNeeded`), `run.js`, `context.js` |
| 1 Repeated year still ramping | The first year that starts after the last change is the one repeated (`ceil`, was `floor`) | `dispatch.js`, `dispatchYears` |

| 3 Export income ignores the price multiplier | Export price × multiplier | `dispatch.js` |
| 11 Plant below its minimum stable load | Runs at its minimum and serves up to the whole remaining demand; the grid import backs off; only output above demand is surplus. On the old code, 24 sampled T1 options had 1,176 hours importing while curtailing; now none | `dispatch.js`, firm plant loop |
| 15 Rented plant paid from availability | Hired `settings.rentalLeadMonths` (1, judgement, 0–6) before the first month of shortfall (islanded: of load). `bridge.earliestMonth` keeps the programme month; dispatch, time-to-power and rental start at the hire month; a sizing sentence says so | `sizing.js`, `defaults.js` |
| 16 Retained plant bought at once | Bought in step with the buildings: the bridging units at the bridging date, then each building's critical-load units with redundancy at its date (capex lines "(bridging)" and "(retained as backup)"); fixed O&M follows the units owned; retained rented plant is hired in the same steps. Dispatch still counts the whole retained fleet from its first date (it serves only as backup or above-grid prime) | `sizing.js` (`retainedPhasing`), `evaluate.js` |
| 10 Gas flag with liquid-fuel backup | `res-gas` only when plant that does not burn gas covers less than the critical load (message gives both MW); resilience status "Meets with flags" follows the same rule; `resilience()` returns `nonGasFirmMW` and `gasDependent` | `evaluate.js`, `resilience` |
| H1 L-1 Seveso reads only the backup store | Liquid fuel for running HVO-diesel prime or bridging plant (autonomy hours at full output) counts; retained plant that is the backup is already in the backup store | `permitting.js` (`runFuelM3`) |
| 14 Time-to-power rewards early plant | Axis = months late (first power against phase 1 + full load against the last phase) + half the power gap in months of design load; labels "months late". Card pros need no more than 10 % of the run as good or better | `evaluate.js`, `pareto.js`, `cards.js`, `ParetoTable.jsx`, `OptionDetail.jsx` |
| 9 First power at month 0 | First power = the later of the phase 1 date and the month the supply is ready (`supplyReadyM` keeps the latter) | `evaluate.js`, `timeToPower` |
| 8 Opex year 1 = €0 | "Opex, first year with load" (`opexYear1Index`) | `evaluate.js`, `OptionDetail.jsx` |

OPEN-QUESTIONS 74 (the flexibility recovery period also follows events that end on their own) is kept as built.

T1 after the fixes (2,000 options, full year): 1,537 adequate; leader O7PMBVU "Grid + HVO-diesel prime bridge → retain as prime · HVO N+1 · DLC · PPA hybrid 100 %", score 93.89, LCOE €153.5/MWh, capex €100 m, 122 g/kWh location-based; first power month 18 (phase 1), opex in the first year with load (2028) €5.8 m. 1,537 options are on time with no gap (time-to-power 0), so the axis now separates only options that are late. `res-gas` on 501 options (1,246 options with gas plant are not flagged because plant that does not burn gas covers the critical load). The leader changed from OUK8E73 because the retained fleet is no longer bought at once and rented plant is no longer paid from availability.

Tests: new module `tests/engine-fixes-tests.mjs`, tests 35–37 (adder, timeline, repeated year, truncation flag; minimum stable load, export, PPA escalation, rented hire month, retained phasing and rental, gas flag, Seveso store; time-to-power metric, first power, opex year, pros). Tests 18 and 21 now compare rented plant's `earliestMonth` with the programme rule. Tests 1 and 2 record 30 years. 68/68 Node tests passed.

### Known issue 19: PPA energy displaced by plant at its minimum stable load (2026-10-24)

Found in the Pego study, revision 3.1: with a 50 % hybrid PPA, when the PPA filled the grid connection and the campus needed a few MW more, a CCGT unit ran at its minimum (192 MW) and the output above the demand was curtailed, 256 GWh in 2032. Fix (OPEN-QUESTIONS 77, Claude's recommendation accepted in advance by the owner): after the firm plant step, the PPA delivery backs off by up to the plants' output above the demand at minimum stable load; that volume moves from delivered to undelivered PPA (the hedge line, outside the ledger), the network charge on it is not paid, and the plant's curtailed output falls by the same amount. New year field `ppaMslBackMWh` and hedge field `mslBackMWh`; the `ppa-above-load` flag names the volume. Test 38: 60 T1 options with a PPA and firm plant, no hour takes PPA energy while output is curtailed (6,352 such hours on the code before the fix); 17,358 MWh displaced.

## Work package 12: existing plant near the site, leased (2026-10-24)

Branch `feature/engine-fixes` (continued). Known issue 12, found in the Pego study, which needed a study build for the Elecgás CCGT. Decisions: OPEN-QUESTIONS 78–82, Claude's recommendations accepted in advance by the owner.

- **Inputs** (`inputs.existing`, section 4, off by default): name, type (`ccgt` / `ocgt` / `engines`), units offered and net output per unit (no defaults, required), available from (YYYY-MM; empty = analysis start), lease €/kW a month (6, judgement), efficiency, minimum stable load and variable O&M (empty = by type), connection to the campus (€). New records `existing.*`; the CCGT type has three judgement records (`existing.types.ccgt.*`: 56 %, 45 % of a unit, €3/MWh); open-cycle turbines and engines reuse the `gasTurbine` and `gasRecip` records. Inputs saved before this package get the defaults in `normalizeInputs`.
- **Technology record** `TECH.existing` (`defaults.js`): set from the inputs at every `buildContext` (`applyExistingPlant`), so the worker, the inspector and the tornado all follow the run's inputs. Flags on the record: `fixedUnits`, `lease`, `offsite`, `ownConsent`. `TECH.rented.lease = true`, so rented and leased plant share one hire rule and one rental loop.
- **Choices and rules:** C2 and C4 gain "Existing plant (lease)", in the default open set only when the plant is entered (`existingEntered`); rules (l) entered and one role, (m) as bridging only with C3 = Remove or Retain as prime and never C5 = Retained, (n) as firm plant one C4 sizing. "Values not generated" says why when no plant is entered; run flag `existing-incomplete` when ticked without units or unit size.
- **Sizing:** exactly the units offered, no N+1, with a sentence; available from the month entered (no permitting allowance); lease start = max(available, first need − `settings.rentalLeadMonths`): as bridging, the first month of shortfall; as firm plant, the first month the design load exceeds the grid plus any bridging plant still on site (baseload: the first load). Fuel available from the start (own gas supply).
- **Economics:** no plant capex or fixed O&M; the lease by the month in the rental line (bridging while on site, firm from its start); the connection to the campus as one capex line when the lease starts (life 25; none if the plant is handed back when the grid arrives).
- **Outside the campus:** not in the IED / MCP aggregation (`combustionPlant`), not a reason for the campus gas pipe or the gas-unknown deliverability item, not drawn on the plan (`plantBlocks`).
- **Report:** the PDF lists the existing-plant inputs as their own section.

T1 with an example plant (2 × 40 MW CCGT from January 2028, €6/kW a month, €8 m connection), before work package 14 (superseded; after it, 141 and 305 options, cheapest €139.2/MWh, Haiku check H4): 112 options use it as bridging plant and 311 as firm plant out of 2,000; the cheapest of them, "Grid · Existing plant (lease) top-up · Diesel N+1 · Evaporative-adiabatic · PV 5.6 MWp · PPA hybrid 100 %", costs €142.7/MWh against the leader's €152.3 but ranks 25th on carbon (188 g/kWh against 122). Test 39; 70/70 Node tests.

## Work package 13: site layout in the run (2026-10-24)

Known issue 20, OPEN-QUESTIONS 83 (Claude's recommendation accepted in advance by the owner). Found by running Pego on the product with the new existing-plant input: the site-layout stage dominated the run (about 230 ground blocks an option, hundreds of them genset blocks; the per-set cost grows roughly with the square of the blocks).

| Case | Options | Distinct block sets | Blocks per set | Grid cells | Layout per set | All sets |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | 2,000 | 1,650 | 21.3 | 238 × 159 (2 m) | 14 ms (alone; 5.4 s as one shared batch) | 22 s alone, 5–10 s in the run |
| Pego (product inputs, before work package 14) | 2,000 | 1,433 | 229.2 | 300 × 200 (5 m) | 364 ms (one set alone) | about 9 minutes on one core (estimate: sets × time per set) |

- **Rule:** layout work = ground blocks squared, summed over the options of the run (a pool worker scales its interleaved slice to the whole list, so every worker decides alike). Above `settings.siteLayoutWorkLimit` (20 million, judgement; T1 0.94 million, Pego about 105 million), and when site fit cannot change the ranking (deliverability site-fit penalty off, compliant-first ranking off) and `settings.siteLayoutScope` is "auto", every option gets the site fit "Not laid out" (`notLaidOut()` in `run.js`, `skipped: true`), the layout stage logs `onDemand`, and an info run flag `site-layout-on-demand` explains it.
- **On demand:** the Site tab already laid out the option it shows (`layoutForOption`); it now takes that layout's site fit for options the run skipped, in the plan and in the small multiples. The option detail says "Not laid out in the run" with a link to the Site tab; the ranked list shows "Not laid out" and no land take; land-take sorting and the land-take ceiling filter treat such options as unknown. The inspector does not compare site fit for them.
- **Unchanged:** scores, ranks, LCOE, deliverability, adequacy and main-flow flags (test 40 compares all 2,000 T1 options with the limit forced down). Pego on the product with the existing-plant input, before work package 14: 16 s, leader "rent, grid, existing CCGT top-up" (no PPA) at €141.1/MWh. After work package 14 the sampled 2,000 options differ (the combination space changed), and the stored study `studies/pego-rev3/pego-product.study.json` (run with `pego-product.mjs`) gives 14.4 s and the leader "rent, grid, existing CCGT top-up, 50 % PPA" at €144.2/MWh; the Haiku check (H4) reproduced €144.19 with its own rebuilt inputs. The study build of revision 3.1 (€141.3/MWh without a PPA) remains the basis of the delivered report.
- **For the owner (not decided):** skipping the layout in every run while the Site tab is off would make T1 run in about 8 s instead of 17 s.
- Tests: test 40; 71/71 Node tests.
