OPEN-QUESTIONS 27 (Claude's recommendation accepted in advance by the owner). `src/engine/study.js`:

## Fixes after Haiku check H4 (2026-10-24)

Report: `verification/engine-fixes/H4-packages-11-15.md` (33 verified, 15 failed, 7 not verified; 0 high, 4 medium, 12 low). The engine, the energy balance, the ledger and the flag-only rule held; the failures were claims, figures and edge cases.

| Finding | Fix |
|---|---|
| M-1: a land-take ceiling (or a site-fit filter) hid every option of a large campus that was not laid out | Options not laid out stay in view under both filters; the chip says how many ("N not laid out, kept"). Land-take sorting keeps them last as a group (L-5, no change) |

## Investigation: a search around the best options (2026-10-24, not built)

Question: with sampling (2,000 of 48,000–120,000 combinations), can the run miss a better option next to its leader? A prototype (scratch script, not in the app) took the best 10 options, evaluated every option one choice away from them that the sample had not screened (choices that become applicable expanded over their open values), re-scored, and repeated three times.

| Case | Neighbours evaluated | Leader before | Leader after | Time added |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | 353 (168 + 123 + 62) | OP13Y1Y, 94.15, €151.9/MWh | unchanged; the next nine places filled by close variants (other PV sizes, split instead of prime) within 0.02 points | about 7 s |
| Pego on the product (`pego-product.study.json`) | 196 (127 + 62 + 7) | rent, grid, existing CCGT top-up, 50 % PPA: 86.24, €144.2/MWh | unchanged; the same plan without the PPA was found at €141.0/MWh, rank 4 (lower score: more carbon) | about 20 minutes here, because each extra option was laid out on the site |

Conclusion: in both cases the sampled leader held; the search added near-variants, not better strategies. The €141.1 against €144.2 difference seen for Pego is the PPA trade-off on the score, not a missed option. Not built; the top-five cards already show distinct strategies. If wanted later, it would need the layout skipped for large campuses and the pool's post-merge slicing (as the screening verification uses).

## Work package 16: plain-English summary (2026-10-24)

OPEN-QUESTIONS 12 and 86–92 (Claude's recommendations accepted in advance by the owner). `src/engine/summary.js`, pure, no network, no language model:

- `plainSummary(run, { sensitivity, siteBeta, wordLimit = 250 })` → `{ title: 'Summary', parts: [7 × { id, heading, text }], details: { part id: [lines] }, guardrail, text, words, moved, weightChanges, usedTornado, notBuilt, leaderId, comparedWith }`. Parts in the §14 order: `leader`, `why`, `gives-up`, `confidence`, `change`, `flags`, `inputs`. Each part is built from required and optional sentences (priority 1–4); over the limit, optional sentences move to the part's detail, highest priority number first, from the last part back. Words = tokens with a letter or digit; the count includes the guardrail, not the headings.
- Part 2: #2, or the next option whose figures differ (`sameFigures`); the gap is split into `w_k × (s_k(#1) − s_k(#2))`, whose four parts, with their signs, add up to it exactly. Every part above 0.005 points is named: those for #1 with their figures (`compactPair` writes a shared unit once: "€152 against €153/MWh"), those for #2 by their points ("less 0.02 points on hourly CFE, where #2 does better"), so the named parts add up to the stated gap to rounding (Haiku check H5, M4).
- Part 3: the axes where #2 is better; then up to two axes where the best option of the leader's group (same adequacy, and the same compliance when "rank requirement-compliant first" is on) is more than 0.5 axis points better (judgement; smaller gaps are in the detail).
- Part 4: options of the group within 1 point (0.1 and 5 in the detail); the leader's `assumed:*` flags; unverified registry records. "Rank probability and the robust pick are not built yet" is optional (priority 3).
- Part 5, `weightChanges(run)`: for each axis, up and down, the crossing `t = gap ÷ (gap + D_j − D_L)` with D the score at the target weights (all of the axis; or none of it, the rest in proportion), minimised over the group; reported at the first whole per cent past the crossing, with the best option there (checked, not assumed). Sorted by the change in percentage points. Test 43 re-scores each one with `scoreOptions` (adequacy grouping included) and checks one per cent short of it too.
- Part 6: resilience status and its flags; Permitting and Fuel and utilities (majors by their first clause via `shortFlag`, which also drops "(flagged in §8 C2)"; minors counted, or named when there is one); other majors; `visibleFlags(o, siteBeta)` keeps site fit out unless the Site tab is on; the PDF always passes `siteBeta: false`.
- Part 7 and the part 1 range: only from a tornado result whose `optionId` is the leader (`latestSensitivity` in the app; the `sensitivity` view of the PDF). Rows sorted by `measureSwing` (LCOE when the measure is the score), ties by record confidence (C first). A tornado on another option is named as such.
- Speed: the leader and #2 are found by a linear pass (a full sort only when #2 has the same figures); 10 ms for T1's 2,000 options (of 48,340 combinations) in Node. Browser check B38 measures the weight preset to summary repaint (bound 300 ms, as B9).
- UI: `SummaryPanel.jsx` at the top of the Options tab (Hide/Show kept in App state), "Edit summary" → textarea → "Save note": the note `{ text, key: summaryKey(run), leaderId, leaderName, editedAt }` is App state, saved in the study file as `notes.summary` (a damaged note is dropped with a warning), printed in the PDF; a key change shows "Written for an earlier result" and opens the generated summary under the note. `summaryKey` = input hash, leader id, weights (per mille), cost and carbon metrics, compliant-first. "Load T1 example" and "Reset to defaults" clear the note.
- PDF: a "Summary" page after the cover (sections list starts with "Summary"; test 30 updated), the generated text, the note with "(edited)", and the detail of every part but the flags (section 8 lists them).
- T1 (analysis start 2027-01): 242 words; with the seven default tornado drivers on the leader, 250 words, LCOE €140–171/MWh, most influence grid import price (€30.7/MWh), grid full-connection date (€11.7/MWh), capex (€10.7/MWh). Nearest weight change: deliverability 15 % → 0 % (crossing 0.3 %), then #26 leads.
- Not built and said so: ranges (Monte Carlo), rank probability and the robust pick. Breakevens: built in work package 17 (below); part 5 names the two nearest once run (`opts.breakevens`), and "More detail" lists the tornado and the breakevens as not run until they are (`notRun`). The word-limit loop takes the priorities present in descending order (the tornado range is 1.5, kept longer than the "Next:" inputs at 1).
- Tests: Node test 43 (`tests/summary-tests.mjs`); browser check B38.

### Known issue 21: assumed-default flags in pool workers (2026-10-24)

Found while capturing the summary for the guide: the app said the T1 leader uses 2 key inputs at their defaults, Node said 13 (17 for the leader of test 44's 30-option run). `assumedBase()` reads the registry, whose input and run-setting records are created by `buildDefaultInputs()`; a pool worker runs inputs built on the main thread and never called it, so every registry-backed key input went unflagged and the "assumed-records" flag counted 387 records instead of 563. Fix: `assumedBase()` calls `buildDefaultInputs()` when the registry has no input records (`economics.wacc` as the marker), as `registryValue()` already does for a missing path. Test 44 runs the engine in a fresh Node process from inputs JSON and compares the leader's assumed flags and record count with this process (before the fix: 2 against 17 for that run's leader).

## Work package 17: breakeven table (2026-10-24)

OPEN-QUESTIONS 93–97 (Claude's recommendations accepted in advance by the owner). `src/engine/breakeven.js`, pure:

- `breakevenCompetitors(run)`: #2 (or the next option whose figures differ, `sameFigures`), then the best option of each other strategy from `topCards`, filled with next-ranked options; sorted by rank; `sensitivity.breakevenCompetitors` (5, clamped 1–10).
- `breakevenSteps(run, { site })` (generator, one re-run per step) → `{ leaderId, competitors, rows: [{ id, label, low, high, base, whole, swing, cells: [{ competitorId, flips: { down, up } }] }], tolerance, evaluations, gridDate }`. Step 1 re-runs the leader at the low and high of all sixteen `TORNADO_DRIVERS` and keeps the `sensitivity.breakevenDrivers` (8) largest score swings (clipped scores, as the tornado shows them). Step 2 re-runs the leader and every competitor at the middle and end of each side (the run's own figures at the current value). Step 3 bisects the first change of order on each side, walking out from the current value, to `sensitivity.breakevenTolerance` (2 %) of the range; whole-number drivers (grid date, evaluation period, test hours) to 1. Each re-run point builds one context for all options (`buildContext` with the driver applied as the tornado applies it; gas and carbon price from the base context).
- Order (`lead`): adequacy, then compliance when "rank requirement-compliant first" is on, then the weighted score on the run's axis ranges **without clipping** (`lineScore`), ties to the lower option code. Clipping would let both options score 100 on an axis once a driver moves them past the run's range (every LCOE below the run's lowest at grid price × 0.7).
- `driverBase(d, inputs)`: the current value on the driver's scale (factors 1, grid date 0 months, WACC, period, escalation and test hours from the inputs). `nearestFlips(be, n)`: flips sorted by |flip − current| ÷ range; `flipText` gives "#7 would lead if the grid full-connection date slipped by 14 months or more".
- UI: `BreakevenPanel.jsx` under the tornado on the Sensitivity tab; results cached by run, weights and scoring settings; `latestBreakevens(run)` for the PDF (section 7, "Breakevens") and the summary.
- T1 (analysis start 2027-01): competitors #2, #5, #7, #11, #16; drivers escalation, grid price, grid date, grid carbon, period, capex, WACC, battery capex; 224 re-runs, 4–6 s in Node; two flips: #7 if the grid date slips 14 months or more, #2 if capex rises to × 1.41 (checked by direct evaluation: at × 1.5 the leader's LCOE is €158.57 against #2's €158.07/MWh, the leader's capex lines growing faster).
- §17 target ≤ 3 s: not met (full engine on the main thread): 224 re-runs in 6.8 s in Chromium on this machine (check B39).
- Three new run-setting and judgement records: the registry has 566 records (appendix A regenerated).
- Tests: Node test 45 (`tests/breakeven-tests.mjs`: competitors, drivers against the leader's full tornado, every flip re-run on both sides of its bracket, every "no flip" re-run at the range end, determinism, the run unchanged, the PDF); browser check B39.

## Work package 18: uncertainty by Monte Carlo (2026-10-25)

OPEN-QUESTIONS 98–104 (Claude's recommendations accepted in advance by the owner). `src/engine/montecarlo.js`, pure:

- Inputs: `MC_DRIVER_IDS`, thirteen of the sixteen `TORNADO_DRIVERS` (not `it-load`, `period`, `flex-trigger`), each `triangular(u, min(low, current), current, max(high, current))` with `driverBase` as the current value; grid date and test hours rounded to whole numbers; applied together with each driver's `apply` (gas and carbon price from the base context).
- `latinHypercube(n, ids, seed, correlations)`: one stratum per sample per driver, shuffled with `mulberry32(subSeed(seed, id))`, a random point inside each stratum; correlated pairs `[a, b, ρ]` by a normal copula (`normInv`, Acklam; `normCdf`, Abramowitz–Stegun 7.1.26), b taken as ρ·z_a + √(1 − ρ²)·z_b. Pairs from `uncertainty.correlationPrices` (grid–gas 0.6) and `uncertainty.correlationCapex` (capex–battery capex, capex–grid capex 0.8). Test 46: rank correlations 0.60 and 0.78 on 300 samples; unrelated 0.06.
- `monteCarloSteps(run, { site, samples, nOptions, seed, screening })`: the top `uncertainty.options` (20) by rank plus pinned options; per sample one `buildContext` with every driver applied, `representativeHours(ctx)` when `uncertainty.screening`, then `evaluateOption(ctx, v, { rep, skipLayout })` for each option. `skipLayout` follows run.js (no layout unless the site-fit penalty or compliant-first is on, or the run itself skipped it): the layout changes no figure the ranking reads (test 46 compares LCOE, carbon and deliverability with and without it). Order per sample: adequacy, compliance (setting), unclipped `lineScore`, option code. Counts: leads, top three, within `uncertainty.robustBand` (1 %) of the best score among the sample's first group; adequate. A `partial` summary every ten samples; the first three samples kept whole (`firstSamples`) so test 46 re-runs one alone and gets the same LCOE and score.
- Result: `rows` (P10/P50/P90 of LCOE, NPV, capex, location carbon, first power, full load, score; `pRank1`, `pTop3`, `pRobust`, `pAdequate`), `robustPickId` (ties: leads more often, then rank), `mostOftenFirstId`, `drivers` (the sampled values' P10/P50/P90), `complete`.
- `evaluateOption(ctx, v, { recordYears, rep, skipLayout })`: `rep` passes representative hours to `dispatchAll`; `skipLayout` gives `notLaidOut(net area)` (moved from run.js to sitefit.js, still exported from run.js).
- UI: `UncertaintyPanel.jsx` (samples 100 / 300 / 1,000; steps run until 25 ms have passed, then yield to the page; cached by run, weights and scoring settings, and samples); `latestMonteCarlo(run)` (complete results only) for the header strip ("Leads in 82 % of 100 Monte Carlo samples"), the summary and the PDF. `onResult` from the breakeven and Monte Carlo panels re-renders App so the header and summary pick up the result.
- Summary: part 1 gives "In N Monte Carlo samples its LCOE is … to … (P10 to P90; P50 …)" (the tornado range then goes to the detail); part 4 gives the probability of leading and of the top three and whether the robust pick differs. `notBuilt` is now empty; `notRun` lists the tornado, breakevens and Monte Carlo until they are run; `summaryKey` adds `m` for the Monte Carlo.
- PDF: section 7 "Uncertainty (Monte Carlo)" after the breakevens.
- Speed: T1, 100 samples × 20 options: 4.8 s in Node (2.4 ms a re-run with representative days; about 12 ms with the full year), 9.4 s in Chromium (B40); 300 × 20 about 12 s and 30 s. §17's ≤ 8 s is not met.
- T1 (100 samples): #1 leads in 82 %, top three 98 %, LCOE P10–P90 €145–171/MWh (the run's €151.9 is a central value of skewed ranges: the grid price runs × 0.7 to × 1.5 and the grid date −6 to +24 months), robust pick #1; #7 leads in 17 %.
- Seven new records (`uncertainty.samples`, `.options`, `.robustBand`, `.seed`, `.screening`, `.correlationPrices`, `.correlationCapex`): 573 records.
- Tests: Node test 46 (`tests/montecarlo-tests.mjs`); browser check B40 (also checks the page stays responsive: the longest timer wait while it runs was 115 ms).

## Phone version (2026-10-30, branch feature/phone-2026-10-30)

The owner asked on 2026-10-30, in writing: "Create a proper working phone version" (the phone had been dropped on 2026-10-21). Built from `main` (commit 3eb59a9) by Claude Opus 5.5.

**Form.** The hosted page (`dist/energy-tool-hosted.html`), republished to the existing private Claude link "Concept Energy Optioneering" (https://claude.ai/artifact/KZ5isQcoksrwHY1AqTHU55, version 5, readable only by the owner) with the host's `downloads` capability. The earlier version on that link was the 2026-10-21 build (810 KB, no capabilities). The installable web app (PWA) was not chosen: hosting it needs a repository setting (GitHub Pages) and makes the tool public.

**What was wrong on a phone and what changed.**

| Found | Change |
|---|---|
| The hosted page could not save anything: the host ignores downloads a page starts itself, so "Save PDF report" and "Save study" were hidden and the run log could only be copied | `saveFile.js`: in the hosted build, saves go through `claude.use('downloads').save({ filename, data })`; the host asks the viewer to confirm each save. The buttons appear once the host answers; without the capability the page behaves as before (no save buttons, run log copied). A declined or refused save says so in plain words (`saveRefusal`). The single file and the Windows program keep the browser download; each bundle carries only its own save code (test 14) |
| Fields at 14 px: iOS zooms the page into any field below 16 px, on every tap | Fields at 16 px below 768 px wide or on a touch screen (`pointer: coarse`) |
| Buttons 30 px high | 40 px on touch screens; tick boxes 22 px |
| The inputs drawer covered the "Inputs" / "Close inputs" button, so on a phone it could not be closed without a keyboard | The drawer has its own head with "Done" (sticky at its top), a backdrop that closes it when tapped, and Escape; focus returns to "Inputs". Below 1280 px only; wider screens unchanged |
| With "Run details" open, three run-summary tables (shortfall, grid periods, phasing; up to 464 px) widened the whole page, so a phone zoomed out to 502 px | `.rs-wide` scrolls sideways inside itself; grid children `min-width: 0`. B19 checked this section only closed |
| The drawer's first and last controls could sit under the status bar or home indicator | Safe-area padding on the drawer below 1280 px |

Mouse-driven screens, and so the Windows program, are unchanged except for the drawer's "Done" head and backdrop below 1280 px wide.

**Checks.** New browser check B41: the hosted page in a host-like document with a stand-in for the downloads capability, 390 × 844 touch screen: T1 runs; no sideways scroll with the run details open; PDF, study and run log saved through the host; a declined save keeps nothing and says so; fields ≥ 16 px; "Done" reachable, closes the drawer and returns focus; a tap beside the drawer closes it; the saved study reopens through the file field and matches; no browser download, outside request or console error. Test 14 now requires the capability call in the hosted page and its absence from the single file. A longer phone check outside the suite (`/home/claude/review/phone-check.mjs` in the session, not committed) also ran the breakevens (5.5 s) and the Monte Carlo with 300 samples (21.8 s) at 390 px with no sideways scroll, and every results tab. Not checked: a real phone, and the real host's save prompt (the stand-in resolves at once).

**Test runs (2026-10-30).** `npm test` 77/77. First full `npm run test:browser`: 40 of 41, the one failure being B19 (axe: the grid-shortfall table could scroll sideways at 375 px but a keyboard could not reach it). Fix: that section is focusable (`tabIndex` 0, in `RunSummary.jsx`); focus shows the usual outline. Second full run on the final code (the build committed as adeb929): `npm run test:browser` 41/41, including B19 and B41. The Node suite was not re-run after the one-line focus change (no engine file changed).
